It then puts the `modified` status on `ABSENT_RESULT`, a SHOULD whose statement is about the bytes being present.

That §8 sentence fits neither reading. If it is a rule, it stands outside §9, which contradicts §1 (:22) and §9 (:1025-1026). It is not one of the exceptions §1 names: the two recommendations at :798 and :978 and the two runner duties. A tool can check it (RFC :87). If it is not a rule, the RFC's MUST is lost. Design §10 maps the MUST to "spec 8" prose and `modified` to `ABSENT_RESULT` (:598-599). §11 (:677-716) lists no departure for it.

**Evidence.**

- `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:46`: "Whatever is at a result's default place MUST be its recorded bytes or something standing in for them."
- `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:86-87`: "| A result's recorded bytes are at its default place | check | `absent` ... |" and "| Whatever is at a result's default place is its recorded bytes or a stand-in for them | tooling that recognises stand-ins (MAY) | `modified` |"
- `docs/specification.md:891-892`: "Whatever is at a result's default place is its recorded bytes or something standing in for them." (no keyword, no code)
- `docs/specification.md:1025-1026`: "This section is the rule catalogue: the only place this specification states a rule that a tool checks or keeps."
- `docs/specification.md:1184`: "| `ABSENT_RESULT` | SHOULD | Every result's recorded bytes are at its default place | `absent`, or `modified` | 0005 §1 |"
- `docs/specification.md:1173` gives the MAY only in the statuses table, and :1074 says "Other tables, such as the check layer's statuses, are not read." So the MAY never reaches `rules.json`.

A listing of the check-layer rules in `rules.json`:

```
check | ABSENT_RESULT | SHOULD | `absent`, or `modified` | Every result's recorded bytes are at its default place
rules mentioning 'modified' or 'stand': ['MISSING_INPUT_FILE', 'INPUT_NOT_TRACKED', 'ABSENT_RESULT']
```

The first two are about inputs. No MUST covers what the default place holds. For inputs the spec does have a MUST code, `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` (:1130: "An input's `path` names a file, or something standing in for one").

**Why it matters.** RFC 0005's own example (:246) puts `mlp_svm`'s accuracy at `classification`'s default place. The RFC forbids this with a MUST. Under the catalogue it breaks only a SHOULD about presence. Checkers can still tell the two cases apart by status (`absent` vs `modified`). What `rules.json` loses is the rule and its level. Runner authors and tools that read the catalogue (astra-tools, astra-typescript) find no prohibition, and they report a wrong file at the same level as a file that was never fetched. No JSON Schema verdict changes.

**Suggested fix.** Add a check row, e.g. `MODIFIED_RESULT` | MUST | "Whatever is at a result's default place is its recorded bytes or a stand-in for them (needs stand-in recognition)" | `modified` | 0005 §1, and keep `ABSENT_RESULT` (SHOULD) for `absent` only. Or record the merge as a departure in design §11 and the tidy-up RFC.

### F019. No rule or status covers a record whose commit is not in the shared history, and the SHOULD-merge advice doesn't prevent it

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Standards · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:975-979`, `docs/specification.md:24-27`, `docs/specification.md:887-891`, `docs/specification.md:901-903`, `docs/specification.md:1126-1127`, `docs/specification.md:1168-1170`, `docs/specification.md:1183`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:75-77`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:153`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:119`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:605`

**What is wrong.** RFC 0005 says the commit a record names "has to stay in the history of the commits that hold the record". RFC 0007 relies on that, so that commit A "can be got wherever `<commit B>` can". The spec states the property as a fact (:977-978) and backs it only with a SHOULD to merge rather than squash or rebase. It has no rule or code for the property, and §1 lists the merge advice among the "recommendations no tool can check". Declarations have `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND` and `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` (:1126-1127); records have nothing. A record whose commit is gone is still valid and in its place, so it isn't `missing`, and `STALE_RESULT` (:1183) can't be evaluated without A's `astra.yaml`, so it is neither `unchanged` nor `stale`. No status applies. The gap is inherited from RFC 0005, whose "What's checked" tables have no row for it, but §9 is "the only place this specification states a rule", so :977-978 is not a rule.

The commit can leave, or never reach, the shared history in two ways:

| Case | How | Does the SHOULD help? |
|---|---|---|
| Squash or rebase | The user squash-merges or rebases a branch with records, deletes it, and gc runs (or someone clones after the delete). | Only if followed. |
| git-annex adjusted branch | After `git annex adjust --unlock`, or `git-annex init` on a filesystem without symlinks (FAT, Windows), `HEAD` is a local "git-annex adjusted branch" commit. `git annex sync` re-creates commits on the original branch and doesn't push the adjusted one. | No. The user only merges; the tool does the equivalent of a rebase. |

While A still exists but isn't an ancestor (before gc, or with the branch kept), "since the commit" (:1183) reads two ways with opposite verdicts: a tree diff `A..S` shows only the record (unchanged), a history walk finds S's format change (stale). No rule says which commit a runner records on an adjusted branch either: `HEAD`, the original branch's commit, or none (refuse to run). `git status` is clean there, so `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` doesn't fire.

**Evidence.**

- `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:153`: "The commit a record names has to stay in the history of the commits that hold the record, so branches with records should be merged rather than squashed or rebased."
- `docs/specification.md:977-979`: "The commit a record names stays in the history of every commit that holds the record, so branches with records SHOULD be merged rather than squashed or rebased."
- `docs/specification.md:24-25`: "The two recommendations no tool can check are stated where they apply (§7, §8)".
- `docs/specification.md:1170`: "`missing` | The result has no valid record where its address says: `MISSING_RECORD`, `INVALID_RECORD`, `MISPLACED_RECORD` or `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS` doesn't hold".
- `docs/specification.md:889-891` names git-annex as a way to keep results; :901 defines `commit` as "the full id of the git commit the recipe ran from".

Squash repro (scratch repo; commit A on `feature` changes `data/raw.csv`, the next commit adds `.astra/records/total.json` naming A; squash-merge, delete the branch, gc; a rebase gives the same output):

```
record names A=ffa39644c95a7f87434b3c1db80fb111846e786c
is-ancestor exit 128: fatal: Not a valid commit name ffa39644c95a7f87434b3c1db80fb111846e786c
fatal: git cat-file: could not get object info
validate.py  ProvenanceRecord total.json -> VALID ; Analysis astra.yaml -> VALID
validate.mjs ProvenanceRecord total.json -> VALID ; Analysis astra.yaml -> VALID
```

`git merge-base --is-ancestor <A> HEAD` exits 0 when the history is kept, 1 when A exists but isn't an ancestor, and 128 when A is gone.

git-annex repro (git 2.56.0, git-annex 10.20260421, reproduced twice; bare remote, one annexed file, `main` pushed, `git annex adjust --unlock`, a record naming `HEAD` committed, `git annex sync`, `git clone --no-local`):

```
branch now: adjusted/main(unlocked)
git status --porcelain: []
runner records HEAD = A = 6b175bc90c11825b1f8e3e0fade13ea8e7f05463
A's subject: git-annex adjusted branch; A's parent: ae635240810ffa317e29330504ed3ccd4fcbcc8e
A ancestor of main (after sync): no
record on main: {"generated_by":{"commit":"6b175bc90c11825b1f8e3e0fade13ea8e7f05463"}}
A object in clone: absent
A object in bare remote: absent
```

A variant that commits code C on the adjusted branch first gives "is C an ancestor of main? no" and no local branch containing C; only the reflog keeps it until gc. The record is schema-valid in both validators. git-annex-adjust(1): "Any commits that you make will initially only be made to the adjusted branch." The adjusted-branches design page: "B does not have A' or C in its history"; "The adjusted/master branch is not pushed to remotes." The unlocked-files tip: "git-annex init detects such filesystems and automatically sets up a repository to use all unlocked files" (checked against the documentation only).

**Why it matters.** Squash-merge is the default on many forges, and git-annex users on Windows, FAT or exFAT media, or after `git annex adjust --unlock` hit the same state while following the SHOULD. A checker author then has to invent a status: one checker reports `stale`, another `missing`, a third fails on `git diff A..HEAD`. Runners can't apply `STALE_INPUT` (:969), which builds only from `unchanged` results. Two runners on an adjusted branch record different commits. RFC 0007 consumers lose the guarantee that commit A can be fetched, and nothing tells them.

**Suggested fix.** Add a check rule, e.g. `RECORD_COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` | MUST | "Every record's commit is an ancestor of the commit being checked", with a status in the table, and define `STALE_RESULT` as a comparison of the two commits' contents; reword :977-978 as the reason for the SHOULD (a tool that re-creates commits counts as rebasing), correct §1, and in §8 have a runner on a git-annex adjusted branch record the original branch's commit or refuse to run. Tom decides.

### F020. How a record is parsed is never stated or tested, so JSON parsers and YAML loaders give one record different verdicts

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Internal consistency, Standards, Edge cases · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:79`, `docs/specification.md:184-186`, `docs/specification.md:1097`, `docs/specification.md:1098`, `tests/conftest.py:27-30`, `tests/conftest.py:259`, `tests/test_specification.py:103`, `tests/test_fixtures.py:44`, `tests/test_fixtures.py:59`, `tests/test_provenance.py:21`, `tests/data/valid/ProvenanceRecord-001-full.yaml:1`

**What is wrong.** The specification points two ways. §2's layer table says "any strict YAML 1.2 loader" enforces the parse layer. §3 and §9 say a record is well-formed JSON, read "as `astra.yaml` is, by the same parse and schema rules". A careful reading gives answers: a repeated name is `DUPLICATE_KEY`, and a trailing comma or `NaN` is `INVALID_SYNTAX`. But no stock parser enforces both rules, and the specification never says which parser it means:

- A YAML 1.2 loader rejects repeated names but accepts text that is not JSON (block YAML, comments, trailing commas, `0x2DC`), and reads `NaN` as a string.
- A stock JSON parser (`json.loads`, `JSON.parse`) rejects non-JSON text but keeps the last of two repeated names without error. RFC 8259 §4 only says names "SHOULD be unique", so `INVALID_SYNTAX` doesn't catch it, and JSON Schema 2019-09 leaves such an object undefined (core §4.2.1), so the schema layer can't either.
- The specification says nothing about encoding or a byte order mark (a grep for `utf-8`, `encoding`, `unicode` and `BOM` finds nothing). RFC 8259 §8.1 lets a parser ignore a BOM or reject it.

The suite reads records both ways and tests neither parse code for records. The §8 JSON example goes through plain `json.loads` (`tests/test_specification.py:103`). All 16 record fixtures are block YAML, read by `load_yaml` (`tests/test_fixtures.py:44`, `:59`; `tests/test_provenance.py:21`), and none parses as JSON. `PARSE_ERRORS` maps the parse codes to YAML loader errors only, `fixtures()` globs only `*.yaml` (`tests/conftest.py:259`), and the only `fails at: parse` fixtures are Analysis-101, -146, -182 and -183.

**Evidence.**

```
docs/specification.md:79    | **parse** | any strict YAML 1.2 loader | one file's text |
docs/specification.md:184   **Records** are JSON ([RFC 8259](...)),
                            well-formed ([`INVALID_SYNTAX`](#parse-and-schema)). A record is read as one
                            document, as `astra.yaml` is, by the same parse and schema rules.
docs/specification.md:1097  `INVALID_SYNTAX` | parse | MUST | ... and every record is well-formed JSON
docs/specification.md:1098  `DUPLICATE_KEY` | parse | MUST | Every key of a map is distinct. ...
```

Base: a valid one-line record.

```json
{"spec_version": "0.1.0", "address": "comparison", "content_hash": "sha256:7af13cf14f96106b729279ec96430c5b115437053c29aeca4ff70b95770fb83e", "size": 732, "generated_by": {"commit": "a7d9ea93c409ab6da0d919308f91f51de7c7ec6f", "environment": {"iri": "urn:x:env:1"}, "runner": {"id": "r", "version": "1.2.0"}}}
```

Each variant changes one thing; each path validates against the committed `provenance.schema.json`:

| variant | load_yaml + jsonschema | yaml 2.9 + Ajv | json.loads + jsonschema | JSON.parse + Ajv 8.20.0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `"size": 732, "size": 733` | DuplicateKeyError | DUPLICATE_KEY | VALID (size=733) | VALID (size=733) |
| second `"content_hash": "sha256:000…0"` | DuplicateKeyError | not run | VALID (keeps `000…0`) | VALID (keeps `000…0`) |
| `"version": NaN` | VALID (string "NaN") | VALID | WRONG_TYPE (nan) | SyntaxError |
| `"size": NaN` | WRONG_TYPE ('NaN') | not run | WRONG_TYPE (nan) | SyntaxError: Unexpected token 'N' |
| UTF-8 BOM | VALID | VALID | bytes: VALID; str: "Unexpected UTF-8 BOM" | SyntaxError |
| trailing comma, single quotes, `0x2DC`, `# comment`, YAML block | VALID | VALID | JSONDecodeError | SyntaxError |

```
$ python3 -c '... json.load(open(p)) for p in glob("tests/data/*/ProvenanceRecord-*.yaml") ...'
16 record fixtures, 16 JSONDecodeError
$ ls tests/data/*/*.json
no matches found
```

The harness can't express a record parse failure. In a clone, a fixture `ProvenanceRecord-216` with `# rule: INVALID_SYNTAX` / `# fails at: parse` and an unquoted `"address": comparison` fails with `DID NOT RAISE`. And `"size": -1,` inserted before spec:927 (`"size": 732,`) leaves `pytest tests/test_specification.py -k whole` at 3 passed.

**Why it matters.** Checker authors in astra-tools and astra-typescript have to guess which parser the rules mean. After a hand-resolved merge leaves two `content_hash` lines, a checker on `json.loads` or `JSON.parse` keeps the last value and accepts the record, while one that follows §2 reports `DUPLICATE_KEY`, so the record is `missing` through `INVALID_RECORD`. A checker that reuses the YAML loader, as §2 and the tests do, accepts records §9 calls `INVALID_SYNTAX`. A `NaN` gets three readings and two codes. A JSON-parsing checker can't run the repository's record fixtures, and the suite passes in every one of these cases.

**Suggested fix.** In §3 and §2's parse row, say a record is parsed as RFC 8259 JSON in UTF-8 (I-JSON, RFC 7493, is a ready profile) by a parser that rejects repeated names (`DUPLICATE_KEY`) and non-JSON forms such as `NaN`, comments and trailing commas (`INVALID_SYNTAX`), and say whether a leading BOM is allowed. Read JSON in the tests with an `object_pairs_hook` that rejects repeats and a `parse_constant` that rejects `NaN`, and add `.json` record fixtures for both parse codes, with a header convention JSON can carry (such as a sidecar file).

### F021. LinkML's RDF conversion, the route §10 names to PROV-O, crashes on `urn:` environment IRIs, including §8's example

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Internal consistency, Standards, Edge cases · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:910-913`, `docs/specification.md:937`, `docs/specification.md:952-953`, `docs/specification.md:1339-1347`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:13-17`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:141-152`, `tests/test_provenance.py:85-100`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:106,122,132`

**What is wrong.** §10 says a record's node types come "through LinkML's RDF conversion of the record", and that "Converted to RDF that way, a record is PROV-O". §8 and the `Environment.iri` description recommend "a `urn:` IRI or a scheme of the runner's own", and §8's example uses `urn:example-runner:env:sha256:…`, as RFC 0005 does. `Environment.iri` is the class's `identifier`, with `range: uriorcurie`. LinkML 1.12.1's RDF dumper sends every identifier to `Namespaces.uri_for` (`linkml_runtime/utils/namespaces.py:236-250`, through `rdflib_dumper._as_uri`, lines 203-207). That function keeps a value unchanged only if it contains `://`. Anything else it reads as a CURIE, and it raises on an unknown prefix. provenance.yaml declares only `astra`, `linkml`, `prov` and `schema`. So the conversion fails on the spec's example record and on both valid record fixtures. It also fails on `tag:` IRIs and on a runner's own scheme. LinkML's other route, `-t json-ld`, does not crash, but it types only the root node. The JSON Schema contract is unaffected: every record here validates.

An IRI whose scheme matches a declared prefix is also rewritten, both by the dumper and by the published context. `astra:env:5d13` becomes `https://w3id.org/astra/env:5d13`. For such a record, "the environment's IRI is its `@id`" (spec:1346-1347) does not hold. This is a narrower edge case.

**Evidence.** LinkML 1.12.1, pinned at `pyproject.toml:62`. The record is `sed -n '923,942p' docs/specification.md`, VALID under both toolkit validators.

```
$ uv run linkml-convert -s src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml -C ProvenanceRecord -t ttl -o out.ttl spec-record.json
  File ".../linkml_runtime/dumpers/rdflib_dumper.py", line 207, in _as_uri
  File ".../linkml_runtime/utils/namespaces.py", line 250, in uri_for
ValueError: : Unknown CURIE prefix: urn          (out.ttl: 0 bytes)
$ ... tests/data/valid/ProvenanceRecord-001-full.yaml
ValueError: File "ProvenanceRecord-001-full.yaml", line 12, col 10: Unknown CURIE prefix: urn
$ ... tests/data/valid/ProvenanceRecord-002-built-once.yaml
ValueError: File "ProvenanceRecord-002-built-once.yaml", line 8, col 10: Unknown CURIE prefix: urn
```

The same record, with only `environment.iri` changed:

```
https://example.org/env/5d13   -> converts: "_:c14n0 a prov:Entity", "a prov:Activity", "a prov:SoftwareAgent"
example-runner:env:sha256:5d13 -> ValueError: : Unknown CURIE prefix: example-runner
tag:example.org,2026:env:5d13  -> ValueError: : Unknown CURIE prefix: tag
astra:env:5d13                 -> converts; prov:used is https://w3id.org/astra/env:5d13
```

With `-t json-ld`, the only `@type` is `"ProvenanceRecord"` (line 25 of the output); rdflib finds one `rdf:type` triple, `prov:Entity`. Generation, Runner, RecordedInput and Environment are untyped.

`tests/test_provenance.py:85-100` (`test_classes_map_onto_prov`) asserts only `sv.get_uri(c, expand=True)`. `grep -rn -il 'linkml-convert\|rdflib_dumper\|as_rdf_graph\|rdflib' tests tools justfile .github` finds nothing. Design §10 row 602 ("Converted to RDF, a record is PROV-O") is marked landed, and §§11-13 do not mention the conversion.

**Why it matters.** Anyone who builds a PROV-O graph from records the way §10 says gets an exception on every record that follows §8's advice, and on the spec's own example. astra-tools would hit this the first time it exports RDF through LinkML. The suite passes because nothing converts a record. The mapping itself is right: with a `urn` prefix the conversion gives exactly §10's triples.

**Suggested fix.** Define the RDF reading in §10 so that it keeps absolute IRIs as written (JSON-LD expansion with the context, plus the class URIs as `rdf:type`), or declare `urn: "urn:"` in provenance.yaml and say which schemes LinkML's conversion handles (`range: uri` does not help). Add a test that converts the valid record fixtures and the §8 example and checks each node's type and the environment's IRI.

### F022. Two narrowings, lowercase content hashes and three-part `spec_version`, aren't recorded in design §11 or the tidy-up RFC

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `src/astra/schema/types.yaml:104-111`, `src/astra/schema/types.yaml:113-121`, `docs/specification.md:54-61`, `docs/specification.md:231`, `docs/specification.md:232`, `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-133-spec-version-without-patch.yaml:3`, `tests/test_types.py:61-64`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:202`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:203`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:601`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:660-716`, `astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:48,98,167,185-187,211,223`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:116,130,142`

**What is wrong.** Design §11 lists the design's changes beyond the accepted RFCs and `main`, and the tidy-up RFC turns them into changes and migration steps. Two narrowings that the contract enforces are in neither. Each is decided in the design's §4 type table and stated in the specification, so each rule has a source. What is missing is the record that it narrows something.

| Narrowing | Accepted before | Now | Recorded? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `content_hash` is lowercase hex | RFC 0005 doesn't set the case | `^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$(?!\n)` | No. §11 item 14 and tidy-up change 25 cover commit ids only. Design §10 files it under RFC 0005's "Every hash names its algorithm" (design:601), which says nothing about case. |
| `spec_version` is `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, no leading zeros | `main`: `^\d+\.\d+(\.\d+)?$`, so `"1.0"` and `"01.1.0"` | three parts, no leading zeros | No. No §10 row, no §11 item. Tidy-up change 8 and change 20 record only the rename. |

The comparable narrowings each have both a record and a migration step: §11 items 14-16 become tidy-up changes 25-27 (commit-id case, paths, times). Commit-id case, the parallel silence in RFC 0007, was recorded. Hash case and version form were not. Pre-release versions are not part of the narrowing: `main` already rejected `0.1.0-rc1`.

**Evidence.**

*Content hash.*

- `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:116`: "`content_hash`: *required* - the SHA-256 of the result's bytes"; :130: "Every hash names its algorithm (`sha256:<SHA-256 hash>`)."; :142: "hashes that name their algorithm".
- `src/astra/schema/types.yaml:107-109`: "`sha256:` followed by 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits", `pattern: "^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$(?!\\n)"`.
- `design/...:704`: "14. Commit ids are lowercase hexadecimal (RFC 0007 doesn't say which case)."
- Tidy-up :185-187: "A `commit`, in a declaration and in a record, is 40 or 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits, as git writes it."; :223: "write commit ids in lowercase". No line addresses the case of a hash.

Each test record is the specification's §8 example (specification.md:922-944) with one hash changed:

```
$ uv run python validate.py ProvenanceRecord rec-lower.json rec-upper.json rec-upper-input.json rec-mixed.json
rec-lower.json        VALID
rec-upper.json        /content_hash | …/provenance.schema.json#/properties/content_hash/pattern | INVALID_VALUE | 'sha256:7AF13CF1…' does not match '^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$(?!\\n)'
rec-upper-input.json  /generated_by/inputs/0/content_hash | …#/$defs/RecordedInput/properties/content_hash/pattern | INVALID_VALUE
rec-mixed.json        /content_hash | …#/properties/content_hash/pattern | INVALID_VALUE
$ uv run python validate.py ProvenanceRecord --open rec-upper.json
/content_hash | …/provenance.open.schema.json#/properties/content_hash/pattern | INVALID_VALUE
```

Ajv gives the same verdicts, pointers and codes. The result is `INVALID_RECORD`, and then `missing` (specification.md:1180). Hash case is tested only by the unit test at `tests/test_types.py:56`; no invalid fixture covers it.

*`spec_version`.*

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1"
outputs:
  result:
    type: metric
    format: json
```

```
$ uv run python toolkit/validate.py Analysis a-0_1.yaml a-1_0.yaml a-0_1_0.yaml a-01_1_0.yaml
a-0_1.yaml    /spec_version | https://w3id.org/astra/analysis.schema.json#/properties/spec_version/pattern | INVALID_VALUE | '0.1' does not match '^(0|[1-9][0-9]*)\\.(0|[1-9][0-9]*)\\.(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$(?!\\n)'
a-1_0.yaml    /spec_version | ...#/properties/spec_version/pattern | INVALID_VALUE
a-0_1_0.yaml  VALID
a-01_1_0.yaml /spec_version | ...#/properties/spec_version/pattern | INVALID_VALUE
```

Ajv agrees. A record with `spec_version: "0.1"` fails the same way at `provenance.schema.json#/properties/spec_version/pattern`.

- On `main`, `analysis.yaml:551` has `pattern: "^\\d+\\.\\d+(\\.\\d+)?$"`, and `docs/specification.md:744` has ``| Version | `^\d+\.\d+(\.\d+)?$` | `"1.0"`, `"1.0.0"` |``.
- `tests/test_types.py:63` rejects `["0.1", "v0.1.0", "01.1.0", "0.1.0-rc1", "0.1.0\n"]`.
- Tidy-up :98 (change 8): "`Analysis.version` is renamed `spec_version` …", with nothing on the form. The only migration step (:211) is "rename `version` to `spec_version` in every analysis".
- Design §12 (:731-732): "`spec_version` only checks the format here." `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-changes.md:41` attributes the named types to "The RFCs' grammars", but no RFC gives one for this type; the RFCs' examples are all three-part (`0.0.14`, `0.1.0`).

**Why it matters.** The tidy-up RFC gives the specification its authority to narrow the accepted RFCs and `main`, and its reader can't learn of these two narrowings. A runner that follows RFC 0005 with an encoder that prints uppercase hex (.NET's `Convert.ToHexString`) writes records that every conforming checker reports as `missing`, under the open schema too. An author who writes `spec_version: "0.1"`, a natural form since `main` showed `"1.0"` and the spec calls itself "version 0.1", gets `INVALID_VALUE` from a change no Compatibility paragraph or migration step mentions. Lowercase hashes remain the natural reading: RFC 0005 cites the Git LFS pointer spec (rfcs/0005:268), which says "The hash is lower case hexadecimal", and compares a pointer's hash with the record's (rfcs/0005:244).

**Suggested fix.** Record both in design §11 (extend item 14 to "Commit ids and content hashes are lowercase hexadecimal", add a `spec_version` item, update the count) and in the tidy-up's changes and migration steps ("write hashes in lowercase"; "write `spec_version` with three parts, such as `0.1.0`"); move design:601 to a §10 row citing the tidy-up. Optionally add an invalid fixture for an uppercase hash.

### F023. `Evidence.doi`'s pattern narrows DOI syntax without a source or a record, and rejects real DOIs

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Standards · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:68-73`, `src/astra/schema/generated/analysis.schema.json:149`, `docs/specification.md:252-253`, `docs/specification.md:501-503`, `docs/specification.md:1112`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:229`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:662-713`, `astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:203`, `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-166-doi-not-a-doi.yaml:12`

**What is wrong.** The pattern `^10\.[0-9]{4,}/[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+$(?!\n)` is narrower than the DOI standard (ISO 26324, as the DOI Handbook gives it) and narrower than `main` (`^10\.\d{4,}/.*$`). Nothing grounds it in the standard and nothing records the narrowing. The design gives it only in its table of single-use patterns (design:229), with no reason. No RFC, tidy-up change, ruling, design §10 row or §11 item defines DOI syntax, and nothing in the repository cites ISO 26324. The description, "The DOI of the cited work", suggests any DOI is accepted. The facets:

| Facet | Pattern | Standard | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| Subdivided registrant code | `[0-9]{4,}`, one unbroken run | digit runs separated by `.`, no length limit | registered DOIs rejected, including every ISBN-A (`10.978.…`, `10.979.…`) |
| Space in the suffix | suffix class excludes U+0020 | Unicode Graphic code points, spaces included | registered DOIs rejected; U+00A0, U+3000 and C1 controls such as U+0085 still pass |
| Suffix vs `main` | non-empty, no ASCII space or C0 control | `main`: `.*` | `main`-era files fail; the tidy-up's list of invalidating changes doesn't say so |
| Directory indicator | literal `10` | 2022 edition drops the `10` requirement and allows no registrant code | Analysis-166 calls `11.1/x`, a well-formed DOI name, "not a DOI" |

The specification and the schema agree with each other (spec:501-503: "`10.`, four or more digits, `/`, then visible characters"). What is missing is a sound source and a record.

**Evidence.** The pattern, `insight.yaml:69`, generated unchanged at `analysis.schema.json:149`:

```yaml
pattern: '^10\.[0-9]{4,}/[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+$(?!\n)'
```

On `main`:

```
$ git show main:src/astra/schema/insight.yaml | grep -n 'pattern: "^10'
72:        pattern: "^10\\.\\d{4,}/.*$"
```

The standard. DOI Handbook 2025, §4.3.1: "There is no defined limit on the length of the DOI name, or of the DOI prefix or DOI suffix." and "A DOI name consists of an ordered sequence of code points of the Graphic type ... The Graphic type includes all code points that are letter, mark, number, punctuation, symbol and spaces." §4.3.2: "The registrant code consists of sequences of digits between 0 and 9, separated by U+002E FULL STOP." The IANA URN:DOI registration (2023-03-14): "The 2022 edition of ISO 26324 has amended the syntax of the prefix by removing the requirement for the directory indicator to be "10" and allow also DOI names without a registrant code", with `DOI-CHARS = %x20-7E`.

Registered DOIs the contract rejects:

```
$ curl -s https://doi.org/api/handles/10.978.8889637/173
{"responseCode":1,"handle":"10.978.8889637/173", ... "Rapporto sullo stato dell'editoria in Italia 2006" ...
$ curl -s https://doi.org/api/handles/10.3929/ethz-a-%20007549606
{"responseCode":1,"handle":"10.3929/ethz-a- 007549606",...
```

(DataCite lists four such ETH DOIs with a space, each `findable`; each has a twin without the space that also resolves.)

The minimal document, with only the `doi` varied:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
outputs:
  result:
    type: metric
    format: json
prior_insights:
  p:
    claim: C
    evidence:
      - doi: 10.978.8889637/173
```

jsonschema and Ajv agree. `10.978.8889637/173`, `10.3929/ethz-a- 007549606`, `10.1234/`, `10.1234/a\tb`, `15434/182` and `11.1/x` each give:

```
/prior_insights/p/evidence/0/doi | https://w3id.org/astra/analysis.schema.json#/$defs/Evidence/properties/doi/pattern | INVALID_VALUE
```

`10.48550/arXiv.1706.03762` is valid, and so are `10.1234/abc def` (U+00A0), `10.1234/abc　def` (U+3000) and `10.1234/abc\u0085def` (NEL).

The missing record: the tidy-up RFC's compatibility line (NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:203) lists the changes that can make a file invalid, and none touches `doi`; no migration step mentions a DOI. Design §10 (662-674) has a row for `Output.format`'s pattern and none for `Evidence.doi`; §11 (677-713) has no DOI item. Spec §9's `INVALID_VALUE` row (spec:1112) gives as sources "0005, 0007, #73", none of which defines a DOI pattern. Analysis-166 (`doi: 11.1/x`, line 12) is the only DOI fixture, and it tests only the prefix.

**Why it matters.** An author can't cite a book by its ISBN-A, or a registered DOI with a space, as written: the file fails with `INVALID_VALUE`, and every tool built on the contract (astra-tools, astra-typescript, editors) rejects identifiers that doi.org resolves. An implementer who checks ISO 26324 finds a stricter contract with no stated reason, and ports a fixture whose name asserts something false. A `main`-era file that now fails has no listed cause.

**Suggested fix.** Choose the DOI language against ISO 26324: at least a prefix of `10.` then dot-divided digit runs (`^10\.[0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)*/`) and a suffix that allows inner spaces (e.g. `[^\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]+`). Record it in the description, §6, design §11 and the tidy-up RFC (with `main` and `tidy-up` among `INVALID_VALUE`'s sources), and rename Analysis-166 to what it breaks (e.g. `doi-not-directory-10`) and add a suffix fixture. Tom decides.

### F024. STALE_RESULT's "the options of its decisions" has two readings, and the literal one misses the spec's own example

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Author · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:990`, `docs/specification.md:995-996`, `docs/specification.md:1000-1002`, `docs/specification.md:1183`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:666`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:172`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:180`

**What is wrong.** STALE_RESULT lists what must not change for a result to stay unchanged. One item is "the options of its decisions" (§8), or "its decisions' options" (§9). Elsewhere the spec uses "a decision's options" for the decision's `options` map (spec :430-433, `Decision.options`). Read that way, the rule doesn't cover a change of the universe's selection. But the spec's example says a change of selection makes the result stale. Read as "the selected option", the rule doesn't say which of that option's fields count. It also doesn't say whether adding or editing an unselected option counts. The output's declaration lists the fields that count. The options item lists none.

The wording comes from RFC 0005 §4 (:172, :180), which has the same ambiguity. This is not a departure from the RFC. Spec :945-947 ("the options of its decisions … are in the `astra.yaml` files at that commit") fits either reading.

**Evidence.**

- spec:990: "- the options of its decisions;"
- spec:995-996: "Nothing else counts: editing a finding, a description or another output leaves a result unchanged."
- spec:1000-1001: "selecting `random_forest` in the universe `svm` makes `accuracy#svm` stale"
- spec:1183 and rules.json:666: "… its decisions' options, its inputs' content, or the results it reads"

A is the §2 example. Each variant changes one thing, and all four files are VALID under jsonschema and Ajv:

```
diff A C-select:             <     decisions: {model: svm, scaling: standard}
                             >     decisions: {model: random_forest, scaling: standard}
diff A D-add-option:         >       knn: {}
diff A E-option-description: >         description: Support vector machine.
```

The comparison was run under three readings: R1, the decisions' `options` maps; R2a, the selected option; R2b, the selected option and its definition. Every other STALE_RESULT item is the same in every case.

```
accuracy#svm,    A -> C-select:             R1 same     R2a CHANGED  R2b CHANGED
accuracy#svm,    A -> D-add-option:         R1 CHANGED  R2a same     R2b same
accuracy#svm,    A -> E-option-description: R1 CHANGED  R2a same     R2b CHANGED
accuracy#forest, A -> D-add-option:         R1 CHANGED  R2a same     R2b same
```

- Under R1, C (the spec's own example) leaves `accuracy#svm` unchanged.
- Under R1, D makes `accuracy#forest`, `accuracy#svm` and `comparison` stale. Under R2a and R2b, D changes nothing.
- Under R1 and R2b, E (a description added to the selected option) is stale, which strains :995-996.

The example rules out R1 alone. Nothing in the text decides between R2a, R2b, and R1 combined with R2a.

**Why it matters.** STALE_RESULT is a MUST, and checkers in astra-tools and astra-typescript implement it from §9 or `rules.json`. After a routine edit, such as adding an option to explore later, two conforming checkers give the same result different statuses. STALE_EVIDENCE carries the difference to findings, and STALE_INPUT carries it to a runner's decision to build. An author can't tell whether adding an option invalidates published results.

**Suggested fix.** In §8 (:990) and the §9 row, write "the option its universe's effective selection gives each of its decisions". Then say which of that option's fields count, if any, and whether changes to a decision's other options count. Carry the wording into the tidy-up RFC, since RFC 0005 §4 is the source.

### F025. MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS doesn't say at which commit "its output" is read, so a changed input list gives `missing` or `stale`

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Internal consistency · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:1182`, `docs/specification.md:904-907`, `docs/specification.md:1169-1170`, `docs/specification.md:987-988`, `docs/specification.md:945-947`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:83-86`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:122-123`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:120`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:171`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:177-178`

**What is wrong.** MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS checks a record's `inputs` against "each entry its output lists". It names no commit. A checker can read the output at the commit being checked, or at the commit the record names. The two readings give different statuses for the commonest edit to an output, adding an input:

- At the commit being checked, the record has no item for the new entry. The rule breaks, so the result is `missing`. A result that breaks the rule has no valid record, and `stale` needs a valid record.
- At the record's commit, the record is valid. STALE_RESULT's "its inputs and their order" clause breaks instead, so the result is `stale`. RFC 0005 §4 asks for this.

A renamed entry behaves like a remove plus an add, so it gives `missing` under the first reading. The rule also doesn't say whether a record may keep an item for an entry the output doesn't list. That decides what a removed input gives under the first reading: `stale` if extra items are allowed, `missing` if they aren't.

`Generation.inputs` and `RecordedInput.entry` name no commit either. RFC 0005 §2 has the same gap; its §4 is what points to the record's commit. In the specification only §8 hints at it (945-947, "the output's declaration ... [is] in the `astra.yaml` files at that commit"), and that passage is not about this rule. None of D1-D13 settles it, and design §12 doesn't list it.

**Evidence.** The rule, the statuses and the RFC:

- `docs/specification.md:1182`: "Every record has exactly one `inputs` item for each entry its output lists, matched by `entry` (their order carries no meaning) | `missing`"
- `docs/specification.md:1169`: "`stale` | The result has a valid record where its address says, and `STALE_RESULT` doesn't hold. The checker says what changed"
- `docs/specification.md:1170`: "`missing` | The result has no valid record where its address says: ... or `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS` doesn't hold"
- `docs/specification.md:987`: "its output's declaration: its recipe's command, its inputs and their order"
- `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:84-85`: "one item for each entry the output's `inputs` lists, matched by `entry`"
- `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:177-178`: "its output's declaration has changed / the order of its inputs counts"

The reproduction uses a scratch repository. At commit A, output `accuracy` has `inputs: [iris]`. Commit B adds a record that names A and has the item `{entry: iris}`. Branch `add-input` changes the list to `[iris, extra]`. Branch `remove-input` does the reverse, from `[iris, extra]` to `[iris]`. Every `astra.yaml` version and both records are VALID under jsonschema and under Ajv.

```
$ uv run python $W/readings.py $W/repo add-input
  listed at the record's commit: ['iris']
  listed at add-input HEAD      : ['iris', 'extra']
  MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS vs record's commit (extra items ok): holds
  MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS vs HEAD            (extra items ok): BROKEN -> missing
  STALE_RESULT inputs clause (inputs and their order changed): BROKEN -> stale
$ uv run python $W/readings.py $W/repo remove-input
  MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS vs HEAD            (extra items ok): holds
  MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS vs HEAD            (no extra items): BROKEN -> missing
```

**Why it matters.** Two checkers give the same result different statuses after an ordinary edit to an output. `missing` says there is no valid record. `stale` says rebuild, and only `stale` makes the checker say what changed. Evidence that cites the result inherits whichever status it gets.

**Suggested fix.** Reword 1182 as "Every record has exactly one `inputs` item for each entry its output lists in the `astra.yaml` at the commit the record names, and no other item". Say the same in `Generation.inputs` and `RecordedInput.entry`, so that STALE_RESULT reports a later change to the list.

### F026. A path-plus-commit declaration doesn't say which repository's commit it names, or what happens at a submodule's gitlink

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Author · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:639-640`, `docs/specification.md:760-765`, `docs/specification.md:774-786`, `docs/specification.md:813-815`, `docs/specification.md:1125-1127`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json` (COMMIT_NOT_FOUND), `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:195`

**What is wrong.** A declared analysis can be a git submodule, and a `path` declaration can carry a `commit`. When the two meet, there are two local repositories and each commit is in only one of them. The spec doesn't say which one `commit` refers to. RFC 0007 does: the declaring analysis's repository. The spec drops that qualifier in §7 and §9, so "the repository" can be read as the root's. That is a departure from the RFC.

A second gap is inherited from the RFCs. Neither RFC 0004, RFC 0007 nor the spec says what a path read at a commit means when it crosses a submodule. At that commit the path is a gitlink, with no `astra.yaml` in the tree. Only the record's `commit` covers submodules ("through the commit the submodule is pinned at", `docs/specification.md:901-903`, `provenance.yaml:75-77`). "What is read" (`:774-786`) names no second repository and no `.gitmodules`. Restoring the RFC's wording settles COMMIT_NOT_FOUND. It does not settle MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS for a root commit whose path is a gitlink. It also leaves §7:813-815 inconsistent: that sentence reads the records "at the declared commit ... in that analysis's repository", and the submodule's repository doesn't have a root commit.

**Evidence.**

- `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:195`: "| A `path` with a `commit` names a commit the declaring analysis's repository has | error |"
- `docs/specification.md:760-762`: "With a `path`, it is the analysis at that path at the commit, which the repository has ([`COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`](#validator))"
- `docs/specification.md:1126` (and `rules.json`): "A declaration with a `path` and a `commit` names a commit the repository has". The row before it, `:1124`, says "the repository of the root".
- `docs/specification.md:639-640`: "A declared analysis can be a git submodule."

The setup is a root repository with the analysis `lib` as the submodule `vendor/lib`. Both documents below pass both JSON Schema validators, so only the validator layer decides.

```yaml
# vendor/lib/astra.yaml (L1)              # root astra.yaml (L2)
analyses:                                 analyses:
  previous: {path: ., commit: LIBC1}        lib_rootc: {path: vendor/lib, commit: ROOTC}
                                            lib_libc:  {path: vendor/lib, commit: LIBC2}
```

```
$ git cat-file -e "${LIBC1}^{commit}"                 # root repo
fatal: Not a valid object name 8785eeb…^{commit}      exit=128
$ git -C vendor/lib cat-file -e "${LIBC1}^{commit}"   # submodule repo
exit=0
$ git ls-tree $ROOTC vendor/lib
160000 commit cce4e64547ab5de3fb758a0e52ccf7d4b829e4e4	vendor/lib
$ git cat-file -e "${ROOTC}:vendor/lib/astra.yaml"
fatal: path 'vendor/lib/astra.yaml' exists on disk, but not in '64efb57…'   exit=128
$ git -C vendor/lib cat-file -e "cce4e64…:astra.yaml"   # following the gitlink
exit=0
$ git cat-file -e "${LIBC2}^{commit}"                   # root repo
fatal: Not a valid object name cce4e64…^{commit}        exit=128
```

- **L1.** Under the root's repository the declaration gets COMMIT_NOT_FOUND. Under the declaring analysis's repository (RFC 0007:195) it passes.
- **L2, `commit: ROOTC`.** The commit exists, but the path is a gitlink, so the result is MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS, or a pass if the gitlink is followed.
- **L2, `commit: LIBC2`.** The root's repository gives COMMIT_NOT_FOUND and the submodule's passes. COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR against the root's commit has no meaning.

**Why it matters.** Validator authors give opposite verdicts on COMMIT_NOT_FOUND, MISSING_DECLARED_ANALYSIS and COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR for documents that are valid at the schema layer. A submodule analysis that declares its own earlier commit can pass when it is validated alone and fail when it is validated from its superproject. Authors who keep a shared pipeline as a submodule can't tell which commit id to write. The JSON Schema contract is unaffected.

**Suggested fix.** In §7 and §9, say "a commit the declaring analysis's repository has" (RFC 0007:195). Then either say that a path crossing a submodule at that commit follows the gitlink to the pinned commit, as `Generation.commit` does, and add the submodule repository and `.gitmodules` to "What is read". Or forbid the case under a code of its own and point to `url` plus `commit`. The second option is a new rule, so it goes in the tidy-up RFC.

### F027. `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED` names no repository, so a file in a submodule is tracked or not depending on the git call

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:356-359`, `docs/specification.md:1131`, `docs/specification.md:639-642`, `docs/specification.md:900-903`, `docs/specification.md:774-776`, `docs/specification.md:826-827`, `rfcs/0006-data-inputs.md:86`

**What is wrong.** The rule says an input's file "is tracked in git", but it doesn't say which repository. The spec allows a declared analysis to be a git submodule, and the record's commit pins it "through the commit the submodule is pinned at". A file in a submodule is in the submodule's index but not in the superproject's. So the verdict depends on where the validator runs `git ls-files`, and with which flags. There are two cases:

- **Case A:** the inputs of a submodule analysis.
- **Case B:** an input path that crosses into a plain data submodule (`vendor/data/x.csv`). `INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS` doesn't exclude it, because `vendor/data` has no `astra.yaml`.

Case B is the open question. The root commit pins the bytes through the gitlink, which reads as "committed with the analysis". But the file is not in the root commit's tree, and RFC 0006 §3 ("Data from outside the repository") sends data from another repository through an output's recipe.

This is inherited, not a departure. The spec copies RFC 0006:86 word for word. No RFC, the tidy-up draft, design §11, §12 or §13 settles it. The neighbouring rows do name their repository (`DECLARATION_OUTSIDE_REPOSITORY`, :1124: "the repository of the root"; `COMMIT_NOT_FOUND`, :1126: "the repository").

**Evidence.** The rule, `docs/specification.md:358-359` and `:1131`:

> The file, or what stands in for it, is tracked in git ([`INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`](#validator)).

> | `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED` | MUST | An input's file, or what stands in for it, is tracked in git | 0006 |

`:639-640`: "A declared analysis can be a git submodule."

Reproduction with git 2.56.0. Case A: the root declares `stage: {path: stage}`, a submodule whose `astra.yaml` has `inputs: {data: {path: data.csv}}`. Case B: the root has `inputs: {data: {path: vendor/data/x.csv}}`. All the `astra.yaml` files validate against the JSON Schema under both jsonschema and Ajv.

```
=== Case A
$ git ls-files --error-unmatch stage/data.csv
error: pathspec 'stage/data.csv' did not match any file(s) known to git    (exit 1)
$ git ls-files --recurse-submodules stage/data.csv
stage/data.csv                                                              (exit 0)
$ git -C stage ls-files --error-unmatch data.csv
data.csv                                                                    (exit 0)
=== Case B
$ git ls-files --error-unmatch vendor/data/x.csv                            (exit 1)
$ git ls-files --recurse-submodules vendor/data/x.csv
vendor/data/x.csv                                                           (exit 0)
$ git -C vendor/data ls-files --error-unmatch x.csv
x.csv                                                                       (exit 0)
```

The same gap affects reading an analysis from a commit. `:774-775` says it "is read from git at its commit", and `:826-827` says an input is got "from its `path` at the declared commit". The commit doesn't hold such a path:

```
$ git cat-file -e HEAD:vendor/data/x.csv
fatal: path 'vendor/data/x.csv' exists on disk, but not in 'HEAD'   (exit 128)
$ git cat-file -e HEAD:stage/data.csv
fatal: path 'stage/data.csv' exists on disk, but not in 'HEAD'      (exit 128)
```

**Why it matters.**

- Validator authors: astra-tools and astra-typescript can each pick a plausible git call and reach opposite verdicts on the same file.
- Analysis authors: someone who keeps data, or a whole stage, in a submodule can't tell whether their inputs are valid. A validator run from the root can reject a file that the same check run inside the submodule accepts.

**Suggested fix.** In the tidy-up RFC, say that the file (or its stand-in) is tracked in the repository that holds the input's analysis, as that repository's index lists it. Then either forbid an input path that enters a submodule (under `INPUT_NOT_TRACKED`), or allow it and say how it is read at a commit.

### F028. A remembered answer for an analysis from a commit can go stale, because its validity depends on nested remotes being fetchable

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:794-802`, `docs/specification.md:765-766`, `docs/specification.md:788-789`, `docs/specification.md:89-91`, `docs/specification.md:1128`, `docs/specification.md:1129`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:138`

**What is wrong.** The Validity paragraph says the validity of an analysis from a commit can't change, so a validator may remember it. That validity covers "everything it declares", and every rule applies to the analysis, `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` among them. Whether a remote can be fetched, and whether it still has the commit, is a fact about the network and the remote's host now, not about the commit's files. It can change. A validator that remembers its answer and one that re-checks then give different verdicts on the same root, and each can cite the spec. The sentence copies RFC 0007:138, so the defect is in the RFC as well. It is not a departure from it.

**Evidence.** The claim, `docs/specification.md:796-798`: "A commit's files never change, so neither does the answer, and a validator can remember it for a commit it has validated."

What brings nested remotes into that answer:

- spec:765-766: "Every rule of this specification applies to an analysis from a commit as it is, with its files read at its commit."
- spec:89-91: a validator "reports every parse, schema and validator rule the analysis breaks. An analysis is valid when it breaks none of their `MUST`s."
- spec:1128: "`REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` | MUST | A declaration's `url` can be fetched and has its `commit`"
- spec:1129: "`DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` | MUST | An analysis from a commit, with everything it declares, is valid at that commit"

Nothing limits `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` to the root's own declarations. It appears only at spec:655, 802 and 1128.

The scenario is valid at the schema layer. The root declares `upstream` by `url` + `commit`, and `upstream` at that commit declares `nested`:

```yaml
# upstream/astra.yaml, at the declared commit
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses:
  nested:
    url: https://example.org/private/nested
    commit: "21ca41dd31d8d87c017e04a7b02b0ab7f7d6b30d"
outputs:
  summary: {type: metric, format: json, inputs: ["nested.table"]}
```

Both documents validate under `jsonschema` and Ajv 8.20. A local demo with a `file://` remote showed the split. `git fetch <remote> <commit>` succeeds, and after the remote moves away it fails ("fatal: '.../upstream2' does not appear to be a git repository"). `git show HEAD:astra.yaml` in `upstream` gives the same bytes before and after.

The RFC, 0007:138: "That can't change - a commit's files never change and each `astra.yaml` is read by the version it states. A validator can therefore either read every `astra.yaml` the declared analysis reaches or remember the answer". The design has no entry for this in §11-13.

**Why it matters.** Validator authors split. After a nested remote goes private, is deleted, or loses the commit to a force-push and gc, a caching validator reports the root valid. A re-checking one reports `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` in `upstream` and `DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID` in the root. When a private remote is made public, the split runs the other way. Offline validation has the same gap, at the root too. Spec:788-789 says "validators and runners can work offline from what they kept", but "can be fetched" can't be judged offline, and the spec doesn't say whether a kept copy satisfies it.

**Suggested fix.** Say that a remembered answer covers every rule except `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE`. Then either check `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` only for the root's own declarations, or say that a copy kept from an earlier fetch satisfies it. Carry the same wording back to RFC 0007 through the tidy-up RFC.

### F029. `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT` covers only outputs built once of a driven analysis; no runner rule stops other results of an analysis from a commit being built

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:804-806`, `docs/specification.md:813-815`, `docs/specification.md:833-836`, `docs/specification.md:852-854`, `docs/specification.md:1025-1026`, `docs/specification.md:1193-1201`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:96`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:172`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:635`

**What is wrong.** RFC 0007 says that only what the driver's universes build is built, and that an undriven analysis from a commit runs none of its recipes. Every other result of it is read through its record. In the specification this limit is in §7 prose only. The one runner rule about it, `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT`, forbids building only the outputs built once of an analysis from a commit "that it drives". §9 says it is the only place a rule a runner keeps is stated, and a runner conforms when it keeps the runner rules (`:94-95`). So a runner that builds these results still conforms:

- **Undriven analysis from a commit.** "Unless it is driven, none of the recipes of an analysis from a commit run" (`:804`) has no code. Its pinned outputs (`upstream.summary#u1`) and its outputs built once are uncovered.
- **Driven analysis, results that are not the driver's.** A driven analysis can read a result built in its own universe (a self-pin) or in a universe of an analysis it declares without driving. By §8 that result belongs to the declared analysis, not the driver (`:852`), and no row covers it.

RFC 0007's "Only what the driver's universes build is built" (`0007:172`) is not in the specification. Design §10 traces the undriven case to "spec 7 (follows from 0004's rules)" (`design/...:635`). But 0004's rules limit which addresses can be read, not what a runner builds, and RFC 0004 lets an undriven declared analysis be built in its own universes (0004:291, :363). The restriction is new in 0007 and applies only to commits. Design §11-13 and the tidy-up RFC don't mention the gap.

**Evidence.**

```
rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:96: Unless it's driven (section 7) none of the declared analysis's recipes run so it can only be used for
rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:172: Only what the driver's universes build is built. ...
docs/specification.md:804: **Use.** Unless it is driven, none of the recipes of an analysis from a
docs/specification.md:1025: This section is the rule catalogue: the only place this specification states
docs/specification.md:1026: a rule that a tool checks or keeps. ...
docs/specification.md:1200: | `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT` | MUST | A runner builds none of the outputs built once of an analysis from a commit that it drives, and reads them through their records | 0007 §7 |
```

The prose links the whole limit to the narrower row (`:834-836`): "Its outputs built once aren't built: like the rest of its results, they are read through their records at the declared commit ([`REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT`](#runner))." `grep -c "Only what the driver" docs/specification.md` gives `0`. The runner rules in `rules.json` are `UNRECORDED_RESULT`, `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`, `UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`, `UNDECLARED_READ`, `NOT_AT_COMMIT`, `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT` and `STALE_INPUT`.

Undriven case. This document is valid in both validators. `followup` reads `previous.out#u1` from itself at a commit:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses:
  previous: {path: ., commit: "0dbb615a0fa1b1d6ecf3158e97e35b57c606beb3"}
inputs:
  data: {path: data.csv}
outputs:
  out: {type: metric, format: json, inputs: [data], decisions: [model]}
  compare: {type: report, format: json, inputs: [out, "previous.out#u1"]}
decisions:
  model: {options: {a: {}, b: {}}}
universes:
  u1: {decisions: {model: a}}
```

Driven case. Both validators give VALID for both files, and every validator-layer rule holds by hand (`MISSING_DECISION`, `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN`, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION`, `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE`, `UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE`):

```yaml
# root/astra.yaml (the driver)
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses:
  up: {url: https://example.org/lab/upstream.git, commit: "0dbb615a0fa1b1d6ecf3158e97e35b57c606beb3"}
outputs:
  summary: {type: metric, format: json, inputs: [up.gain]}
universes:
  u1: {decisions: {up.prior: wide}}
```

```yaml
# up as it is at the commit
spec_version: "0.1.0"
decisions:
  prior: {options: {narrow: {}, wide: {}}}
outputs:
  fit:  {type: metric, format: json, decisions: [prior]}
  gain: {type: metric, format: json, inputs: [fit, "fit#baseline"], decisions: [prior]}
universes:
  baseline: {decisions: {prior: narrow}}
```

To build `summary#u1` the runner runs `up.gain` in u1, which reads `fit#baseline`. That pin names `up`'s own universe (`:732-735`), so the result belongs to `up` at the commit. `fit` is built per universe, so the row does not cover it. A variant reading `x_undriven.out#v1` through an undriven second declaration, the pattern RFC 0004 prescribes (`0004:307`), has the same gap.

**Why it matters.** Runner authors working from §9 or `rules.json` find no rule against running these recipes, and a conformance fixture has no code to report. For a `url` remote, `UNRECORDED_RESULT` (`:1195`) already blocks a rebuild, since the record would go into another repository. For a `path`+`commit` declaration it does not. A runner can rebuild from the old files and write the record into the working tree. `NOT_AT_COMMIT` holds, and `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` (`:1196`) allows "the results and records it writes". In the `followup` example, a rebuilt `previous.out#u1` has its record at `.astra/records/u1/out.json` in `.` (§8, `:852-856`, `:866`). That is the same path and address as `followup`'s own `out#u1`, so `MISPLACED_RECORD` does not catch it either. This follows from the text; no runner was run. `STALE_INPUT` limits the damage: it stops `compare` being built while the record at the commit is missing.

**Suggested fix.** Widen `REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT` to every result that belongs to an analysis from a commit, driven or not, for example "A runner builds no result that belongs to an analysis from a commit, and reads each through its record at the declared commit" (0007 §3, §7). Link it from the §7 "Use" paragraph, and carry RFC 0007's "Only what the driver's universes build is built" into §7.

### F030. RFC 0004's "recipes run in their own analysis's directory" has no runner rule or code

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:159-160`, `docs/specification.md:403-404`, `docs/specification.md:22-27`, `docs/specification.md:94-95`, `docs/specification.md:1025-1026`, `docs/specification.md:1203-1206`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:567`

**What is wrong.** RFC 0004 makes a recipe's working directory normative: a recipe runs in its own analysis's directory, even when another analysis drives it. The specification says so only in prose, in §3 and §5, with no code. The §9 runner table has no matching rule. But §1 and §9 say that §9 states every rule a tool keeps, and that a runner conforms when it keeps the runner rules. The working directory is not one of the two uncheckable runner duties that §9 sets aside either. So a runner that ignores it conforms. The prose at l.403-404 also has the same present-tense form as the coded rule at l.425-426, so one implementer reads it as binding and another as informative. Separately, design §10 traces the statement to "spec 3, spec 7". §7 doesn't hold it: it lands in §3 and §5.

**Evidence.**

- `rfcs/0004-composition.md:45`: "An analysis' recipes always run in its own directory." `rfcs/0004-composition.md:361`: "A recipe always runs in its own analysis' directory, but the runner tells it where to write its result (`{output}`), so a driven analysis' recipes write into the driver's results."
- `docs/specification.md:159-160`: "An **analysis** is a directory with an `astra.yaml` at its top. Its recipes run in that directory." `docs/specification.md:403-404`: "A recipe runs in its analysis's directory." Neither links a code.
- `docs/specification.md:1025-1026`: "This section is the rule catalogue: the only place this specification states a rule that a tool checks or keeps." `docs/specification.md:94-95`: a runner "conforms when it keeps the runner rules."
- `docs/specification.md:1203-1205`: the two exempt duties are that a runner "SHOULD let someone see what an environment it recorded holds, and MAY enforce `UNDECLARED_READ`".

```
$ jq -r '.rules[] | select(.layer=="runner") | .code' src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json
UNRECORDED_RESULT
UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES
UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT
UNDECLARED_READ
NOT_AT_COMMIT
REBUILT_FROM_COMMIT
STALE_INPUT
$ jq -r '[.rules[]|select(.statement|test("runs? in|working|director|cwd";"i"))|.code]' src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json
["INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS"]   # about an input's file, not where a recipe runs
```

- `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:567`: "| An analysis is a directory with an `astra.yaml`; recipes run in its directory | spec 3, spec 7 |". In §7, the nearest statement is l.830-831: "Its recipes then run on exactly its files at the declared commit ([`NOT_AT_COMMIT`](#runner))". That is about which files a recipe sees, not where it runs. Design l.535 ("100 rows, all landed. No corrections were needed.") is wrong for this row.

**Why it matters.** It bites runner authors and anyone who states a runner's conformance. `examples/iris_pipeline/feature_extraction/astra.yaml` is a driven analysis whose commands use paths relative to its own directory (`python src/extract.py …`, l.51; `python src/plot.py …`, l.63). Suppose a runner runs them from the driver's directory, where the results go. Then `src/extract.py` resolves against `examples/iris_pipeline/`, the wrong place. Yet that runner keeps all seven runner rules and conforms. With no code, design §12's planned runner fixtures can't cover this duty. Where a substituted `{output}` or `{inputs.<entry>}` path is relative to is also only fixed once the working directory is.

**Suggested fix.** Add a runner row, e.g. `WRONG_WORKING_DIRECTORY` | MUST | A runner runs each recipe in the directory of the recipe's own analysis | 0004 §1, 0004 §5, and link it from §3 and §5. Correct design l.567 to "spec 3, spec 5".

### F031. Fixtures are required per code, not per constraint, so a schema constraint can be loosened with `just test` green

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Internal consistency · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `CLAUDE.md:50`, `CLAUDE.md:65`, `tests/test_specification.py:73`, `tests/test_contract.py:1`, `tests/test_contract.py:195-200`, `tests/test_rules.py:385`, `tests/test_rules.py:421-491`, `tests/test_rules.py:498-512`, `tests/test_types.py:55`, `tests/test_provenance.py:56-84`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-changes.md:87`, `src/astra/schema/types.yaml:109`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:36`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:37`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:43`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:50`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:54`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:56`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:72`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:73`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:96`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:100`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:106`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:119`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:120`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:125`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:131`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:132`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:133`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:145`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:159`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:145`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:156`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:172`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:187`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:197`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:227`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:238`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:257`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:268`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:277`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:281`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:301`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:315`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:360`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:365`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:375`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:388`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:429`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:446-451`, `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:69`, `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:82`, `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:130`, `src/astra/schema/generated/provenance.schema.json:79`, `src/astra/schema/generated/provenance.open.schema.json:79`, `src/astra/schema/generated/provenance.context.jsonld:47-48`, `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-122-no-outputs.yaml:1-4`, `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-124-path-leaves-the-analysis.yaml:6`, `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-125-path-with-dot-dot-segment.yaml:6`, `tests/data/invalid/Analysis-155-home-declaration-path.yaml:15`, `docs/specification.md:230-239`, `docs/specification.md:336-337`, `docs/specification.md:345-348`, `docs/specification.md:1099`, `docs/specification.md:1101`, `docs/specification.md:1107`, `docs/specification.md:1110`, `docs/specification.md:1112`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:403`

**What is wrong.** The fixture rule asks for one invalid fixture per code, and `test_every_schema_rule_has_a_fixture` checks only that. One code covers many constraints: in the closed schemas `WRONG_TYPE` has 120 keywords, `INVALID_ID` 21, `INVALID_VALUE` 16, `MISSING_FIELD` 15. `CODES` in `test_rules.py` is a sample of 45 locations and checks their code, not what they enforce. `test_contract.py` pins the structural properties (`uniqueItems`, closed objects, `propertyNames` present), and `test_types` pins the named types' patterns through a harness. Nothing else pins a constraint. The committed contract is correct today; the gap is that `just test` would not notice a loosening. Every instance below was a mutant of the LinkML source, then `just gen-schema && just test`: drift check clean, `361 passed`, `✓ No problems found`.

| Constraint | Loosening that passes | Witness: committed → mutant |
|---|---|---|
| `required` entries: `Analysis.outputs`; record `spec_version`, `address`, `size`, `Generation.commit`, `.runner`, `RecordedInput.entry`, `.size`, `Runner.id` | `required: true` deleted, each alone or all nine | `spec_version: "0.1.0"` alone: `MISSING_FIELD` → VALID |
| `range: integer` on `ProvenanceRecord.size`, `RecordedInput.size`, `Evidence.version` | `integer` → `float` | `size: 732.5`: `WRONG_TYPE` → VALID |
| `RecordedInput.size` `minimum_value: 0` | line deleted | `inputs[0].size: -10`: `INVALID_VALUE` → VALID |
| A typed slot's type (14 of 20 typed non-key slots) | `range: <type>` → `string`, or an own pattern with the type's `rule` | `incompatible_with: ["~d.b"]`: `INVALID_REFERENCE` → VALID |
| `content_hash`'s `sha256:` prefix (`types.yaml:109`) | `^sha256:` → `^[a-z0-9-]+:` | `blake3:<64 hex>`: `INVALID_VALUE` → VALID |
| A map key's type (seven key slots, eight maps) | `range: id` → `address` plus `rule: INVALID_ID` | `outputs: {result.v2: …}`: `INVALID_ID` → VALID |
| Facets of seven patterns written on a slot | one facet dropped each | `format: .png`, `doi: 10.1/x`, a URL with a space, an IRI with a space, a declaration path with a tab, `excluded: "\t"`, input path `~/data.csv`, `..`, `data/..`: each rejected → VALID |
| The code of a typed slot's pattern | a slot-level `rule` override | input `content_hash: "549eeec2"`: `INVALID_VALUE` → `INVALID_COMMIT` |

**Evidence.** The rule, as written: `CLAUDE.md:50` "A new schema-layer code also needs an invalid fixture."; `CLAUDE.md:65` "Every parse- and schema-layer code in the catalogue has at least one invalid fixture."; `tests/test_specification.py:73-75`:

```python
def test_every_schema_rule_has_a_fixture():
    rules = {fixture_header(path)[0] for path in fixtures("invalid")}
    assert sorted(set(codes("parse", "schema")) - rules) == []
```

Error-map keywords the invalid fixtures reach (closed schemas, `jsonschema`, context errors included):

```
closed error-map keywords: 231
tripped (incl. context errors): 71
  DUPLICATE_ENTRY: 1/14  INVALID_ID: 7/21  INVALID_REFERENCE: 6/9
  INVALID_VALUE: 11/16  MISSING_FIELD: 11/15  UNKNOWN_FIELD: 10/18  WRONG_TYPE: 9/120
```

Three are `required: []` (Universe, Option, AnalysisDeclaration), which no document can trip. Counted over `rules.json`'s 266 locations, closed and open, 185 are reached by no fixture, among them 15 `pattern`s and 3 `minimum`s. jsonschema and Ajv agree on every witness. Per instance:

- **Required fields.** Only five record fields have a `MISSING_FIELD` fixture (201, 202, 203, 204, 213). `Analysis-122-no-outputs.yaml` has `outputs: {}`, so it tests `minProperties`, not `required`. Deleting `provenance.yaml` lines 37, 43, 56, 73, 96, 120, 133, 159 shrinks the generated lists to `root [content_hash, generated_by]; Generation [environment]; RecordedInput [content_hash]; Runner [version]`, and `rules.json` stays byte-identical, since each class keeps one covered entry. Controls: deleting lines 51, 164 or 126 fails fixtures 201, 204 or 213. This record is VALID under the mutant:

  ```yaml
  content_hash: "sha256:7af13cf14f96106b729279ec96430c5b115437053c29aeca4ff70b95770fb83e"
  generated_by:
    inputs:
      - content_hash: "sha256:549eeec2a96f853c662f95e730265f2e2b6fd840047ef2e5e487892a0ed5757e"
    environment:
      iri: "urn:example-runner:env:x"
  ```

  RFC 0004 requires `outputs` (`rfcs/0004-composition.md:403`: "Every analysis has `version`, `name` and `outputs`"), and so does `docs/specification.md:1101`.
- **Integers.** The ten `WRONG_TYPE` fixtures all target string, map or list fields. `grep -rn integer tests/ --include='*.py'` finds nothing. The `RecordedInput.size` mutant also turns the context's `"size": "xsd:integer"` into `"xsd:float"`, and `test_context_maps_the_record_onto_prov` checks `@type` only for `started` and `finished`.
- **Minimum.** `CODES` has only `RECORD + "/properties/size/minimum"` and the `gpus` minimum (`tests/test_rules.py:483-484`). Control: deleting `ProvenanceRecord.size`'s minimum fails fixture 211.
- **Typed slots.** No test compares a slot's generated pattern with its type's. Mutants that went green: `range → string` at `provenance.yaml:36, :119, :125` and `analysis.yaml:238, :277`; own patterns on both `content_hash`s, both `spec_version`s, `Generation.finished`, `.started`, `.commit` (40 only), `AnalysisDeclaration.commit`, `RecordedInput.entry` (no pin), `Option.insights`, `Universe.derived_from`, `Output.inputs`. Two of them reject valid records: a 64-digit `Generation.commit` becomes `INVALID_COMMIT`, a pinned `entry` `INVALID_REFERENCE`. `tests/test_types.py:55` rejects `"sha1:" + HEX_40`, which fails on length whatever the prefix.
- **Map keys.** `test_every_map_constrains_its_keys` (`test_contract.py:195`) asserts only `isinstance(key_pattern(node), str)`; `test_every_map_key_is_an_id` (`test_rules.py:498`) checks only the code, "whatever that type (an `address` included)". The key fixtures (`Result`, `"two "`, `rf-standard`, `séed`) are invalid as addresses too. Control: `range: address` without the annotation fails `test_every_map_key_is_an_id`. The mutant breaks `docs/specification.md:1099` ("Every map key matches its key's type (an `id`, or an `address` in `Universe.decisions`)").
- **Pattern facets.** Mutants: `format` `'^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.+-]*$(?!\n)'` → `'^[a-z0-9_.+-]+$(?!\n)'`; `doi` `'^10\.[0-9]{4,}/…'` → `'^10\.[^\x00-\x1f\x7f]+$(?!\n)'`; `url` `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]` → `[^\x00-\x1f\x7f]`; `iri` → `[^\x00]+`; declaration path drops the control-character class; `excluded` `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]` → `[^ ]`; `Input.path` `^(?![/~])(?!(?:[^\x00-\x1f\x7f]*/)?\.\.(?:/|$))` → `^(?!/)(?!(?:[^\x00-\x1f\x7f]*/)?\.\./)`. The only `~` fixture (155) is a declaration's path, with its own pattern. Each lost facet is stated in normative text: `analysis.yaml:190-192` ("without the leading dot"), `docs/specification.md:502` ("four or more digits"), `analysis.yaml:371-373` and `docs/specification.md:1107` (no control character), `docs/specification.md:446-448`, `provenance.yaml:148` ("an absolute IRI"), `docs/specification.md:345-348` ("doesn't start with `/` or `~`", no `..` segment).
- **Codes.** A slot `rule` annotation wins over its type's (`tools/rules.py:533`). Overrides at `provenance.yaml:36, :106, :119, :125` and `analysis.yaml:238, :277` change only `rules.json` and pass, against `docs/specification.md:235-239`: "a map key or an `id` as `INVALID_ID`, any other address, reference, option reference or condition as `INVALID_REFERENCE`, a commit id as `INVALID_COMMIT`, and the others as `INVALID_VALUE`". Controls: the same edit on `ProvenanceRecord.content_hash` or `Option.insights` fails a fixture or `CODES`.

**Why it matters.** `test_contract.py:1` says the tests exist "so that a LinkML upgrade or a schema edit can't quietly loosen it", and `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-changes.md:87` says "Guards so the contract can't loosen quietly". For slot-level constraints this does not hold. The drift check regenerates from the edited source, so the only sign of a slip is a diff at review. A reviewer who trusts a green `just test` can merge a contract that accepts what §9 forbids (dotted map keys, records without `commit` or `entry`, `size: 732.5`, `~/data.csv`) or rejects valid records, and astra-tools and astra-typescript ship it and report its codes.

**Suggested fix.** Pin each constraint, not each code: a meta-test that every error-map location and every `required` entry is reached by a fixture with its mapped code (with an explicit allow-list), plus contract checks of each typed slot's and map key's pattern and code against its type, and accept/reject tables, as in `test_types`, for each stated facet of a slot's own pattern. Then change `CLAUDE.md:65` to match, or scale back the "can't loosen quietly" claims.

### F032. An input's stand-in can be recorded as its content: RFC 0006's "a stand-in isn't the content" is dropped

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `rfcs/0006-data-inputs.md:55`, `docs/specification.md:356-360`, `docs/specification.md:362-364`, `docs/specification.md:823-827`, `docs/specification.md:907-909`, `docs/specification.md:968-970`, `docs/specification.md:1130`, `docs/specification.md:1183`, `docs/specification.md:1201`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:127-129`

**What is wrong.** RFC 0006 lets a stand-in be committed for an input and then says which one counts: "Something standing in for the content isn't the content". The specification keeps the first half and drops the second. For results it keeps the distinction: a stand-in at a result's place is `absent`, and `STALE_INPUT` makes a runner build only when a result's bytes can be got. For inputs it keeps it only for an analysis from a commit (l.823-827). For the analysis's own inputs, and for a declared analysis given by `path`, nothing says the recipe reads the content and not the stand-in. `RecordedInput.content_hash` is "what the recipe read", and with `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` "the file an input names" can be the stand-in.

**Evidence.**

- `rfcs/0006-data-inputs.md:55`: "What's committed is the file itself, or something standing in for it that tooling recognises ... Something standing in for the content isn't the content".
- `docs/specification.md:356-360` allows the stand-in and says no more. `grep -n "isn't the content\|not the content" docs/specification.md` finds nothing, and `git log -S"isn't the content"` shows the sentence was never in the specification.
- `docs/specification.md:1130`: `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` "An input's `path` names a file, or something standing in for one".
- `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:127-129`: "The SHA-256 digest of what the recipe read through the entry: another output's result, or the file an input names."
- `docs/specification.md:1201`: `STALE_INPUT` "A runner builds an output only when every result it reads is `unchanged` and its bytes can be got and are the recorded ones". It covers results only. No other runner code concerns an input's file.

A 28-byte `data.csv` committed through Git LFS; a clone without git-lfs has the pointer at the path:

```
content sha256:2aa77e0156ee827b633df92c81ecf1d09e57cb7a6c3e23eaed6cf59a00c53d38 size 28
pointer sha256:09d102230bfd3bb45cd6165bd0ee5abba6b18277eeb7871ed3bd026ea2b072f1 size 127
```

A runner that runs the recipe on the pointer and records its hash breaks no rule of §9. Both records, content and pointer, validate (as expected at the schema layer).

**Why it matters.** Two conforming runners record different input hashes for the same commit, so comparing records to tell whether a result reproduces (l.953-955) fails across them. A checker applying `STALE_RESULT` ("its inputs' content", l.1183) gets `unchanged` if it hashes the file at the path and `stale` if it resolves the pointer. The method is left to the tooling (l.1002-1003), so the two disagree. Unsmudged LFS clones are common in CI. A DVC stand-in sits beside the path, so the recipe fails to read and records nothing.

**Suggested fix.** Restore RFC 0006's sentence in §5 and make `RecordedInput.content_hash` and `STALE_RESULT` say the content, not a stand-in. Optionally widen `STALE_INPUT` (or add a runner code) so a runner refuses when an input's content can't be got. That is new normative content and belongs in the tidy-up RFC.

### F033. Nothing defines when a universe drives a declaration

- **Lens:** Implementer · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:48`, `docs/specification.md:543-545`, `docs/specification.md:562-570`, `docs/specification.md:664-665`, `docs/specification.md:690-695`, `docs/specification.md:697-704`, `docs/specification.md:725-727`, `docs/specification.md:732-735`, `docs/specification.md:742`, `docs/specification.md:1135`, `docs/specification.md:1140`, `docs/specification.md:1146`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:137`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:221`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:334`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:780`

**What is wrong.** §7 defines driving in one sentence (l.690-695) and never says when a given universe drives a given declaration. `MISSING_DECISION`, `PARTIALLY_DRIVEN`, `UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION` and `UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE` all depend on that term. Three questions are left open:

| Question | Readings | Effect |
|---|---|---|
