LinkML's Turtle prints `astra:env:sha256:abc a prov:Entity .`. With `"iri": "astra:commit"` the environment node is `https://w3id.org/astra/commit`, the predicate IRI of the record's `commit`.

JSON-LD 1.1 API §5.2.2 (IRI Expansion), step 6.4: "If active context contains a term definition for prefix having a non-null IRI mapping and the prefix flag of the term definition is true, return the result of concatenating the IRI mapping associated with prefix and suffix."

**Why it matters.** A runner author who picks `astra:` as their scheme, a natural choice for an ASTRA runner, gets an environment that RDF merges with `https://w3id.org/astra/env:…`, and possibly with an ASTRA vocabulary term. A checker comparing record strings (§8, "the same commit, inputs and environment") sees two environments where an RDF consumer sees one. The two implementers disagree, and the description gives runner authors no way to know which schemes to avoid.

**Suggested fix.** Say in the description and §8/§10 that `iri` is never a CURIE, and either exclude the context's prefix names in the pattern (`(?!(astra|linkml|prov|schema|xsd):)`) or name an RDF route that keeps absolute IRIs as written. `range: uri` alone does not help: LinkML's dumper treats any value without `://` as a CURIE whatever the range. For the same reason the pattern fix alone leaves the spec's own `urn:` example failing in `linkml-convert` ("Unknown CURIE prefix: urn"), a related defect with the same cause.

### F071. A checker without an input's bytes has no correct `STALE_RESULT` status when the stand-in carries no content hash

- **Lens:** Standards · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:356-360`, `docs/specification.md:985-991`, `docs/specification.md:1002-1003`, `docs/specification.md:1161-1169`, `docs/specification.md:1183`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:173`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:193`, `rfcs/0006-data-inputs.md:55`

**What is wrong.** A result is unchanged only if nothing has changed in "the content of its inputs" (:991, :1183). An input's bytes needn't be in git: the spec accepts "a git-annex link or a Git LFS pointer at the path, or a DVC file beside it" with no backend required, and "How a large file's bytes are kept is the tooling's" (:356-360). The method is left open: "How a checker finds this out is the tooling's choice" (:1002-1003). Yet every planned result gets one of `unchanged`, `stale` or `missing` (:1161-1163), and `stale` means "The checker says what changed" (:1169).

LFS pointers, SHA256E and MD5E annex keys, and a single-file `.dvc` carry a content hash, so a checker can compare them across commits. A WORM annex key carries size, mtime and file name, but no hash. When the bytes can't be fetched, nothing settles the input's content. `unchanged` is unfounded. `stale` breaks the definition when the content is the same, and leaves the checker with nothing true to name as the change. The spec offers no third outcome, so implementers will pick `stale`, `unchanged` or an error, each differently. The same gap is in RFC 0005 §4 (:173, :193). RFC 0006:55 says "Something standing in for the content isn't the content", but the spec doesn't carry that sentence.

The gap covers file inputs only. A result read as an input can be compared from the records' hashes alone.

**Evidence.** Both documents are VALID under `validate.py` and `validate.mjs`. `astra.yaml` has `inputs: {data: {path: data/in.csv}}`, read by output `total`. Record `total.json` has `inputs: [{entry: data, content_hash: "sha256:492d5ea4…7470", size: 8}]`. The input was added with `--backend=WORM` and committed (C1). It was then unlocked, only its mtime was touched, and it was re-added and committed (HEAD). git-annex 10.20260421:

```
$ nix shell nixpkgs#git-annex nixpkgs#git nixpkgs#coreutils --command bash worm.sh
key at C1:   WORM-s8-m1793784231--data/in.csv
key at HEAD: WORM-s8-m1767308400--data/in.csv
492d5ea496056f1a6a6592241032fab764c321596317930b4fa0e1e8bc3b7470  data/in.csv   (C1 and HEAD)
git diff --stat C1 HEAD:  data/in.csv | 2 +-
--- what a clone without the bytes sees
bytes absent (dangling link)
```

Under :991 the right status is `unchanged`. A clone can't establish that without the bytes. With DVC 3.67.1, a single-file `data/in.csv.dvc` stays the same after an mtime-only change (`git diff C1 HEAD -- data/in.csv.dvc` is empty), so that case can be decided.

**Why it matters.** astra-tools and astra-typescript must each invent the behaviour. The same commit can get `stale` from one checker and `unchanged` from another, or from the same checker before and after `git annex get`. Evidence citing the result inherits the status (:1163).

**Suggested fix.** Say what a checker reports when it can't compare an input's content: compare by bytes (fetched if needed), with a stand-in counting only if it names the SHA-256, or add an explicit outcome for "can't tell". Carry RFC 0006's "a stand-in isn't the content" into the spec. Tom decides.

### F072. The harness's YAML loader accepts ill-formed YAML 1.2 that the spec makes `INVALID_SYNTAX`

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `tests/conftest.py:126-132`, `tests/conftest.py:135-147`, `docs/specification.md:79`, `docs/specification.md:163-165`, `docs/specification.md:1097`, `CLAUDE.md:56`

**What is wrong.** The spec makes the parse layer "any strict YAML 1.2 loader" and `INVALID_SYNTAX` a MUST. The harness loader is ruamel.yaml 0.19.1's pure safe loader. Its restrictions cover the `%YAML` directive, the resolver and the constructor. Nothing tightens the scanner. It loads three common author slips that YAML 1.2.2 forbids: a comment glued to a quoted scalar (§6.6, production [77]), and a flow sequence or a quoted scalar whose continuation line is not indented past its parent (§8.2.3, productions [69], [197]). It loads each one as the author meant it, so the file passes. yaml 2.9.1, the conformant stand-in used by the review, rejects all three. The contract is not at fault: once the text is parsed, both stacks give the same verdict. The defect is in the harness, which claims to validate "as a consumer would".

**Evidence.** Minimal `Analysis` documents, each with one slip. The same files written correctly are VALID in both stacks.

```yaml
    format: "json"# the default          # A: comment glued to a quoted scalar
    tags: [ml,                           # B2: under outputs.result, continuation aligned with the key
    baseline]
    description: "A long description     # C2: same, for a double-quoted scalar
    wrapped onto a second line"
```

```
Python (conftest.load_yaml + jsonschema)   A, B2, C2: VALID
  A  -> "format": "json"   B2 -> "tags": ["ml", "baseline"]
  C2 -> "description": "A long description wrapped onto a second line"
yaml 2.9.1 + Ajv
  A   PARSE-ERROR MISSING_CHAR: Comments must be separated from other tokens by white space characters
  B2  PARSE-ERROR BAD_INDENT: Flow sequence in block collection must be sufficiently indented and end with a ]
  C2  PARSE-ERROR MISSING_CHAR: Missing closing "quote
```

yaml-test-suite (data-2022-01-17): of the 94 cases with an `error` file, `conftest.load_yaml_text` accepts 9: `9C9N 9JBA CVW2 DK95/01 G5U8 QB6E SU5Z Y79Y/003 YJV2`. yaml 2.9.1 rejects all 9. For example, SU5Z (`key: "value"# invalid comment`) loads as `{'key': 'value'}`.

`docs/specification.md:1097`: "Every `astra.yaml` is well-formed YAML 1.2 under the core schema". The only `INVALID_SYNTAX` fixtures are `Analysis-182-malformed-yaml.yaml` (an unclosed flow map) and `Analysis-183-yaml-1-1-directive.yaml`. Design §12 and §13 record no limit on ruamel's conformance.

**Why it matters.** `load_yaml` and `load_yaml_text` feed `test_examples.py:14`, `test_fixtures.py:44,55,59` and the spec-fence checks (`test_specification.py:103`). Those checks can pass for the wrong reason: a valid fixture, an example or a spec example containing one of these slips would pass `just test` and fail with `INVALID_SYNTAX` in a strict loader. No committed file is affected today. All valid fixtures, all `examples/**/astra.yaml` and all spec YAML fences parse under yaml 2.9.1.

**Suggested fix.** Also parse the valid fixtures, examples and spec fences with a conformant YAML 1.2 parser, or subclass the scanner to reject these cases. ruamel has no option for this. Add `# fails at: parse` fixtures for a glued comment and an under-indented continuation, and add both to the check-yaml and yamllint exclusions. If ruamel stays, record its leniency in design §12 and stop calling the harness loader strict.

### F073. Nothing says which bytes a content hash covers, the committed blob or the checked-out file, and git's checkout conversions make them differ in a clean clone

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Important · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:362-364`, `docs/specification.md:774-776`, `docs/specification.md:784-786`, `docs/specification.md:823-827`, `docs/specification.md:891-892`, `docs/specification.md:905-909`, `docs/specification.md:955-957`, `docs/specification.md:991`, `docs/specification.md:1001-1003`, `docs/specification.md:1007-1010`, `docs/specification.md:1019-1021`, `docs/specification.md:1171`, `docs/specification.md:1183`, `docs/specification.md:1187-1189`, `docs/specification.md:1201`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:52`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:128-129`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:46`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:242`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:84`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:130`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:638`

**What is wrong.** A record's `content_hash` and `size` are "of the result's bytes" (provenance.yaml:52), and an input's are "of what the recipe read through the entry" (provenance.yaml:128-129). The text never says which bytes those are, and passages imply different ones:

- Local `present` hashes "the bytes at the result's default place" (spec:1171). In practice that is the working tree: the record is committed only in a later commit (spec:975-976), and RFC 0005:242 has the file agree after `git lfs pull`, so after a smudge filter.
- §7 reads an analysis from a commit with "no checkout" (spec:776), and its result bytes "come from what git holds at the result's default place at the declared commit" (spec:823-826): git's stored form. Spec:1019-1021 and 1187-1189 say both paths are "checked by the same rules".
- "What git holds" splits again: `git cat-file blob` gives the stored bytes, `git cat-file blob --filters` (and `git archive`) the working-tree bytes, which depend on the reader's git config.
- Staleness compares "the content of its inputs" (spec:991, 1183), undefined. The commit reader sees only "which differ between two commits, without their contents" (spec:784-785), and "How a checker finds this out is the tooling's choice" (spec:1002-1003).

Git's checkout conversions (`core.autocrlf`, `text eol=crlf`, `ident`, `working-tree-encoding`, a clean/smudge filter) make the working-tree bytes differ from the blob while `git status` is clean. In those clones the readings give opposite statuses for one result at one commit. No decision in the design, its §11-§13, the RFCs or the tidy-up draft addresses this.

**Evidence.** Toy runners on spec §2's analysis with a committed `data/iris.csv`, git 2.56.0. Every analysis, declaring document and record passes both toolkit validators (Python and Ajv). Scripts: `/tmp/claude-1001/-home-r-Projects-LightconeResearch-astra-spec/836ccf00-3f7e-40b0-b6e4-af24d5cd3606/scratchpad/work/repro-c195/`.

(1) Built on Linux (LF), cloned with `core.autocrlf=true` (Git for Windows' installer default):

```
== clone -c core.autocrlf=true; status --porcelain: ''
  result results/forest/accuracy.json: record 74fc5701c3d6 19B | wtree 03649cf09192 20B -> absent | blob 74fc5701c3d6 19B -> present
    input iris = data/iris.csv: record dbbb6986b9d2 61B | wtree 748b61a66fa0 65B -> CHANGED | blob dbbb6986b9d2 61B -> same
git cat-file blob           HEAD:data/iris.csv -> dbbb6986b9d2
git cat-file blob --filters HEAD:data/iris.csv -> 748b61a66fa0   (= working tree)
```

(2) Built in an autocrlf clone whose recipes write CRLF, checked in a plain clone:

```
  result results/forest/accuracy.json: record 03649cf09192 20B | wtree 74fc5701c3d6 19B -> absent | blob 74fc5701c3d6 19B -> absent
    input iris: record 748b61a66fa0 65B | wtree dbbb6986b9d2 61B -> CHANGED | blob dbbb6986b9d2 61B -> CHANGED
git diff --quiet A HEAD -- data/iris.csv: exit 0   (same blob 87059e526eb0 at A and B)
```

The same commit A gives two records of `accuracy#forest`: `iris` as `sha256:dbbb6986b9d2… 61` and as `sha256:748b61a66fa0… 65`.

(3) Conversions in committed `.gitattributes`, built and checked in fresh clones (status `''`):

```
3a '*.csv text eol=crlf':  input iris: wtree -> same, blob -> CHANGED;  comparison.csv: wtree 42B -> absent, blob 39B -> present
3b '*.csv ident':          input iris: record 111B = wtree -> same, blob 68B -> CHANGED
3c 'working-tree-encoding=UTF-16LE-BOM': input iris: wtree 124B -> same, blob 61B -> CHANGED
3d 'filter=crypt' (clean/smudge): wtree -> same, blob -> CHANGED; clone without the driver: both CHANGED
```

(4) One commit read three ways (`*.json text eol=crlf`, Linux build):

```
git cat-file blob      B:results/forest/accuracy.json -> ce0b1dedce5f 23B (present)
git cat-file --filters B:results/forest/accuracy.json -> 5a928c841a57 26B (absent)
git archive B -- results/forest/accuracy.json         -> 5a928c841a57 26B (absent)
```

There the upstream record says its recipe read `iris` as 69 bytes; a downstream recipe reading `upstream.iris` from the commit (spec:826-827) gets the 66-byte blob.

**Why it matters.** Checker authors (astra-tools, astra-typescript) must pick a reading, and by spec:1002-1003 any reading conforms, so two conforming checkers give one clean clone different statuses. Tools on raw blobs (libgit2, isomorphic-git) and tools on `--filters` or a checkout disagree. It bites anyone building on Windows with `core.autocrlf=true` (which Pro Git recommends) and any repository with `eol` or filter attributes. A Windows-built analysis is `present` on the building machine and `absent` and `stale` elsewhere though git shows nothing changed, so `STALE_INPUT` (spec:1201) blocks every analysis that reads it through a declaration, including the spec's self-declaration pattern. A runner checking the working tree refuses to build; one checking blobs builds on input bytes that differ from the recorded ones. Records of one commit made on two machines differ, so spec:955-957's reproducibility comparison fails for a result that reproduces.

**Suggested fix.** Say once, in §8, which bytes every `content_hash` and `size` covers (git's stored blob, or the checked-out file), and have `present`, STALE_RESULT, STALE_INPUT and §7's read-from-a-commit path cite it. If the checked-out file, add a coded rule (or at least a SHOULD) that inputs and result places carry no git conversion (`-text`) other than a recognised stand-in's filter.

### F074. "A driven analysis's own universes aren't used" is contradicted by §7's own example and by own-universe pins

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:705-706`, `docs/specification.md:732-752`, `docs/specification.md:664-665`, `docs/specification.md:852-853`, `docs/specification.md:1146`, `examples/iris/astra.yaml:75-77`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:149`

**What is wrong.** §7 says flatly that "A driven analysis's own universes aren't used" (705). The same section contradicts that sentence twice. First, its example reads a driven analysis in one of its own universes, through a second, undriven declaration of the same directory (741, 751). §7 calls such a directory "the same analysis" (664). Second, a pin in the driven analysis's own file names "one of its own universes" (732-735, row 1146), and no rule makes an exception while the analysis is driven. §8 has the same overstatement: "so the results of a driven analysis belong to its driver" (853). Read alone, it contradicts the rule just before it (852).

The specific rules already settle the cases that matter. The pin is valid (732-735, 1146). A pinned result is read "as built in the universe `svm`, whichever universe is being built" (616-617). The result belongs to "the analysis whose universe it was built in" (852). "What is built in a driver's universes belongs to the driver" (756) gives the exact scope. So the defect is two over-broad sentences, not a missing rule. Line 705 restates RFC 0004:149 ("universes existing in those other driven analyses are considered to not exist"). RFC 0004 has the same tension with its own pin rule (0004:357), so this is not a departure from the RFCs.

**Evidence.**

```
docs/specification.md:705  - A driven analysis's own universes aren't used. Each universe being built
docs/specification.md:741    inner: {path: stage/inner}            # the root's universes set inner.d
docs/specification.md:751      inputs: [inner_undriven.result#v1]  # inner's own universe v1
docs/specification.md:664  The same directory is the same analysis, whichever analysis declares it and
docs/specification.md:852  A result belongs to the analysis whose universe it was built in, so the
docs/specification.md:853  results of a driven analysis belong to its driver. ...
```

A driven analysis whose own output pins its own universes:

```yaml
# a/astra.yaml (the driver)
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses: {stage: {path: stage}}
outputs: {report: {type: report, format: md, inputs: [stage.result, stage.cmp]}}
universes: {r1: {decisions: {stage.d: d1}}, r2: {decisions: {stage.d: d2}}}
# a/stage/astra.yaml (driven; stands alone)
spec_version: "0.1.0"
decisions: {d: {options: {d1: {}, d2: {}}}}
outputs:
  result: {type: metric, format: json, decisions: [d]}
  cmp: {type: table, format: csv, inputs: [result#v1, result#v2]}
universes: {v1: {decisions: {d: d1}}, v2: {decisions: {d: d2}}}
```

Both files are VALID in the Python and Ajv validators. That is expected, because the rules involved are validator-layer rules. By the text, `result#v1` and `result#v2` name stage's own `v1` and `v2`, so `UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE` and `PIN_INACTIVE` pass. `cmp` needs `result` built in the universes that 705 says "aren't used". The same holds for `examples/iris/astra.yaml:75-77` (`accuracy#baseline`, `accuracy#svm_focused`) and for the §2 example's `comparison`, once a parent drives either of them. Both driven setups are VALID in both validators.

**Why it matters.** A runner author who reads 705 literally won't build a driven analysis's own universes. Their runner then can't build §7's own example, or examples/iris once a parent drives it. A checker author who reads 853 literally files `stage.result#v1` under the driver, not under stage. The correct reading can be recovered, but only against two flat sentences that say otherwise.

**Suggested fix.** Reword 705: "In a driver's universes, a driven analysis's own universes don't set its decisions; a pin in its own file, or through an undriven declaration, still reads them." Reword 853: "so what a driven analysis builds in its driver's universes belongs to the driver."

### F075. UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION is worded two ways, and neither wording states the rule alone

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Internal consistency · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:720-727`, `docs/specification.md:735-752`, `docs/specification.md:1031-1033`, `docs/specification.md:1142`, `docs/specification.md:1143`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:564`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:411`

**What is wrong.** §7 words the rule about the element ("it is reached through a declaration that analysis drives"). §9 words it about the read ("every such read goes through a declaration that analysis drives"). Each wording loses a case that RFC 0004 and the other wording cover:

| Wording | Problem | Case it lets through |
| --- | --- | --- |
| §9 / `rules.json` | "every such read" takes its subject from the row above. In `rules.json` the statement has no subject; it is the only one of the 65 statements that leans on another row. "Read" also leaves out decisions, which are "used", not read. | A consumed decision of another analysis reached through an undriven declaration |
| §7 | "it is reached through" can mean "some driven declaration in the file leads to the element". | An unpinned read through the undriven second declaration of a driven directory |

**Evidence.**

- `docs/specification.md:725-726` (§7): "in the file of the analysis whose universe is being built, it is reached through a declaration that analysis drives".
- `docs/specification.md:1143` (§9), same `statement` at `rules.json:564`: "In the file of the analysis whose universe is being built, every such read goes through a declaration that analysis drives".
- `docs/specification.md:1142`, the antecedent: "Every output of another analysis read without a pin, and every decision of another analysis, is used only where that analysis is built in the universe being built".
- `docs/specification.md:1031-1033`: the Rule cell is "one sentence … its subject is what the rule is about".
- `rfcs/0004-composition.md:411`, self-contained and settling both cases: "another analysis' output read without a pin and another analysis' decision go through a declaration that analysis drives (`subsubanalysis1_undriven.output1` has no match) | error".
- The pattern at :735-752 shows only pinned reads, so the failing case is never shown.

Two schema-valid stacks (the rule is validator-layer) on which the readings disagree:

```yaml
# Decision through an undriven declaration: §7 fails it; §9's "read" does not cover it
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses:
  b: {path: stages/b}        # stages/b declares c: {path: ../c}, decision x
  c_own: {path: stages/c}    # stages/c has decision d
outputs:
  z: {type: metric, format: json, decisions: [c_own.d]}
universes:
  u1: {decisions: {b.x: x1, b.c.d: p}}
```

```yaml
# Read through an undriven second declaration: §9 fails it; §7 read element-wise passes it
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses:
  inner: {path: stage/inner}            # driven
  inner_undriven: {path: stage/inner}   # never named in the root's universes
outputs:
  in_root:
    type: metric
    format: json
    inputs: [inner_undriven.result]     # unpinned, through the undriven declaration
universes:
  u1: {decisions: {inner.d: x}}
  u2: {decisions: {inner.d: y}}
```

In the first, C is built in `u1` through `b.c`, so `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` holds, but `z` reaches `d` through `c_own`, which `u1` never drives. In the second, `stage/inner` declares decision `d` (options `x`, `y`), output `result` with `decisions: [d]`, and universe `v1`; `NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE` holds (same directory, same analysis, :664-665), and `result` is reached through `inner`, but the read goes through `inner_undriven`.

**Why it matters.** astra-tools and astra-typescript show the catalogue's wording next to the code (D13), and for this rule that wording has no subject. A validator written from §9 or `rules.json` alone can skip consumed decisions; one written from §7 alone can accept a read through an undriven declaration, which gives the result two names (:855-856). The specification settles both cases only through RFC 0004, so this is a wording defect.

**Suggested fix.** Give §9 and §7 one self-contained wording about the address, covering outputs and decisions, e.g. "In the file of the analysis whose universe is being built, every address that reads an output of another analysis without a pin, or uses a decision of another analysis, goes through a declaration that analysis drives"; regenerate `rules.json`, update the design's copy of the table (`design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:393`), and add the RFC's failing `inner_undriven.result` case below the §7 example. Tom decides.

### F076. The `id` row of §4's named-types table reads as excluding `Universe.derived_from`, `Option.insights` and the selection values

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Author, Internal consistency, Standards, Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:225`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:196`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:266-268`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:314-315`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:345-348`

**What is wrong.** The "Used by" cell of the `id` row puts the three fields that use `id` straight after "except those of", in one comma list. Read as written, `Universe.derived_from`, `Option.insights` and the values of `Universe.decisions` are further exceptions. The intended meaning is two parts: every map key except the keys of `Universe.decisions`, plus those three fields. The contract has the intended meaning. This is wording.

The design's cell (design:196) has no "except" and is wrong the other way: it gives `id` "every map key", while its own `address` row (design:197) takes "the keys of `Universe.decisions`". The spec added the exception to fix that, and joined it to the list.

**Evidence.**

```
$ sed -n 225p docs/specification.md
| [`id`](elements/Id.md) | `id` | every map key except those of `Universe.decisions`, `Universe.derived_from`, `Option.insights`, the values of `Universe.decisions` |
```

No other row gives these three fields a type; the `address` row (spec:226) has only "`Output.decisions`, the keys of `Universe.decisions`". In the LinkML, all three have `range: id` (`insights` at analysis.yaml:268, `derived_from` at :315, `DecisionSelection.option` at :346), and a SchemaView pass finds no other non-key slot with `range: id`. So the intended list is complete.

A valid document, each bad variant changing one line, gives INVALID_ID in both Python `jsonschema` and Ajv:

```
/universes/alt/derived_from              | …#/$defs/Universe__identifier_optional/properties/derived_from/pattern               | INVALID_ID   ('stage.base')
/decisions/model/options/rf/insights/0   | …#/$defs/Option__identifier_optional/properties/insights/items/pattern               | INVALID_ID   ('Noise')
/universes/base/decisions/model          | …#/$defs/Universe__identifier_optional/properties/decisions/additionalProperties/pattern | INVALID_ID ('Rf')
```

A careful reader can recover the meaning. "Those of" can't apply to a scalar (`derived_from`) or a list (`insights`), and "except … the values of" doesn't parse. Spec:452 says `insights` "are the ids of the prior insights", and spec:540 says a selection value "is the id of one of that decision's options". For `derived_from`, though, the spec says only that it "names another universe" (spec:543), so the table is the one place in the spec that gives it a type. The LinkML description ("The id of another universe", analysis.yaml:317) says it too.

**Why it matters.** Implementers who build checks or editor completion from the spec's table, not the JSON Schema, could leave these fields unchecked or give them the wrong code. The prose after the table (spec:235-236) assigns codes by type: "a map key or an `id` as `INVALID_ID`, any other address, reference … as `INVALID_REFERENCE`".

**Suggested fix.** Split the two parts: "every map key but those of `Universe.decisions`; also `Universe.derived_from`, `Option.insights` and the values of `Universe.decisions`". Or list the three fields first.

### F077. "The same directory is the same analysis" ignores the commit, and "the same commit and `path`" compares a relative path

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Author · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:664-669`, `docs/specification.md:758-766`, `docs/specification.md:768-770`, `docs/specification.md:633-634`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:365`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:61`

**What is wrong.** §7 defines an analysis's identity twice, and each definition is wrong if read literally. Line 664 gives no exception for a `commit`: "The same directory is the same analysis, whichever analysis declares it and by whatever id: it is read once". This sentence comes from RFC 0004:65. The spec omits the qualifier RFC 0007 adds to it (rfcs/0007:61, "A declaration with a `path` and no `commit` means exactly what it means in RFC 0004"). Lines 768-770 make identity "the same commit and `path`". But `path` is written relative to the declaring file (l.633-634), and the schema does not normalise it. So one directory can be spelled several ways, and one spelling can name different directories. Line 770 repeats RFC 0007:82 almost word for word, so this second half is a wording ambiguity in the RFC, not a departure from it.

A careful reading resolves both halves the same way. Line 668-669 ("A declaration with a `commit` is never part of a cycle"), l.758-761 ("as it was at that commit"), l.765-766 and l.633 rule out the literal readings. No single sentence states the identity rule correctly, though.

**Evidence.** Both validators accept every document below; identity is a validator-layer matter.

```yaml
# e2: one directory, with and without a commit
analyses:
  lib_then: {path: lib, commit: "0dbb615a0fa1b1d6ecf3158e97e35b57c606beb3"}
  lib_now: {path: lib}
```
Line 664 says these are one analysis, read once. Lines 758-761 say `lib_then` is lib as it was at the commit.

```yaml
# e4: one directory, one commit, four `path` strings
analyses:
  a: {path: lib, commit: "0dbb…"}
  b: {path: ./lib, commit: "0dbb…"}
  c: {path: lib/, commit: "0dbb…"}
  d: {path: sub/../lib, commit: "0dbb…"}
```
Comparing `path` strings splits this one analysis into four. The opposite also happens. `previous: {path: ., commit: X}` in both `stage1/astra.yaml` and `stage2/astra.yaml` has the same commit and `path`, but the two name different directories. The declaration pattern is `'^(?![/~])[^\x00-\x1f\x7f\\]+$(?!\n)'` (analysis.yaml:365).

The case is wider than "with a commit". A declaration by `path` alone that is reached through an analysis from a commit is also read at that commit (l.765-766; l.778-779 lists "the `astra.yaml` of the declared analysis and of every analysis it declares"):

```yaml
# e5: the root; its astra.yaml at 0dbb… also declares lib: {path: lib}
analyses:
  lib: {path: lib}
  previous: {path: ., commit: "0dbb…"}
```
`lib` and `previous.lib` are the same directory, and neither is declared with a commit. Yet one is the working tree and the other is lib at 0dbb….

**Why it matters.** Validator and runner authors who key a cache on the directory (l.664) merge an analysis with its earlier self. They then read the working tree instead of the commit, which breaks RFC 0007's "compare with an earlier commit" use. Authors who compare `path` strings (l.770) read one analysis several times, or merge two different ones. Either mistake changes how universes are pushed and which selections conflict. The tidy-up RFC already fixed this problem for commits written two ways (NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:48, change 25), but not for paths.

**Suggested fix.** State identity once. An analysis is its directory, resolved from the top of its repository, together with the commit it is read at. That commit is the declaration's `commit` or, without one, the commit its declaring analysis is read at (the working tree for the root and anything reached from it by `path` alone). For a `url`, it is the commit alone. Then qualify l.664 and l.770 by that definition.

### F078. §9 says §8 states the two runner "duties", but §8 writes them in lowercase, and one of them is a MAY

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Standards · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:1203-1206`, `docs/specification.md:24-27`, `docs/specification.md:967-968`, `docs/specification.md:972-973`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:453-456`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:606`

**What is wrong.** The note after the runner table states the two statements no tool can check in capitals. Its last sentence then says "The specification states them in its §8." §8 writes both in lowercase, and §1 makes keywords normative "when, and only when, they appear in all capitals". So §8 does not state them normatively. §1 says the opposite of §9: the duties are stated "after the runner's rules (§9)". The lowercase in §8 is deliberate. Commit 62ef963 ("keywords only where §1 says") lowercased §8 and repointed §1 at §9, but left §9's closing sentence as it was. The sentence is a stale pointer. The design's copy of the note (design:453-456) has the same sentence.

Both §1 (spec:26) and §9 (spec:1203) also call the pair "duties". One of them is a MAY, which RFC 2119 §5 defines as "truly optional".

The levels themselves are right. §9's SHOULD and MAY match RFC 0005:151 and :155.

**Evidence.**

```
docs/specification.md:13-16    ... when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here.
docs/specification.md:24-27    ... and the two duties of a runner that no tool can check after the runner's rules (§9).
docs/specification.md:1203-1206
  Two duties that are not rules of their own, because nothing can check them: a
  runner SHOULD let someone see what an environment it recorded holds, and MAY
  enforce `UNDECLARED_READ` (with a sandbox, say). The specification states them
  in its §8.
docs/specification.md:967-968  ..., and should let someone see what that environment holds, as PROV for example.
docs/specification.md:972-973  A runner may enforce this, with a sandbox say.
```

In the same §8 subsection the merge recommendation, which §1 names, is in capitals: spec:978-979 "branches with records SHOULD be merged".

```
$ git show 62ef963 -- docs/specification.md
-environment as an IRI that it can recreate at its version, and SHOULD let
+([`UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`](#runner)), and should let someone see what that
-only through its output's inputs, which a runner MAY enforce
+it uses is pinned by the commit or is part of the environment. A runner may
```

The commit message says "§8's runner duties are written in prose". No later commit touches spec:1203-1206.

The design's traceability table is also off. Row 606 reads "Environment as a recreatable IRI; SHOULD be inspectable | `UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT`; `Environment.iri`". Neither the rule (spec:1197) nor `Environment.iri`'s description (`provenance.yaml:148-152`) says anything about inspecting the environment. Row 607 ("runners MAY enforce | `UNDECLARED_READ`") is a milder case of the same thing: the MAY is in the note, not in the `UNDECLARED_READ` row (spec:1198).

**Why it matters.** A runner author who follows §9's pointer lands on lowercase text that §1 says is not binding, and §8's links to `#runner` send them straight back. "Duty" tells them that enforcing `UNDECLARED_READ` is expected when it is optional. No implementer gets a wrong level, because §9's capitals are clear. This is a wording and cross-reference defect.

**Suggested fix.** Delete "The specification states them in its §8." (or write "§8 explains them") in spec:1205-1206 and design:455-456. Keep §8 lowercase. Call the pair "a recommendation and a permission" in spec:26 and spec:1203, and point design rows 606-607 at §9's closing note.

### F079. A declaration or evidence item that isn't an object gets a second code, INVALID_*_SOURCE, at its own pointer

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Author, Standards · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `src/astra/schema/generated/analysis.schema.json:15`, `src/astra/schema/generated/analysis.schema.json:123`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:395`, `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:98`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:763-765`, `tests/test_fixtures.py:73`

**What is wrong.** Each exactly-one-of branch is only `{"properties": {"path": {}}, "required": ["path"]}` (and the same for `url`, `doi` and `artifact`). `required` holds vacuously on a non-object. So for a string, null, list or integer declaration, both branches pass and the `oneOf` fails as "valid under each of". The document gets INVALID_DECLARATION_SOURCE or INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE beside the WRONG_TYPE that §4 promises (`docs/specification.md:266-268`), and both are at the same pointer. This covers every non-object `analyses` entry, and every non-object `evidence` item under `findings` and `prior_insights`. A non-object Output, whose class has no `oneOf`, gets only WRONG_TYPE.

Design §12 records the mechanism for one fixture only: "`Analysis-159` still reports a second code (its ancestor `oneOf`) because LinkML 1.12.1 can't type an `exactly_one_of` branch" (`design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:763-765`). This case is worse in two ways. It hits the most likely slips (`iris: ../iris`, an empty `iris:`, `evidence: [r]`), not only `path: null` beside `url`. And the extra error is at the failing pointer itself, not at an ancestor, so the harness can't excuse it (CLAUDE.md:65: "a `oneOf`/`anyOf` error at an ancestor aside"). Rejecting these documents is right: no RFC gives a string form (`rfcs/0004-composition.md:51-53`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:43-46`). Both codes are true, since a string has neither `path` nor `url`. Only the reason the validators give ("valid under each") is false.

**Evidence.**

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses:
  iris: ../iris          # also `iris:`, `iris: [../iris]`, `iris: 3`
outputs:
  r: {type: metric, format: json}
findings:
  f: {claim: r is high., evidence: [r]}
```

Python (`jsonschema`, Draft 2019-09):

```
/analyses/iris | …#/$defs/AnalysisDeclaration__identifier_optional/oneOf | INVALID_DECLARATION_SOURCE | '../iris' is valid under each of {'properties': {'url': {}}, 'required': ['url']}, {'properties': {'path': {}}, 'required': ['path']}
/analyses/iris | …#/$defs/AnalysisDeclaration__identifier_optional/type | WRONG_TYPE | '../iris' is not of type 'object'
/findings/f/evidence/0 | …#/$defs/Evidence/oneOf | INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE | 'r' is valid under each of …
/findings/f/evidence/0 | …#/$defs/Evidence/type | WRONG_TYPE | 'r' is not of type 'object'
/outputs/r | …#/$defs/Output__identifier_optional/type | WRONG_TYPE        <- control, `r: metric`
```

Ajv 8.20 with `allErrors` gives the same pairs (`oneOf` params `{"passingSchemas":[0,1]}`). The repository's own `test_invalid_fixture`, run on fixtures kept outside the repository:

```
Analysis-901-declaration-as-path-WRONG_TYPE.yaml:                  FAIL at test_fixtures.py:73: assert codes == {rule}
Analysis-901-declaration-as-path-INVALID_DECLARATION_SOURCE.yaml:  FAIL at test_fixtures.py:73
Analysis-902-evidence-as-reference-WRONG_TYPE.yaml:                FAIL at test_fixtures.py:73
Analysis-902-evidence-as-reference-INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE.yaml:   FAIL at test_fixtures.py:73
Analysis-903-output-as-string-WRONG_TYPE.yaml:                     PASS
```

**Why it matters.** Authors who write `iris: ../iris` or leave `iris:` empty are told the declaration has both a path and a url. A tool that shows the first error in `iter_errors` or Ajv order shows the source code, not WRONG_TYPE. (jsonschema's `best_match` picks `type`.) No fixture can pin either code for these values, so the suite can't test the commonest shape slips.

**Suggested fix.** Have the generator guard each exactly-one-of: `allOf: [{if: {type: object}, then: {oneOf: …}}]`. It must sit in an `allOf`, because both defs already use `if`/`then`, and Ajv's `allOf/0/if` error then needs a code. `type: object` inside each branch does not work: the `oneOf` fails as "not valid under any". Then add WRONG_TYPE fixtures for a string declaration and a string evidence item. Failing that, widen the design §12 item to this case.

### F080. A location's `value` is defined only for a PDF, but `location` is allowed on evidence citing an output of any format

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Standards · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:47-58`, `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:91-95`, `docs/specification.md:508-517`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:274`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:277`, `rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:75` (tidy-up draft)

**What is wrong.** The specification and the schema define a FragmentSelector's `value` only as a PDF fragment identifier (RFC 3778). `location` is allowed on every evidence item, though, including an `artifact` that cites an output of any `format` (`png`, `csv`, `parquet`). The specification says nothing about what a location means on such evidence, or that it shouldn't be written. Both validators accept any string. So `row=5-7` on a CSV output is valid, and has no meaning under the specification. So is `page=6` on the same CSV. The schema's PDF wording comes from `main`. The specification's normative "is a PDF fragment identifier" is new in v0.1: on `main` the specification gave a PDF page only as an example. The tidy-up draft's wording is conditional ("for a PDF, …"), and can be read either way.

**Evidence.**

`src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:48-58`: "A place in a PDF, given as a PDF fragment identifier (RFC 3778), such as `page=6`." and `value`: "The PDF fragment identifier (RFC 3778), without the leading `#`, such as `page=6`." `location` (`:91-95`): "The place in the cited work the claim rests on, such as `page=6`."

`docs/specification.md:514-517`: "`location`, a FragmentSelector (`oa:FragmentSelector`), whose `value` is a PDF fragment identifier ([RFC 3778]) without the `#`, such as `page=6`."

Tidy-up draft, change 4 (`NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:75`): "A location is given by its `value`, the form of a W3C FragmentSelector: for a PDF, an RFC 3778 fragment such as `page=6`."

```yaml
# csv-row.yaml (csv-page.yaml is the same with value "page=6")
spec_version: "0.1.0"
outputs:
  table: {type: table, format: csv}
findings:
  row_claim:
    claim: The fifth row holds the best score.
    evidence:
      - artifact: table
        location: {value: "row=5-7"}
```
```yaml
# png-xywh.yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
outputs:
  figure: {type: figure, format: png}
findings:
  peak:
    claim: The curve peaks at epoch 12.
    evidence:
      - artifact: figure
        location: {value: "xywh=160,120,320,240"}
```
```
$ validate.py Analysis csv-row.yaml csv-page.yaml png-xywh.yaml    → VALID / VALID / VALID
$ validate.mjs Analysis csv-row.yaml csv-page.yaml png-xywh.yaml   → VALID / VALID / VALID
```

`$defs/FragmentSelector` in `analysis.schema.json` types `value` as a string with no pattern. No rule in §9 reads the syntax of `value`.

The class maps to `oa:FragmentSelector`. Web Annotation Vocabulary §2.1.7 defines it by the media type: "used to record the segment of a representation using the IRI fragment specification defined by the representation's media type". The Data Model's table (§4.2.1) lists PDF as RFC 3778, CSV as RFC 7111 (`row=5-7`) and media as Media Fragments (`xywh=…`). The design omits `conformsTo` on purpose (design:277: "the description fixes the syntax"). Web Annotation gives `conformsTo` only as a SHOULD, so that omission is a recorded choice, not a defect. Its reason holds only under the PDF-only reading.

**Why it matters.** An author who wants to point at a row of a cited CSV output, or at a region of a figure, has no way to do it that conforms. A reader can't tell whether `row=5-7` is an RFC 7111 fragment, as Web Annotation reads it, or a malformed PDF fragment. One tool that opens cited passages ignores or rejects it, and another reads it by media type. No validity verdict changes.

**Suggested fix.** Either say, in the specification and in `location`'s description, that a location is given only for a cited PDF, or define `value` as a fragment identifier of the cited work's media type (RFC 3778 for a PDF, RFC 7111 for a CSV, Media Fragments for an image) and word tidy-up change 4 to match.

### F081. Tidy-up change 10 says the `excluded` reason can't be empty; `BLANK_EXCLUDED`, which cites it, requires it not be blank

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:445-450`, `docs/specification.md:1108`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:280-286`, `../astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:106`

**What is wrong.** Tidy-up change 10 asks only that the `excluded` reason not be empty. The specification, the LinkML description and the pattern `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]` go further and require a visible character. So the contract rejects a reason made only of spaces, tabs or newlines (`excluded: " "`). The catalogue row for `BLANK_EXCLUDED` cites `tidy-up` as its source (spec:1049: "`tidy-up` is the draft schema tidy-up RFC"), but that RFC says "empty". Design §11 (:677-713) doesn't list the stronger rule as a departure. The intent is recorded only in the design: §4 (:228) says "not blank (contains a non-space character)", and fixture `Analysis-129-blank-excluded.yaml` tests `excluded: "   "` (:12). The contract is stricter than the RFC but doesn't contradict it, since it rejects `""` too. The defect is in the wording of the draft RFC.

This is separate from the known §12 item (:751-752: "`excluded` counts a reason made only of non-ASCII whitespace as not blank"). That item says the pattern is too weak. This finding says the RFC's wording is weaker than the pattern.

**Evidence.** RFC change 10, `NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:106`:

> An option is excluded exactly when it has one and the reason can't be empty, which the schema checks with a pattern.

The RFC says nothing else about blank, whitespace or visible characters in the reason. Specification and schema:

- spec:448-449: "The reason contains a visible character, one outside U+0000–U+0020 and U+007F ([`BLANK_EXCLUDED`](#parse-and-schema))"
- spec:1108: "| `BLANK_EXCLUDED` | schema | MUST | An option's `excluded` reason is not blank | tidy-up |"
- analysis.yaml:281-285: `pattern: '[^\x00-\x20\x7f]'`, `rule: BLANK_EXCLUDED`, "Why the option is ruled out, as a reason that is not blank."

Minimal document. The variants differ only in the value of `excluded`:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
outputs:
  result:
    type: metric
    format: json
decisions:
  d:
    options:
      a: {}
      b:
        excluded: " "
```

```
excluded: "Ruled out by pilot."  VALID (Python and Ajv)
excluded: ""     /decisions/d/options/b/excluded | ...#/$defs/Option__identifier_optional/properties/excluded/pattern | BLANK_EXCLUDED
excluded: " "    /decisions/d/options/b/excluded | ...#/$defs/Option__identifier_optional/properties/excluded/pattern | BLANK_EXCLUDED
excluded: "\t", "\n"   the same location and code
```

Ajv reports the same location and code for each variant.

**Why it matters.** Someone who implements change 10 as written (`minLength: 1`) accepts `" "`, which the contract rejects. Migration hits the same gap. The RFC's step at :212 replaces `excluded_reason: <reason>` with `excluded: <reason>`, so a `main` file with a whitespace-only `excluded_reason` migrates to a document the contract rejects, and the RFC doesn't warn about it. Once the RFC is accepted, it describes a weaker rule than the catalogue row that cites it.

**Suggested fix.** Reword change 10 at :106: "the reason can't be blank: it holds a character outside U+0000–U+0020 and U+007F, which the schema checks with a pattern". The known §12 non-ASCII whitespace note can be settled in the same sentence.

### F082. §9's Source cells leave out sources (tidy-up, `main`, 0004) and write them in two forms

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Internal consistency · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:1038-1039`, `docs/specification.md:1049-1050`, `docs/specification.md:1099`, `docs/specification.md:1101`, `docs/specification.md:1102`, `docs/specification.md:1103`, `docs/specification.md:1106`, `docs/specification.md:1107`, `docs/specification.md:1108`, `docs/specification.md:1109`, `docs/specification.md:1110`, `docs/specification.md:1111`, `docs/specification.md:1112`, `docs/specification.md:1120`, `docs/specification.md:1121`, `docs/specification.md:1123`, `docs/specification.md:1139`, `docs/specification.md:1180`, `docs/specification.md:1252-1257`, `docs/specification.md:1288`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:351`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:353`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:356`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:357`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:362`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:370`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:660-675`, `rfcs/0003-cross-universe-artifact-references.md:230`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:404`, `rfcs/0004-composition.md:406`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:304`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:318`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:339`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:346`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:381`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:402`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:409`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:535`

**What is wrong.** Several Source cells in §9 cite only some of the places a rule comes from. The convention at spec:1049-1050 defines the `tidy-up` and `main` tokens, and other rows use them, but these rows leave them out. The spec copied the cells from the design's §7 catalogue, and `rules.json` copies them word for word, so all three carry the gaps. The cells also use two markups: `main` is always a code span and every other token is plain text.

| Row | Source now | Left out |
|---|---|---|
| `INVALID_COMMIT` (spec:1106) | `0007` | tidy-up change 25: lowercase |
| `INVALID_PATH` (spec:1107) | `0004, 0006` | tidy-up change 26: `/` separator, no `~`, no backslash, no control character, no trailing `/` |
| `INVALID_VALUE` (spec:1112) | `0005, 0007, #73` | tidy-up changes 24 (url form) and 27 (time ranges); `main` (`OutputType`, `Evidence.doi`, `Evidence.version` ≥ 1) |
| `MISSING_FIELD` (spec:1101) | `0004, 0005, 0006, tidy-up` | `main`: `Output.type`, `Decision.options`, `Insight.claim`, `Insight.evidence`, `TextQuoteSelector.exact` |
| `UNKNOWN_OPTION` (spec:1120) | `0003` | 0004: options in conditions, option references and flat keys |
| `INVALID_ID`, `DUPLICATE_ENTRY`, `AMBIGUOUS_ID` (`§4`), `WRONG_TYPE` (`§4, §10`), `UNKNOWN_FIELD` (`§10`), `INVALID_RECORD` (`0005 §2, §8`) | a spec section | tidy-up changes 16, 17, 21-23 (and `main` for `INVALID_ID`); allowed by spec:1038-1039, but inconsistent with rows that cite `tidy-up` |
| `EMPTY_COLLECTION`, `INVALID_INSIGHT_REF`, `EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED` (spec:1103, 1121, 1139) | `` `main` `` as a code span | (markup only) |

The rows that do cite the tidy-up are `MISSING_FIELD`, `BLANK_EXCLUDED`, `INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE` and `EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED`. The likely cause: in design §7 these rows cited design decisions (D1, D2, D3, D5, D10), the spec turned those into §n references, and the decisions later became tidy-up changes 12-28 (design:713) without the Source column being updated.

**Evidence.**

The definition, spec:1038-1039: "**Source:** the RFC section, or the section of this specification that explains the rule." The tokens, spec:1049-1050: "`tidy-up` is the draft schema tidy-up RFC, `main` a rule of the specification before version 0.1".

The rows:

```
1101 | `MISSING_FIELD` | ... | 0004, 0005, 0006, tidy-up |
1103 | `EMPTY_COLLECTION` | ... | 0004, `main` |
1106 | `INVALID_COMMIT` | schema | MUST | Every `commit` is a full commit id: 40 or 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits | 0007 |
1107 | `INVALID_PATH` | schema | MUST | A declaration's `path` is relative (no leading `/` or `~`), `/`-separated, with no backslash and no ASCII control character. An input's `path` is the same, and also has no `..` segment and doesn't end in `/` | 0004, 0006 |
1112 | `INVALID_VALUE` | ... | 0005, 0007, #73 |
1119 | `UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS` | MUST | Every address resolves, ... a decision in conditions and option references, ... | 0004 |
1120 | `UNKNOWN_OPTION` | MUST | Every option named by a condition, option reference or selection is one of its decision's options | 0003 |
1121 | `INVALID_INSIGHT_REF` | ... | `main` |
1139 | `EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED` | ... | `main`, tidy-up |
```

`rules.json` has the same values (`"source": "0007"` at 339, `"0004, 0006"` at 346, `"0005, 0007, #73"` at 381, `"0003"` at 402, `` "0004, `main`" `` at 318), and the §10 example at spec:1288 shows `"source": "0007"`.

What the cited RFCs say, and where the rest comes from:

- RFC 0007:66: "only a full commit id is accepted (40 hexadecimal digits, or 64 in a SHA-256 repository)". No accepted RFC gives a case. Tidy-up change 25 (`NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:185-187`): "A `commit`, in a declaration and in a record, is 40 or 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits, as git writes it." Design:704: "Commit ids are lowercase hexadecimal (RFC 0007 doesn't say which case)."
- RFC 0004:63 and RFC 0006:83 say only that a path is relative, and that an input's path has no `..`. Tidy-up change 26 (tidy-up:191) adds the rest. Design:705-707: "(RFC 0004 and RFC 0006 say only that they are relative)."
- RFC 0007:58 gives no url form; tidy-up:183 (change 24) does. RFC 0005:126 gives no time ranges; tidy-up:195 (change 27) adds "month 01-12, day 01-31, hour 00-23, minute and second 00-59". `grep -n -i -E "doi|OutputType|cpus|gpus" rfcs/000[3-7]*.md` finds only RFC 0003's bibliography DOIs; the constraints are on `main` (`analysis.yaml:86-98`, `insight.yaml:72`, `insight.yaml:75-77`). Design:672 itself credits "`Output.format`'s token pattern (#73, `main`)".
- On `main`, `Output.type`, `Decision.options`, `exact`, `claim` and `evidence` are required (`git show main:src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml` 418-428, 522-534; `insight.yaml` 35-36, 107-108, 118). No in-scope RFC field table requires them, and design §10's table (design:667-675) has no `MISSING_FIELD` row.
- RFC 0003:230 covers only selections: "An effective selection names only declared decisions and their options". RFC 0004:404: "a decision and one of its options in `when`, `requires` and `incompatible_with`"; 0004:406: "A flat key names a declaration path, one of the decisions that analysis defines and one of its options".

The contract enforces the uncited constraints under these codes, and jsonschema and Ajv agree. Each document is a minimal valid one with one change:

```
/analyses/up/commit                   | .../AnalysisDeclaration__identifier_optional/properties/commit/pattern | INVALID_COMMIT   ("D1570F85...")
/inputs/data/path                     | .../Input__identifier_optional/properties/path/pattern                 | INVALID_PATH     ('data\x.csv', 'data/')
/analyses/other/path                  | .../AnalysisDeclaration__identifier_optional/properties/path/pattern   | INVALID_PATH     ('~/other', 'other\r')
/analyses/upstream/url                | .../AnalysisDeclaration__identifier_optional/properties/url/pattern    | INVALID_VALUE    (../repo)
/generated_by/started                 | .../Generation/properties/started/pattern                              | INVALID_VALUE    (2026-13-45T25:61:00.000Z)
/outputs/result/type                  | .../OutputType/enum                                                    | INVALID_VALUE    (plot)
/prior_insights/p/evidence/0/doi      | .../Evidence/properties/doi/pattern                                    | INVALID_VALUE    (arXiv:1706.03762)
/prior_insights/p/evidence/0/version  | .../Evidence/properties/version/minimum                                | INVALID_VALUE    (0)
```

On markup: `` `main` `` is the only one of the 19 distinct source tokens in `rules.json` with backticks. The convention paragraph puts every token it quotes in a code span, including `0005 §1`, which the cells write plain, so it sets no markup. §10 calls only `statement` "inline Markdown" and gives `source` no form (spec:1253-1256). The design has the same markup (design:353, 371, 389).

**Why it matters.** Someone who follows `source` in `rules.json`, or a §9 row, to the cited RFC doesn't find the rule: RFC 0007 allows `D1570F...`, RFCs 0004 and 0006 say only "relative", and no RFC requires `Insight.claim` or covers options in conditions. Anyone listing which rules depend on the draft tidy-up, where "any one can be dropped without touching the rest" (tidy-up RFC:57), misses changes 16, 17 and 21-27. A consumer that splits `source` on ", " has to special-case `` `main` ``. No document validates differently.

**Suggested fix.** Say in §9 that Source names every source of a rule, as plain tokens, and apply it: ``0007, tidy-up``; `0004, 0006, tidy-up`; `0005, 0007, tidy-up, main, #73`; `0004, 0005, 0006, tidy-up, main`; `0003, 0004`; `main` unquoted in every cell; `tidy-up` added beside the §n rows. Make the same change in the design's §7 (and a `MISSING_FIELD` row in its §10), update spec:1288, and regenerate `rules.json`.

### F083. The design's §10 and §11 number the tidy-up changes against an older draft

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:535`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:644-658`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:700-703`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:713`

**What is wrong.** §10's tidy-up rows 8, 9 and 10 are the draft's changes 9 (tags), 10 (`excluded`) and 11 (`doi`/`artifact`). The draft's change 8, `Analysis.version` → `spec_version`, appears only as "Pending". §10 has no rows of its own for changes 12–28. Changes 12, 13, 14, 18, 19, 20, 22, 24 (the url form) and 27 have no row at all. Others (16, 17, 21, 23, and part of 25 and 26) are traced only through rows for other statements. §11 says its seventeen items are "changes 4 and 12–28", but item 13 includes change 8. The row count at line 535 is stale: it was right at a3996e6, and 659bbeb then added rows 674–675 without updating it. Those two rows land. §11's item 12 also says "anything local is a `path`", but the draft, the design's own §4, the spec and the contract all declare another repository on this machine by a `file://` URL.

All of this is in the design only. The spec, the LinkML descriptions and the contract implement changes 4, 8 and 12–28.

**Evidence.**

```
design:655  | 8. `tags` on Input, Output, Option, Universe; not inherited | §5 |
design:657  | 10. Exactly one of `doi` and `artifact` | `INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE` |
design:658  | Pending: `Analysis.version` → `spec_version` | Analysis |
design:702  13. Already pending in the draft: the FragmentSelector swap and
design:703      `Analysis.version` → `spec_version`.
design:713  All seventeen are in the tidy-up RFC draft as its changes 4 and 12–28.
RFC:96      #### 8. `Analysis.version` becomes `spec_version`
RFC:100     #### 9. `tags` on inputs, outputs, options and universes
RFC:104     #### 10. `excluded` holds the reason
RFC:116     #### 11. Evidence has exactly one of `doi` and `artifact`
```

(RFC is `astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md`.) Over design lines 529–676, each of `Analysis.id`, `Evidence.id`, `notes`, `null`, `lowercase`, `backslash`, `xsd`, `leap` and `Evidence.version` occurs 0 times.

```
design:535  Checked on 2026-11-03: 100 rows, all landed. No corrections were needed.
$ awk '<data rows of §10>' design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md | wc -l
102
```

On item 12: design:700-701 says "anything local is a `path`". RFC:183 says "another repository on the same machine by a `file://` URL". design:237-238, specification.md:650-652 and analysis.yaml:383-385 say the same as the RFC. `tests/data/valid/Analysis-005-declarations.yaml` has `url: "file:///srv/repo"` and is valid.

**Why it matters.** §1 makes §10 the evidence that the schema implements every statement of the in-scope RFCs, and the tidy-up draft is in scope. Someone who checks §10 row by row against the draft, or who settles the RFC's final numbering, maps tags, `excluded` and `doi`/`artifact` to the wrong changes. They also can't use §10 to show that changes 12–28 landed. Item 12 suggests that `file://` URLs are not allowed.

**Suggested fix.** Renumber §10's tidy-up rows to the draft's 1–11, with "Pending" becoming change 8. Add rows for 12–28, or point to §11 and give it a "Lands in" column. Change line 713 to "changes 4, 8 and 12–28", and update or date the row count. Reword item 12: "an analysis in the root's repository is a `path`, another repository on this machine a `file://` URL".

### F084. Tidy-up change 16 says map keys follow the id grammar, and never says that `Universe.decisions` keys are addresses

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:151`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:196`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:689-690`

**What is wrong.** Change 16 of the draft tidy-up RFC says a map's keys follow the id grammar. It then makes an exception for a universe's `decisions`, but the exception explains how the entry is written. It does not say what the key is. The only example key, `model`, is a plain id. RFC 0004 requires flat keys there, `<declaration>.<decision>`, and the RFC never states that grammar. One reading exempts the map from both rules. The other applies the id grammar to its keys, which would reject `stage.method: svm`. The design repeats the wording in §11 item 6. Its §4 type table also lists `id` for "every map key", and the next row of that table gives the keys of `Universe.decisions` to `address`. The specification and the contract are right. Only the draft RFC and the design are loose.

**Evidence.** Draft RFC, change 16 (`NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:151`):

> A map's keys follow the id grammar (change 17) and an entry is written one way, as a map without its id (`seed_42: {}`). The exception is a universe's `decisions`, whose entries pair a decision with an option and are written `model: svm`. The schema checks both.

Design §11 item 6 (`:689-690`): "Map keys follow the id grammar; map entries take one form: objects, except the compact `Universe.decisions` (D3)." Design §4 (`:196`): `id` is used by "every map key, …". At `:197`, `address` is used by "the keys of `Universe.decisions`".

RFC 0004 (`rfcs/0004-composition.md:137`): "the decisions of subanalyses are set using flat keys, `<id>.<decision>`".

The specification states the exception: `docs/specification.md:225` gives `id` to "every map key except those of `Universe.decisions`", and `:1099` (`INVALID_ID`) reads "an `address` in `Universe.decisions`". The generated `propertyNames` for `Universe.decisions` is the address pattern (`analysis.schema.json:491-493`). Both validators (the `jsonschema` toolkit and Ajv) agree:

```
flat_key.yaml           (universes: {u: {decisions: {stage.method: svm}}})   VALID
deep_flat_key.yaml      (stage.inner.d: x)                                   VALID
output_key_dotted.yaml  (outputs: {stage.result: ...})       INVALID_ID at /outputs
selection_key_bad.yaml  (decisions: {Stage.method: svm})     INVALID_ID at /universes/u/decisions
```

The committed fixture `tests/data/valid/Analysis-004-universes.yaml:30` (`stage.method: pca`) is valid too.

**Why it matters.** The draft RFC and the design are both sources of truth for v0.1. A reader of either one gets no grammar for the keys of a driven universe. Under the id-grammar reading, the driven universes that RFC 0004 builds on would be rejected. The specification settles it, so the contract is unaffected. Still, the RFC record should not need RFC 0004 to fill the gap.

**Suggested fix.** In change 16, write "A map's keys follow the id grammar, except the keys of a universe's `decisions`, which are decision addresses (RFC-0004's flat keys)". Make the same change in design §11 item 6, and add "except those of `Universe.decisions`" to the `id` row at design `:196`.

### F085. Tidy-up change 14 says every element's free prose is `description`, but a decision's is `rationale`

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:37`, `astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:130`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-changes.md:63`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:231-232`

**What is wrong.** The draft tidy-up RFC's Context item 14 says every element with free prose calls it `description`, except an insight. Change 14 repeats this: "as everywhere else". A decision is a second exception. Its prose is `rationale`, and it has no `description`, both on main and in v0.1. The RFC never mentions `rationale`. The change itself (an insight's `notes` becomes `description`) is implemented exactly. Only the RFC's wording is wrong, and the schema is right as it stands.

**Evidence.** The RFC:

- NNNN:37: "14. **Free prose under two names.** Every element with free prose calls it `description` except an insight, which calls it `notes`."
- NNNN:130: "An insight's free prose is its `description`, as everywhere else."
- `grep -c rationale` on the RFC gives `0`.

In v0.1, Decision (`analysis.yaml:220-250`) has `id`, `label`, `rationale`, `tags`, `when` and `options`:

```
231:      rationale:
232:        description: Why the analysis makes this decision, in free prose.
```

On main, Decision also has `rationale` and no `description` (`git show main:src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml`, line 492). A decision written with `description`:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
outputs:
  result:
    type: metric
    format: json
decisions:
  d:
    description: Which estimator to use.
    options:
      a: {}
```

```
$ uv run python toolkit/validate.py Analysis decision_description.yaml
/decisions/d | https://w3id.org/astra/analysis.schema.json#/$defs/Decision__identifier_optional/additionalProperties | UNKNOWN_FIELD
```

Ajv reports the same location and code. The same document with `rationale:` is valid in both.

Other sources agree with the schema. The accepted RFC-0002 (`rfcs/0002-decouple-reports.md:80-81`) says "`Decision` and `Insight` use the semantically-specific `rationale` and `claim`/`notes`". The design keeps "**Decision** | `label`, `rationale`, `tags`, `when`, `options`" (`design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:257`). The spec is accurate: "Most elements also take ... a `description` in free prose" (`docs/specification.md:337-339`) and "the `rationale` for making it" (`:430`). The companion repeats the RFC's claim: "one name for free prose" (`design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-changes.md:63`).

**Why it matters.** It bites readers of the RFC. They are told to expect `description` on every element. RFC-0002 says otherwise, and that RFC is not among the five the brief lists. v0.1's own wording, `rationale` "in free prose", makes the sentence look false too. RFC-0002's reading is that `rationale` is prose with a specific purpose, not the element's general prose. On that reading the sentence can be defended, but the RFC never says so. The harm is limited, because authors follow the spec, and the spec is accurate.

**Suggested fix.** Reword NNNN:37, NNNN:130 and changes.md:63 to mean an element's general prose, for example "... except an insight (`notes`); a decision's prose is its `rationale`". Don't add `Decision.description`: no source asks for it, and RFC-0002 keeps `rationale` on purpose.

### F086. `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS` doesn't rule out record `inputs` items for entries the output doesn't list

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:1182`, `docs/specification.md:1170`, `docs/specification.md:904-909`, `docs/specification.md:306-307`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:83-86`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:122-123`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:120`

**What is wrong.** The §9 row requires one item for each entry the output lists. It doesn't say anything about an item whose `entry` the output doesn't list. The field definitions do say this: a recorded input's `entry` "is an entry of `Output.inputs` exactly as written". So the definitions rule out extra items, but the catalogue row, which should state the same constraint, is weaker. The code's name points at missing items too. A record that claims its recipe read `ghost` passes the rule as worded, and so does a record with items for an output that lists no inputs. The design repeats the row as it stands (`design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:294`, `:432`), and `rules.json` copies it.

**Evidence.** The §9 row and the status it feeds:

> `docs/specification.md:1182`: | `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS` | MUST | Every record has exactly one `inputs` item for each entry its output lists, matched by `entry` (their order carries no meaning) | `missing` | 0005 §2 |

> `docs/specification.md:1170`: | `missing` | The result has no valid record where its address says: `MISSING_RECORD`, `INVALID_RECORD`, `MISPLACED_RECORD` or `MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS` doesn't hold |

The definitions that rule extras out:

> `docs/specification.md:306-307`: a recorded input's `entry` is an entry of `Output.inputs` exactly as written (§8).

> `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:122-123` (`RecordedInput.entry`): The entry exactly as the output's `inputs` lists it in the `astra.yaml`, such as `root.data`.

The source, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:120`: "for each input the output lists, its `entry` as listed in the `astra.yaml`".

Repro: the analysis of spec §2 (`accuracy` has `inputs: [iris]`) and a record of `accuracy#forest` with an extra item:

```json
"inputs": [
  {"entry": "iris",  "content_hash": "sha256:549eeec2…757e", "size": 10},
  {"entry": "ghost", "content_hash": "sha256:549eeec2…757e", "size": 10}]
```

```
$ uv run python toolkit/validate.py ProvenanceRecord accuracy-forest-extra.json   -> VALID
$ node toolkit/js/validate.mjs <repo> ProvenanceRecord accuracy-forest-extra.json -> VALID
```

Accepting it at the schema layer is correct, because `inputs` is a check rule. At the check layer, `iris` has exactly one item, so the rule as worded holds and the result isn't `missing` by line 1170.

**Why it matters.** Line 22 says "Section 9 states every rule that a tool checks or keeps". A checker author who works from §9 or from `rules.json` checks "each listed entry has one item" and gives this record `unchanged` or `stale`. A checker author who follows the definition at line 306 gives it `missing`, under a code whose wording doesn't cover the case. The same record then gets different statuses from different tools. The case needs a runner that broke `UNDECLARED_READ` or a tampered record.

**Suggested fix.** Reword the row and keep its code: "Every record has exactly one `inputs` item for each entry its output lists, matched by `entry`, and no other item (their order carries no meaning)". Mirror it in the design's copies, the §8 bullet and `Generation.inputs` (`provenance.yaml:84`), then regenerate `rules.json`.

### F087. `Generation.commit` ("the commit the recipe ran from") doesn't say that a driven analysis's record names the driver's commit

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:67-69`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:74-77`, `src/astra/schema/generated/provenance.schema.json:22,25`, `src/astra/schema/generated/provenance.open.schema.json:22,25`, `docs/specification.md:901-903`, `docs/specification.md:829-839`

**What is wrong.** When a driver drives an analysis from a commit, the recipes run on the driven analysis's files at the declared commit C, which may be in another repository. §7 says the records name the driver's commit D. The field's normative definition says only "the commit the recipe ran from". It covers the submodule case and stops there. Read next to §7's "run on exactly its files at the declared commit", it points to C. The specification does answer the question, but in §7's prose, and the field never links to it. This is a wording problem. The contract doesn't change: the field's schema is just the `commit_id` pattern.

**Evidence.** The field definition, which ships verbatim as the description in both provenance JSON Schemas (`:25`; the class description at `:22` has the same gap):

> `provenance.yaml:74-77` The full id of the git commit the recipe ran from. It pins every file of the code and every `astra.yaml`, an analysis in a submodule through the commit the submodule is pinned at.

§8 restates it word for word at `docs/specification.md:901-903`. The class description at `provenance.yaml:67-69` says "the commit it ran from".

§7, Driving:

> `docs/specification.md:830-831` Its recipes then run on exactly its files at the declared commit ([`NOT_AT_COMMIT`](#runner)).
>
> `docs/specification.md:836-838` What the driver's universes build belongs to the driver, and its records name the driver's commit, where the declaration says which commit the driven analysis is from

The source is RFC 0007:179: "Those records name the driver's commit, as in *Result provenance and layout*". The field wording comes from RFC 0005:119, which was written before an analysis could be driven from a commit.

The specification gives two reasons C is wrong. First, "The commit a record names stays in the history of every commit that holds the record" (`docs/specification.md:977-978`), and a C from a `url` declaration is not in the driver's history. Second, `STALE_RESULT` compares "since the commit in a result's record" (`:1183`). A record naming C would be compared from the other repository's commit. For a same-repository `path`+`commit` declaration, it would be compared from an older commit that lacks the driver's universes and declaration.

No catalogue rule and no row of the design's RFC 0007 traceability table covers RFC 0007:179.

**Why it matters.** A runner author working from the field reference, which is how astra-tools would be built, can write C into the driver's records. A checker would then run `STALE_RESULT` against the wrong commit, or against a commit that isn't in the repository at all.

**Suggested fix.** Extend the LinkML description, and §8's bullet to match: "... an analysis in a submodule through the commit the submodule is pinned at, and an analysis from a commit, driven or read, through its declaration's `commit`." Optionally link §8's bullet to §7's Driving paragraph.

### F088. `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` compares with "the commit the declaration is in", which doesn't exist for an uncommitted declaration

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Implementer, Author · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:762-763`, `docs/specification.md:1127`, `src/astra/schema/generated/rules.json:452`, `docs/specification.md:81`, `docs/specification.md:89`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:78`, `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:196`

**What is wrong.** The rule checks a `path` + `commit` declaration against "the commit the declaration is in". A validator usually runs on the working tree, often in a pre-commit hook, before the declaration is committed. At that point no such commit exists. The spec doesn't say whether to compare with HEAD, to skip the check, or something else. The spec repeats RFC 0007's wording word for word, so this is not a departure from the RFC. The ambiguity is in the RFC already and the spec carries it over. The design (§12, §13) and the tidy-up RFC don't mention it.

The gap also covers any `astra.yaml` of the stack that is declared by `path` without a `commit`, because those files are read from the working tree too. An analysis read at a declared commit, or from a remote, is unambiguous. So is a clean checkout, where the commit is HEAD.

**Evidence.**

- `docs/specification.md:762-763`: "which should be an ancestor of the commit the declaration is in ([`COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR`](#validator))".
- `docs/specification.md:1127`: "| `COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR` | SHOULD | A declaration with a `path` and a `commit` names an ancestor of the commit the declaration is in | 0007 |". `rules.json` has the same statement.
- `rfcs/0007-analyses-in-other-repositories.md:78`: "The commit SHOULD be an ancestor of the commit the declaration is in."
- On what a validator reads, the spec says only that it "reads an analysis, every `astra.yaml` of its stack and git" (`:89`; also `:81`). It names no commit for the working tree. The spec's only rule about the working tree binds the runner, not the validator (`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`, `:965`).

In a scratch repository, `C1` on `main` holds `astra.yaml` and `sub/astra.yaml`, and branch `side` adds commit `S`. On `main`, the root is edited but not committed:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
analyses:
  at_head: {path: sub, commit: "2d34b13a2657f278b964b7a2ef8007b6f1c0a416"}   # C1 = HEAD
  at_side: {path: sub, commit: "f75c0d783b9aab79d7f1d93b84e80178d87c8300"}   # S, on branch side
outputs:
  top: {type: metric, format: json, inputs: [at_head.result, at_side.result]}
```

```
$ git status --short
 M astra.yaml
$ git merge-base --is-ancestor $C1 HEAD; echo $?   -> 0
$ git merge-base --is-ancestor $S HEAD;  echo $?   -> 1
$ git cat-file -p HEAD:astra.yaml                  -> no `analyses:`
validate.py / validate.mjs: astra.yaml VALID, sub/astra.yaml VALID
```

If the validator compares with HEAD, `at_side` gets a warning. If it reads the declaration as being in no commit yet, the check doesn't apply and there is no warning.

Comparing with HEAD also gives a false warning during a merge. In a copy of the repository, run `git merge --no-commit --no-ff side`, keeping the uncommitted declaration of `S`:

```
is-ancestor S HEAD (during merge)                         -> 1
is-ancestor S HEAD (after the merge commit that holds it) -> 0
```

**Why it matters.** It affects validator authors, and the authors whose pre-commit hooks run those validators. Two conforming validators give different warnings on the same uncommitted file. Validity doesn't change, because the rule is a SHOULD (spec:89-90). Once the declaration is committed, both readings agree. That is why the severity is Minor.

**Suggested fix.** At spec:763 and in the §9 row, say "an ancestor of the commit the declaration is in or, for a declaration not yet committed, of the commit it would be committed on (HEAD, and MERGE_HEAD during a merge)". Note the change from RFC 0007's text in the tidy-up RFC. Another option is to say the check waits until the declaration is committed, or to say once in §2 which commit stands for the working tree.

### F089. The §1 Terms rows for `address` and `pin` contradict §4's grammar

- **Lens:** RFC fidelity, Standards · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:39`, `docs/specification.md:40`, `docs/specification.md:45`, `docs/specification.md:211-213`, `docs/specification.md:281-295`, `docs/specification.md:311`, `docs/specification.md:328-330`, `docs/specification.md:1018`

**What is wrong.** Two Terms rows say something §4 does not. Row 39 counts options as elements, and row 40 defines an address as "the name of an element". Together with §4's "The last segment is the id of an element of the analysis reached", that gives an option an address. In §4 it has none: an option is named by an option reference, its decision's address and then its id. Row 45 puts the pin "after a reference", but in the grammar the pin is part of the reference and follows its address. The schema and both validators follow §4, so the contract is correct. The defect is in the wording. The same looseness appears at line 1018, which pins an "address" where §4 pins a reference.

**Evidence.** From `docs/specification.md`:

- 39: "| element | an input, output, decision, option, universe, declaration or insight of an analysis | §4 |"
- 40: "| address | the name of an element, through the declarations that lead to it | §4 |"
- 45: "| pin | `#` and a universe id after a reference, which reads the output in that universe | §4 |"
- 212–213: `reference = address [ "#" id ]` and `option-reference = address "." id ; a decision's address, then an option id`
- 282–283: "(In an option reference or a condition, the address is everything before the last segment, which is the option.)"
