**Suggested fix.** Insert `(?![A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://)` before the scp-like alternative, so that anything with `scheme://` must take the first branch. Add an invalid fixture (`url: "https://"`, INVALID_VALUE, fails at `/analyses/up/url`). Checked with Python `re`, the fix rejects the four bare forms and still accepts `https://github.com/owner/analysis`, `file:///srv/repo`, `ssh://git@host/x.git` and `git@github.com:owner/repo`.

### F156. Non-printable characters: the harness rejects them inside quoted scalars, and yaml 2.9 accepts them everywhere

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `tests/conftest.py:126-132`, `tests/conftest.py:27-28`, `docs/specification.md:79`, `docs/specification.md:163-165`, `docs/specification.md:1097`

**What is wrong.** YAML 1.2.2 §5.1 leaves DEL, C1 except NEL, U+FFFE and U+FFFF out of the printable set, so they can't appear raw in a plain scalar or a comment. It also says that processors "must allow all non-C0 characters inside quoted scalars". Neither loader follows this:
- The harness rejects these characters everywhere, quoted scalars included. ruamel's reader checks the whole stream against `NON_PRINTABLE` before it scans anything, and the resulting `ReaderError` is a `YAMLError`, which `PARSE_ERRORS` maps to `INVALID_SYNTAX`.
- yaml 2.9.1 checks nothing, even with `strict: true`. It accepts these characters in plain scalars and comments, and accepts raw C0 such as U+0001, which YAML 1.2.2 forbids everywhere.

The specification settles which is right: `astra.yaml` "is YAML 1.2 ... read strictly" (spec:163-165). It also says the parse layer is enforced by "any strict YAML 1.2 loader" (spec:79), and neither common loader is strict on this point. The spec doesn't warn about that, and the harness docstring (conftest.py:127, "YAML 1.2, restricted to the core schema") claims a conformance it lacks.

**Evidence.** Each document is the minimal valid `Analysis` plus one line with one raw character. U+0092 is the bytes `c2 92`, the cp1252 apostrophe after a wrong decode.

```
file                                   harness (ruamel 0.19.1)               Ajv + yaml 2.9.1
description: "It<U+0092>s quoted"      PARSE-ERROR ReaderError #x0092        VALID
description: 'It<U+0092>s quoted'      PARSE-ERROR ReaderError #x0092        VALID
description: It<U+0092>s plain         PARSE-ERROR ReaderError #x0092        VALID
description: "It\x92s escaped"         VALID                                 VALID
label: "x<DEL>y"  / 'x<DEL>y' / plain  PARSE-ERROR ReaderError #x007f        VALID
label: "x<U+FFFE>y" / plain            PARSE-ERROR ReaderError #xfffe        VALID
label: "x<U+0001>y" / plain            PARSE-ERROR ReaderError #x0001        VALID
# It<U+0092>s a comment                PARSE-ERROR ReaderError #x0092        VALID
label: x<U+0085>y  (NEL)               VALID                                 VALID
inputs: {data: {path: "data<DEL>.csv"}} PARSE-ERROR (INVALID_SYNTAX)         INVALID_PATH at /inputs/data/path
```

`.venv/.../ruamel/yaml/reader.py:187-188`:

```
NON_PRINTABLE = RegExp(
    '[^\x09\x0A\x0D\x20-\x7E\x85' '\xA0-퟿' '-�' '\U00010000-\U0010FFFF' ']'
```

By YAML 1.2.2 ([2] `nb-json`, [107] `nb-double-char`, [118] `nb-single-char`), the quoted non-C0 files are well-formed, and for `path: "data<DEL>.csv"` the right code is `INVALID_PATH`. By [128] `ns-plain-safe-out` and [1] `c-printable`, the plain, comment and C0 files are ill-formed. No fixture, example or specification example holds such a character, so no test covers this.

**Why it matters.** A Python validator built on ruamel and a TypeScript one built on yaml give opposite verdicts, or different codes, on the same file. Mojibake in a `description` or a stray DEL in a pasted path is enough to trigger it. The JSON Schemas aren't involved, and such files are rare.

**Suggested fix.** Either note in §3 that quoted scalars may hold any non-C0 character, that these characters are invalid elsewhere, and that validators must not rely on a loader that deviates, then relax or document the harness. Or tighten ASTRA on purpose: forbid raw C0 (except TAB, LF, CR), DEL, C1 except NEL, U+FFFE and U+FFFF everywhere as `INVALID_SYNTAX`, with a text pre-scan and a parse fixture. Tom decides.

### F157. `excluded: no` is a reason under YAML 1.2, and nothing at `excluded` says so

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:445-450`, `docs/specification.md:170-171`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:280-286`, `src/astra/schema/generated/analysis.schema.json:278-282`, `astra-rfcs/tidy-up/astra-spec/rfcs/NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:212`

**What is wrong.** Tidy-up change 10 turns `excluded` from a boolean into a reason string. Under the YAML 1.2 core schema, `no`, `off`, `n`, `No`, `NO`, `Off` and `OFF` are strings. They pass BLANK_EXCLUDED's pattern `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]`. So `excluded: no` excludes the option, and its reason is "no". Only `false`/`False` get WRONG_TYPE. The spec states each half of this (§3 at 170-171, §5 at 445-449), and both consumer validators agree, so the contract is consistent. The gap is in the wording. §5 names only `excluded: true` as not a reason. The `excluded` description gives no warning. The RFC's migration step names only `excluded: false`. Before the tidy-up, `excluded: no` meant "not excluded". On `main` the field is `range: boolean` (`main:src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:453-455`), and `main`'s tests load YAML with PyYAML (`main:tests/test_data.py:10,67`), which reads `no` as `False`. An author who follows the migration step as written keeps `excluded: no`, and v0.1 then excludes the option. No schema error is raised.

**Evidence.** The minimal document:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
outputs:
  result:
    type: metric
    format: json
    decisions: [outliers]
decisions:
  outliers:
    options:
      keep: {}
      drop:
        excluded: no
```

```
$ uv run python $T/validate.py Analysis excluded-{no,off,n,No,NO,Off,OFF,false,False}.yaml
excluded-no.yaml     VALID   (same for off, n, No, NO, Off, OFF, and with a universe selecting drop)
excluded-false.yaml  /decisions/outliers/options/drop/excluded | WRONG_TYPE | False is not of type 'string'
$ node $T/js/validate.mjs $R Analysis <same files>
identical verdicts (Ajv: "must be string" for false/False)
```

A YAML 1.1 reading gives a different value. PyYAML `safe_load` gives `no`, `No`, `off`, `OFF` -> `False` (WRONG_TYPE against the contract), and keeps `n` as `'n'`. yaml@2.9.1 with `version: '1.1'` reads all of them, `n` included, as `false`.

`docs/specification.md:449-450`: "The reason contains a visible character, one outside U+0000–U+0020 and U+007F ([`BLANK_EXCLUDED`]); `excluded: true` is not a reason ([`WRONG_TYPE`])."

`NNNN-schema-tidy-up.md:212`: "replace `excluded: true` and `excluded_reason: <reason>` with `excluded: <reason>`. An option with `excluded: false` drops it".

Per §5 and the §9 row, a universe that selects `drop` then fails at the validator layer with EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED, an error that doesn't point at the cause. If no universe selects it, the option is excluded and no error is raised. This follows from the spec text. No validator-layer implementation was run.

**Why it matters.** It bites authors who migrate v0.0.x files, where `excluded: no` meant "not excluded", and authors with a YAML 1.1 habit. The meaning reverses with no error. A tool built on a 1.1 loader is non-conforming under R19, but it reports WRONG_TYPE where conforming tools accept the file. No file on `main` writes `excluded: no`/`off`, so the migration risk is theoretical for this repository.

**Suggested fix.** Add a clause after "`excluded: true` is not a reason" in §5 and in the `excluded` description, for example: "`excluded: no` or `off` is the reason 'no' or 'off', which excludes the option". Name `no`/`off` beside `false` in the RFC's migration step. Making the pattern refuse these words would go against R19 and is not proposed.

### F158. A lone CR is a YAML 1.2 line break, but the `yaml` npm package keeps it as content, so the validators disagree

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:79`, `docs/specification.md:163-165`, `docs/specification.md:1097`, `docs/specification.md:246-251`

**What is wrong.** The parse layer is "any strict YAML 1.2 loader" (spec:79), and `astra.yaml` "is YAML 1.2 ..., read strictly" (spec:163-164). YAML 1.2.2 §5.4 makes a CR on its own a line break: "[28] b-break ::= ( b-carriage-return b-line-feed ) | b-carriage-return | b-line-feed", and "Each such line break must be parsed into a single line feed character". A comment ends at a CR, because `nb-char` excludes `b-char`. The harness (ruamel) follows this. yaml 2.9.1, the JS loader the brief cross-checks with, does not. Its lexer counts a CR as a break only when an LF follows it (`node_modules/yaml/dist/parse/lexer.js:171-172`, `:696`), and its maintainer has closed the divergence as not planned (eemeli/yaml#595). The spec is correct. The gap is that it trusts "any" loader, and the most widely used JS loader breaks the rule. The spec says nothing about line endings. It already designs around Python/JS differences at spec:246-251 ("Python's `.` matches `\r`").

**Evidence.** Each `\r` below is a raw CR byte, written with `printf`. Ajv 8.20 is run with yaml 2.9.1.

```sh
printf 'spec_version: "0.1.0"\n# note\rbogus: 1\noutputs:\n  result:\n    type: metric\n    format: json\n' > cr-comment.yaml
printf '# note\rspec_version: "0.1.0"\noutputs:\n  result:\n    type: metric\n    format: json\n' > cr-comment-hides-required.yaml
printf 'spec_version: "0.1.0"\routputs:\r  result:\r    type: metric\r    format: json\r' > cr-only.yaml
```

```
                                harness (ruamel)         Ajv + yaml 2.9.1
cr-comment.yaml                 (root) UNKNOWN_FIELD     VALID
cr-comment-hides-required.yaml  VALID                    (root) MISSING_FIELD 'spec_version'
cr-only.yaml                    VALID                    PARSE-ERROR BLOCK_AS_IMPLICIT_KEY; UNEXPECTED_TOKEN; ...
cr-path-indented.yaml           VALID ('data file.csv')  /inputs/d/path INVALID_PATH ("data\r      file.csv")
crlf.yaml                       VALID                    VALID
```

In `cr-path-indented.yaml` the quoted path's continuation line is indented (`path: "data<CR>      file.csv"`). Single-quoted, plain and block scalars split the same way. CRLF files agree in both. The escape `"a\rb"` gives `'a\rb'` in both.

**Why it matters.** A file with classic-Mac line endings, or a stray CR pasted into a quoted string or after a comment, gets different verdicts from a Python and a TypeScript validator. In `cr-comment.yaml` the JS validator accepts a document that the spec makes invalid: yaml 2.9 swallows the `bogus` key into the comment. Such files are rare, so this is Minor.

**Suggested fix.** In §3, make a CR not followed by LF `INVALID_SYNTAX` (an ASTRA restriction like the `%YAML` one), and add a parse fixture for it (excluded from yamllint and check-yaml). Or add a §10 note that loaders built on the `yaml` package must normalise line endings first.

### F159. A `\U` escape beyond U+10FFFF raises ValueError, not YAMLError, so the harness can't hold it as an `INVALID_SYNTAX` fixture

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `tests/conftest.py:27-31`, `tests/conftest.py:135-147`, `tests/test_fixtures.py:52-55`, `docs/specification.md:1097`

**What is wrong.** `PARSE_ERRORS` maps `INVALID_SYNTAX` to ruamel's `YAMLError`, and its docstring says this covers "any loader error". It doesn't. ruamel.yaml 0.19.1 checks that a `\U` escape is hex but never checks its range. It calls `chr()` directly (`.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/ruamel/yaml/scanner.py:1423-1424`). So `"\U00110000"` raises `ValueError` and `"\UFFFFFFFF"` raises `OverflowError`. Neither is a `YAMLError`. YAML 1.2.2 §5.7 says "Each escape sequence must be parsed into the appropriate Unicode character". U+110000 is not a code point, so the file is ill-formed and breaks `INVALID_SYNTAX` (spec:1097). But `test_invalid_fixture` can't accept a parse fixture for it: `pytest.raises(PARSE_ERRORS[rule])` lets the `ValueError` through. yaml 2.9.1 (`parseDocument`, 1.2, core) rejects the same file with `BAD_DQ_ESCAPE`, so the two loaders agree the file is invalid. Only the harness can't classify the error.

**Evidence.** `load_yaml` on one-line files (Python 3.12.15, ruamel.yaml 0.19.1):

```
label: "\U00110000"  -> ValueError: chr() arg not in range(0x110000)      | YAMLError: False
label: "\UFFFFFFFF"  -> OverflowError: Python int too large to convert to C int | YAMLError: False
label: "\q"          -> ScannerError                                       | YAMLError: True   (control)
label: "\U0010FFFF"  -> loads
```

A fixture added in a scratch clone (its base, with `"\U0010FFFF"`, is valid):

```yaml
# rule: INVALID_SYNTAX
# fails at: parse
spec_version: "0.1.0"
label: "\U00110000"
outputs: {result: {type: metric, format: json}}
```

```
E   ValueError: chr() arg not in range(0x110000)
FAILED tests/test_fixtures.py::test_invalid_fixture[Analysis-990-escape-beyond-unicode]
FAILED tests/test_fixtures.py::test_invalid_fixture[Analysis-991-escape-overflow]
2 failed, 3 passed   (passed: Analysis-182, Analysis-183, and a "\q" fixture)
```

yaml 2.9.1 on the same files: `doc.errors -> YAMLParseError/BAD_DQ_ESCAPE: Invalid escape sequence \U00110000` (and `\UFFFFFFFF`).

Deep nesting is a separate matter, not this defect. ruamel raises `RecursionError` on a flow sequence 493 deep (492 loads). That document is well-formed YAML 1.2, so it doesn't break `INVALID_SYNTAX`. It hits a resource limit, and RFC 8259 §9 lets implementations limit depth.

**Why it matters.** The harness can't hold a fixture for a real `INVALID_SYNTAX` case, and its docstring says it can. A tool built the way the harness is built crashes on such a file instead of reporting a code. Nothing passes for the wrong reason: the failure is loud, and no realistic `astra.yaml` hits it.

**Suggested fix.** In `load_yaml`, catch `ValueError` and `OverflowError` and re-raise them as a ruamel `YAMLError`, or add them to `PARSE_ERRORS["INVALID_SYNTAX"]`. Correct the docstring at `conftest.py:31`, and add an Analysis parse fixture with `"\U00110000"`. Leave `RecursionError` unmapped. Treat it as a resource limit, not as `INVALID_SYNTAX`.

### F160. The record and result places §8 builds from ids and formats need not be valid file names: no length bound, Windows device names, a format ending in `.`

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:198-199`, `docs/specification.md:209`, `docs/specification.md:273`, `docs/specification.md:378-381`, `docs/specification.md:855`, `docs/specification.md:866-869`, `docs/specification.md:1179`, `docs/specification.md:1195`, `src/astra/schema/types.yaml:31`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:187`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:190-194`, `src/astra/schema/generated/analysis.schema.json:358`, `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:35-36`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:59`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:225`

**What is wrong.** §8 fixes each record's and each result's place from the analysis: `.astra/records/{universe}/{name}.json` and `results/{universe}/{name}.{format}` (spec:866-867). The universe id is a directory, the name (declaration ids and the output id joined by `.`) is a file name, and the format is its extension. "Both are worked out from the result's address" (spec:869), and `MISSING_RECORD` and `UNRECORDED_RESULT` check them there. Nothing requires these components to be file names a common file system or Git for Windows accepts. Three valid inputs give places that can't be created or checked out:

| Instance | What the schema accepts | What breaks |
|---|---|---|
| Length | ids (`[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`) and formats have no upper length; declaration chains have no depth limit | a component over NAME_MAX (255 bytes on ext4) can't be created |
| Windows device names | `con`, `prn`, `aux`, `nul`, `com1`-`com9`, `lpt1`-`lpt9`; §4 says "no id is reserved" | Windows reserves them, with or without an extension; Git for Windows refuses any such path segment |
| Trailing `.` in a format | `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.+-]*$` accepts `csv.` and `csv..` | the bytes' place ends in `.`; Windows strips it, Git for Windows refuses the path |

No rule reports any of these, and a conformant runner can't pick another place, since §8 fixes it. D5 dropped reserved words for address shadowing only (design:59) and says nothing about file names; the format pattern is unchanged from `main` (#73). None of this is a regression.

**Evidence.**

- No length bound anywhere: `grep -c maxLength src/astra/schema/generated/*.json` gives 0 for all four files, and no spec, RFC or design text mentions a length limit.
- Length: `validate.py` and `validate.mjs` both return `VALID` on each of these, and on ext4 (`PC_NAME_MAX = 255`) none of the paths can be created:

  ```
  built-once output, id of 251: longest component 256 bytes -> OSError errno=36 File name too long
  driven chain, four 64-char ids, universe u1: longest component 264 bytes -> OSError errno=36 File name too long
  bytes of output 'result', format of 300: longest component 307 bytes -> OSError errno=36 File name too long
  universe id of 256 (directory): longest component 256 bytes -> OSError errno=36 File name too long
  (boundary: output id of 250 -> 255-byte name created; 251 -> errno 36)
  ```

- Device names: both validators return `VALID` on

  ```yaml
  spec_version: "0.1.0"
  analyses:
    con: {path: aux}
  decisions:
    prior: {options: {pro: {}, con: {}}}
  universes:
    pro: {decisions: {prior: pro}}
    con: {decisions: {prior: con}}
  outputs:
    aux: {type: table, format: csv}
    nul: {type: metric, format: json, decisions: [prior]}
  ```

  Its places include `.astra/records/aux.json`, `results/aux.csv`, `.astra/records/con/nul.json` and `results/con/nul.json`; Python 3.13 `ntpath.isreserved` returns True for all four (False for `results/accuracy.csv`, `.astra/records/console.json`). Microsoft, *Naming Files, Paths, and Namespaces*: "Do not use the following reserved names for the name of a file: CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM1, ... LPT9 ... Also avoid these names followed immediately by an extension". Git v2.56.0 `compat/mingw.c` `is_valid_win32_path`: "trailing spaces, a file extension, or an NTFS Alternate Data Stream do not matter, the name is still reserved"; `read-cache.c:1009` calls it for every index entry.
- Trailing `.`: both validators return `VALID` on an output `table` with `format: "csv."` (and `"csv.."`). `ntpath.isreserved('results/table.csv.')` is True. .NET path normalisation: "all trailing periods and spaces (U+0020) are removed". `is_valid_win32_path`: "cannot end in ` ` or `.`". RFC 0005 commits bytes at the default place ("commits a pointer file at the default location", `rfcs/0005-result-provenance-and-layout.md:42`).
- On Linux, Git does not notice: `git -c core.protectNTFS=true clone` checks out the device-name and trailing-`.` paths with exit 0. The Windows behaviour rests on the documentation and source above; nothing was run on Windows.
- A side effect of unbounded ids at the parse layer: a flow-mapping key over 1024 characters is a `ParserError` in ruamel (`scanner.py:346`) but `VALID` in yaml 2.9, though YAML 1.2.2 production [145] sets no limit there.

**Why it matters.** An author on Linux who picks a natural id (`aux` for an auxiliary output, `con` for a pro/con universe) or mistypes `csv.` gets no warning, and the repository then fails to check out for Windows users ("Clone succeeded, but checkout failed"). With overlong ids a conformant runner can only fail, and runner and checker authors have no rule to point to. Only unusual ids reach these cases.

**Suggested fix.** Add one rule that each component of a place §8 builds (universe id, result name, `name.format`) is a portable file name: at most 255 bytes, not a Windows device name, not ending in `.`; tighten the format pattern to forbid a final `.` (`^[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9_.+-]*[a-z0-9_+-])?$(?!\n)`). Or say in §8 that these places are not guaranteed to be portable file names. Tom decides.

### F161. The class the specification calls "a visible character" admits invisible characters and C1 controls in `doi`, `url` and `iri`, and the known item covers only `excluded`

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:252-253`, `docs/specification.md:448`, `docs/specification.md:502`, `docs/specification.md:910-911`, `src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:69`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:375`, `src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:145`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:217`, `design/2026-11-03-v0.1-schema-design.md:752`

**What is wrong.** §4 names `[^\x00-\x20\x7f]` "a visible character", and §5 says a DOI ends in "visible characters". The class matches every code point above U+007F. That includes non-ASCII spaces (NBSP, U+3000), format characters (U+200B, U+FEFF, U+202E), U+2028/U+2029, and the C1 controls (U+0080–U+009F). None of these is visible. Design §12 records the gap for `excluded` only, and only as "non-ASCII whitespace". The same class governs `Evidence.doi`, `AnalysisDeclaration.url` and `Environment.iri`.

The definition is exact, since the spec gives the range in the same sentence, so two implementers build the same class. The defect is the label and the record, not an ambiguity. The class is explicit by design (R7, so that Python and ECMA-262 agree), and this finding doesn't argue against that.

**Evidence.** The class in the patterns:

```
src/astra/schema/insight.yaml:69:     pattern: '^10\.[0-9]{4,}/[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+$(?!\n)'
src/astra/schema/provenance.yaml:145: pattern: '^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*:[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+$(?!\n)'
src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:281:   pattern: '[^\x00-\x20\x7f]'
src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:375:   pattern: '^(?:[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://[^\x00-\x20\x7f]+|...)$(?!\n)'
```

The wording: spec:252-253 "`[^\x00-\x20\x7f]` for a visible character (one outside U+0000–U+0020 and U+007F)"; design:752 "`excluded` counts a reason made only of non-ASCII whitespace as not blank."

Minimal documents, each a valid document with one value changed:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
prior_insights:
  i:
    claim: A claim.
    evidence:
      - doi: "10.1234/abc\u200B"   # also \_ \uFEFF \u2028 \N \u0080, "10.1234/\u200B", "10.1234/\u3000"
outputs:
  result: {type: metric, format: json}
```

The `url` probes are `"\u200Bhttps://github.com/o/r"`, `"https://github.com/owner/repo\_"`, `"...\u2029"` and `"...\u009F"`. The `iri` probes are fixture ProvenanceRecord-002 with `iri: "urn:x:env\u0080"`, `"urn:x:\u202Eenv"` and `"urn:x:env\_"`.

```
validate.py and validate.mjs (Ajv), both:
doi  NBSP, ZWSP, ZWSP-only, BOM, LS, NEL, U+0080, U+3000-only   VALID
url  NBSP, leading ZWSP, PS, U+009F                             VALID
iri  U+0080, NBSP, U+202E                                       VALID (closed and open)
control: an ASCII space in each field                           INVALID_VALUE
```

The leading-ZWSP url matches the scp-like branch, with host `'\u200bhttps'`. Git reads it as a scheme:

```
$ LANG=C git ls-remote "$(printf '\xe2\x80\x8bhttps://github.com/o/r')"   # git 2.56.0
fatal: protocol '​https' is not supported
```

Not every accepted value breaks the target grammar. NBSP and U+3000 are Graphic, so they are legal in a DOI (ISO 26324). Only the Cf, Cc, Zl and Zp characters are not. RFC 3987 §2.2 (`ucschar = %xA0-D7FF / ...`) admits NBSP and ZWSP in an IRI. It excludes the C1 controls, and §4.1 says IRIs "MUST NOT contain" bidi formatting characters such as RLO.

**Why it matters.** A ZWSP, BOM or NEL pasted onto a DOI passes every layer, because no rule checks a DOI, so a finding cites a DOI that doesn't resolve. A pasted invisible character on a `url` surfaces as `REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` at the validator, not `INVALID_VALUE` at the schema. That is by design for malformed URLs that have a remote's form. The word "visible" also tells readers the class excludes characters that it accepts.

**Suggested fix.** Widen the §12 item to say the class admits non-ASCII spaces, format characters and C1 controls in `excluded`, `doi`, `url` and `iri`. Then either rename the class at spec:252, :448, :502 and design:217 ("a character other than an ASCII space or control"), or also exclude `\x80-\xa0` and the Cf/Zl/Zp/Zs ranges with explicit classes that both engines read the same way.

### F162. Input and declaration paths that differ only in case or Unicode normalisation are valid, and nothing warns that they are one file on macOS and Windows

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `docs/specification.md:344-359`, `docs/specification.md:962-965`, `docs/specification.md:1118-1152`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:156`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:365`

**What is wrong.** The path rules check the form of one path: no leading `/` or `~`, no `\`, no `..` segment, no control character, no trailing `/` (spec:344-348, `Input.path` at analysis.yaml:156, `AnalysisDeclaration.path` at :365). No rule compares two paths. Two inputs `data/A.csv` and `data/a.csv`, two `café.csv` inputs (one NFC, one NFD), or two declarations `Stage` and `stage` are valid and are distinct files on Linux. On a case-insensitive file system (the default on macOS and Windows), or a normalisation-insensitive one (APFS), git checks out one file of each colliding group. The spec, the RFCs, the tidy-up RFC and the design never mention case or normalisation, so this is neither settled nor known.

The spec's own MUSTs already stop the harmful outcomes. A fresh clone shows the collision as a modification, so a conforming runner refuses to run (`UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES`, spec:963-965). A checker that reads the commit, as `STALE_RESULT` defines the status (spec:984-985), gets the Linux hashes. What is missing is a warning to the author before anyone clones on macOS or Windows.

**Evidence.** The validators accept:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
inputs:
  upper: {path: data/A.csv}
  lower: {path: data/a.csv}
outputs:
  out: {type: table, format: csv, inputs: [upper, lower]}
```

```
uv run python toolkit/validate.py Analysis case.yaml norm.yaml decl.yaml    -> VALID, VALID, VALID
node toolkit/js/validate.mjs <repo> Analysis case.yaml norm.yaml decl.yaml  -> VALID, VALID, VALID
```

A repository tracking these files was cloned onto a Linux casefold tmpfs, which stands in for macOS and Windows:

```
warning: the following paths have collided (e.g. case-sensitive paths
on a case-insensitive filesystem) and only one from the same
colliding group is in the working tree:
  'data/A.csv'
  'data/a.csv'
  'data/café.csv'
  'data/café.csv'
data/A.csv -> lower  sha256 b908e4daaf9d57fe
data/a.csv -> lower  sha256 b908e4daaf9d57fe
## git status --porcelain:
 M data/A.csv
 M "data/cafe\314\201.csv"
```

On ext4 the two files hash to `e83189db38554920` and `b908e4daaf9d57fe`, and `git status` is empty. `git cat-file blob HEAD:data/A.csv | sha256sum` on the casefold clone gives `e83189db38554920`.

Apple's APFS FAQ: "Being normalization-insensitive ensures that normalization variants of a filename cannot be created in the same directory", with case-insensitive "being the default". Microsoft: "Do not assume case sensitivity ... NTFS supports POSIX semantics for case sensitivity but this is not the default behavior." NTFS is not normalisation-insensitive, so the NFC/NFD half applies to macOS only.

The scope is wider than pairs of inputs. With one input `data/A.csv` and `data/a.csv` tracked but not an input, the input still reads `lower`'s bytes. With an input `sub/x.csv` and a declared analysis `Sub`, `INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS` (spec:352-355) passes on Linux and fails on the casefold file system.

**Why it matters.** An author on Linux writes a valid analysis that a collaborator on macOS or Windows can't run from a clean clone: the runner refuses with `UNCOMMITTED_CHANGES` and no message points at the paths. Tidy-up change 27 already motivates the path rules by cross-platform behaviour (NNNN-settling-the-schema-for-v0.1.md:229).

**Suggested fix.** Add a SHOULD-level validator warning, beside tidy-up change 27, that no input or declaration path, nor any of its directories, equals another tracked path under Unicode case folding and NFC normalisation. Not a MUST: that would reject repositories that are legitimately Linux-only.

### F163. A path holding a lone surrogate is valid, and Python and Node validators look up different files for it

- **Lens:** Edge cases · **Severity:** Minor · **Where:** `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:156`, `src/astra/schema/analysis.yaml:365`, `docs/specification.md:243-245`, `docs/specification.md:252-253`, `docs/specification.md:344-345`, `docs/specification.md:350`

**What is wrong.** Both YAML loaders turn a `\uD800` or `\uDC80` escape into a lone surrogate. Both schema validators accept it in an input's `path` and in a declared analysis's `path`: the class `[^\x00-\x1f\x7f\\]` matches surrogates in Python `re` and in Ajv's u-mode. So the contract agrees with itself. The specification never says what a string's characters are, or how a path maps to a file name, and the two runtimes map a lone surrogate differently. For a high surrogate, Python finds no file and `open` raises, while Node encodes it as U+FFFD and finds `data/�.csv`. For a low surrogate in U+DC80–U+DCFF, both find a file, but different ones: Python's surrogateescape gives the raw byte (0x80), Node gives U+FFFD.

**Evidence.** The document:

```yaml
spec_version: "0.1.0"
inputs:
  d: {path: "data/\uD800.csv"}
outputs:
  o: {type: table, format: csv, inputs: [d]}
```

`validate.py` and `validate.mjs` both print VALID for it, for the `\uDC80` variant, and for a document declaring `analyses: {sub: {path: "sub/\uD800"}}`. Both reject `\u0001` with `INVALID_PATH`. Against a tree whose `data/` holds `\xef\xbf\xbd.csv` (content `x`) and `\x80.csv` (content `raw80`):

```
python, \uD800:  os.path.isfile: False
                 open: UnicodeEncodeError 'utf-8' codec can't encode character '\ud800' ...: surrogates not allowed
node,   \uD800:  fs.existsSync: true   reads: "x\n"
python, \uDC80:  os.path.isfile: True  open reads: 'raw80\n'
node,   \uDC80:  fs.existsSync: true   reads: "x\n"
```

A JSON-style surrogate pair (`😀`) loads to U+1F600 in both runtimes and does not diverge.

- `docs/specification.md:244-245`: "so that a validator in either language accepts the same values."
- `docs/specification.md:252-253`: "`[^\x00-\x1f\x7f]` for any character but an ASCII control."
- `docs/specification.md:350`: "It names one file ([`MISSING_INPUT_FILE`](#validator))."
- `grep -n -i "utf-8\|utf8\|code point\|scalar value\|U+FFFD" docs/specification.md` returns nothing.

YAML 1.2.2 §5.7 has escapes "parsed into the appropriate Unicode character", so a conforming loader accepts the escape. RFC 8259 §8.2 leaves unpaired surrogates unpredictable.

**Why it matters.** On one checkout, astra-tools (Python) and astra-typescript (Node) can give different `MISSING_INPUT_FILE` verdicts, or both pass and read, and hash, different bytes. A Python validator that does not catch the `UnicodeEncodeError` crashes. The input is adversarial or accidental: no editor or tool writes it.

**Suggested fix.** Exclude U+D800–U+DFFF from the two path classes, with a cross-validator test, since Python `re` and Ajv's u-mode treat that range differently. Or state in §3 that every string is a sequence of Unicode scalar values and that a path names the file whose name is its UTF-8 encoding.

## Considered and set aside

What the reviewers looked at and judged fine, settled or already known, one line each, by lens. Lines that begin "Candidate set aside" are candidate findings that a verifier refuted.

### RFC fidelity

- RFC 0003 universes land: Analysis.universes is an id-keyed map with propertyNames, and Universe has id, description, derived_from and decisions plus the tidy-up's label and tags. Universe.analyses and Decision.default are UNKNOWN_FIELD in both validators (fixtures 145, 116), and §6 has no default universe or option.
- derived_from: UNKNOWN_DERIVED_FROM and DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE (a self-reference counts as a cycle) match RFC 0003 row 228. It is typed `id`, so a flat `stage.v1` is INVALID_ID in both validators.
- Effective selection (spec 552-571) covers RFC 0003 rows 229-232. EXCLUDED_OPTION_SELECTED comes from main and tidy-up 10.
- Pin grammar `reference = address ["#" id]`: both validators give INVALID_REFERENCE for `a#rf-standard`, `#u`, `data#`, `result#a#b` and a pinned `when`, and INVALID_ID for a pinned Universe.decisions key (spec §4:311-316). `- a #rf` loads as the plain reference `a` (§4:318-320).
- Levels and scope: every error row of RFCs 0003, 0004, 0006 and 0007 is a MUST and every warning row a SHOULD, with the same scope (0004:408-412 word for word; NO_UNIVERSES keeps 0004:415's exemption). rules.json carries the same codes, levels and sources (PIN_LIKE_COMMENT's 'recommended' aside, F009). UNPINNED_EVIDENCE and REDUNDANT_PIN also cover prior_insights' evidence, a harmless superset of RFC 0003.
- Output.format is required for per-universe outputs too, which is stronger than RFC 0003. But 0003 scheduled it for 0.1.0 (402-405) and RFC 0005:250 requires it, so it is not a departure.
- The RFC examples rewritten in v0.1 form (0003 iris, finding and diffs; 0004 Examples 1 and 2; 0005 records; 0006 inputs and iris 'after'; 0007 followup, previous, upstream_driven, iris_rerun) are VALID in Python jsonschema and Ajv. examples/iris_pipeline matches 0004 Example 2 as amended by 0006 and the tidy-up.
- Removed and superseded forms are rejected in both validators: preset universes, inline sub-analyses, Analysis.path, Input.type/source, `from`, and top-level id/name/version give UNKNOWN_FIELD; `../sibling.out` gives INVALID_REFERENCE; declarations as a list give WRONG_TYPE (Analysis-158). A missing spec_version is MISSING_FIELD and `outputs: {}` is EMPTY_COLLECTION, per RFC 0004 and tidy-up 7/8.
- Address-typed fields (references, addresses, conditions, option references) match RFC 0004 §2. Nesting to any depth is accepted, multi-hop flat keys pass propertyNames, and MISSING_DECISION matches 0004 lines 137 and 407. Option.insights and derived_from stay local ids, which no RFC asks to change. A declaration id equal to an input id is valid, settled by D5.
- RFC 0005 records land. Place is a MUST (MISSING_RECORD, MISPLACED_RECORD → `missing`) and bytes a SHOULD (ABSENT_RESULT), with the runner writing the bytes (UNRECORDED_RESULT). content_hash and size are required, with size ≥ 0 (fixture 211); spec_version replaces `version` (205); Generation.commit is a commit_id (210). The required-if on inputs is the check rule MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS (design §6).
- The closed and open record schemas differ only in additionalProperties (at five levels) and $id. INVALID_RECORD gives `missing` (tidy-up 21). started and finished are optional, as RFC 0005 §2 leaves them.
- RFC 0006 inputs: the fields land and type/source are removed (Analysis-117/118). A hash in astra.yaml is UNKNOWN_FIELD. An input that shares an output's id (AMBIGUOUS_ID) and a pinned input (PIN_ON_INPUT) are validator-layer, which is the right layer. Data from outside comes in through an output's recipe (spec:366-370).
- Input.path at 34 boundaries (`..`, `a/..`, `~x`, `x~`, `a//b`, `data/`, controls, NEL, U+2028, spaces): Python re and JS RegExp agree, and both match tidy-up 26 and §5 (drive paths aside, F010).
- RFC 0007 declarations: exactly one of path/url → INVALID_DECLARATION_SOURCE, and a url needs a commit → URL_WITHOUT_COMMIT, in both validators (Analysis-147/148/149). The full lowercase commit id is a recorded departure (tidy-up 25, design §11 item 14). Its validator rows match its table in level, and 'declare only fetchable remotes' is one of the SHOULDs §1 lists as uncheckable.
- The `uri`/`file` environments in RFC 0007 §7's parenthetical: RFC 0005 defines only `iri`, so there is nothing to land. Pre-0.1 files at old commits (written with `version`) are left to the versioning policy (spec §1, design §12).
- Tidy-up changes 1-28 are all in the schema and contract, with fixtures, and the per-change probes (C1-C27) give the stated codes in both validators. `2026-02-31` and year 0000 are valid, as change 27 and XSD 1.1 allow (the missing caveat is F130). The migration section matches the spec step by step.
- UNSUPPORTED_SPEC_VERSION extends RFC 0007 §6 to the whole stack. This is benign, since no validator can check a version it has no schema for, and design §10 lists it.
- Layer criterion: no validator rule can be expressed in JSON Schema 2019-09, since each one relates keys or values. Every schema-layer code maps to keywords in rules.json.
- EMPTY_COLLECTION's Source `0004, main` is weak for Decision.options but defensible (main's 'exactly one existing option'). Design §10's 'No duplicate entries (D2)' cites D2 wrongly (the sources are tidy-up 23 and R21); this is internal to the design, and the rule itself lands.
- Known, not re-reported (raised by several lenses): one result reached through two driven declarations has two names; an analysis with no decisions reads a per-universe output; spellings of one decision agree (all design §13). Also `when` on an inactive decision (#93); Analysis-159's second code, the empty Runner.id/version/claim/exact/value, the non-ASCII-blank `excluded` and the w3id redirects (design §12); and `$comment`'s 0.0.14, stamped from the latest tag.
- Candidate set aside: suspected that UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE's 'the analysis the output belongs to' (spec:1146) clashes with §8's 'a result belongs to…' (852). It doesn't: the spec and RFC 0004 use the phrase for the defining analysis (spec:723, 0004:410), and §8 defines belonging only for results. Even in §8's sense, `inner_undriven.result#v1` belongs to inner (751, 754).
- Candidate set aside: INVALID_RECORD's Source ('0005 §2, §8') leaves out tidy-up 21. This is fine: RFC 0005 §2 (:136) already limits a record to its version's fields, and the cited §8 (948-952) states the closed/open reading, which meets the Source definition (1038-1039). Other tidy-up-shaped rules don't cite tidy-up either, and design §11 item 11 lists the departure.
- Candidate set aside: the suspicion that design §13's open point (an analysis with no decisions reading a per-universe output) blames rules that reject nothing is refuted. subanalysis3 isn't driven, so the root's universe is never built in it (716-718), and its read through the undriven `subanalysis2` fails NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE/UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION, as §13 says.

### Implementer

- Address resolution (spec:281-299), worked at depths 1-3 through driven, undriven and commit declarations, is unambiguous for unpinned addresses. Wrong-kind addresses give UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS or UNKNOWN_OPTION per the §4 table. An output and a declaration that share an id are told apart by position; AMBIGUOUS_ID is needed only for an input and an output (275-277).
- Placeholders match entries exactly as written: `{inputs.fit.pre.clean}` against the entry `fit.pre.clean#u1` is UNDECLARED_TEMPLATE_REF (spec:417-423).
- A pin in the root's file through one declaration names the right universe and record place. Through a driven declaration it names the root's universe (.astra/records/u1/fit.score.json); through an undriven one, the analysis's own; through a commit declaration, its own at that commit (spec:732-735, 813-816).
- Records: entries are written as the output lists them in its own file, and addresses are relative to the result's analysis (305-307, 852-858, 904-906). The 23 worked-example records validate in both validators, with record and bytes paths per §8 862-869.
- Effective selection (552-574) decides each case: an unset active decision is MISSING_DECISION, and an unset inactive one needs nothing. An own selection of an inactive decision is INACTIVE_DECISION, since step 3 drops only inherited ones, and a dropped inherited selection is not restored down the chain. requires, incompatible_with and excluded apply to the effective selection, and derived_from inherits only the selection (544, 548).
- In the worked examples, whether an output is built once or per universe (decisions listed, unpinned per-universe input, only pinned entries, `when`, pinned or driven commit outputs) is settled by 596-600, 461-485, 733-735 and 813-836. The definition's recursion terminates because OUTPUT_CYCLE forbids cycles.
- NO_UNIVERSES across the examples (no decisions; driven; universes without decisions; a lone analysis with decisions) matches row 1151. Universes with no decisions (`first: {}`) are valid and build every output once.
- Nested driving: reads through `b` and `b.c` get names and record places per 852-858 and 866, and C's built-once outputs belong to C (853-854). Setting both `c.d` and `b.c.d` is CONFLICTING_SELECTIONS (702-704). A pinned `b.c.raw#u1` is PIN_ON_INPUT, and unpinned inputs carry no universe (599).
- Staleness through an analysis from a commit: new upstream bytes make the reader stale, and the same bytes make it unchanged again (992-993). README and src/ edits make nothing stale, because code isn't compared (996-998). A stand-in that matches the hash is `absent`, not `present` (1171-1173). A `started` later than `finished` is accepted, and no rule claims an order.
- §10's mapping was implemented twice from §10 alone (jsonschema error.schema + validator; Ajv parentSchema + keyword; identity walk). No error lacked an entry over 99 invalid and 9 valid fixtures and 717 one-defect mutations, and codes and locations agree between the validators (non-string keys aside, F001).
- rules.json covers all 266 assertion keywords, with §10:1269-1273's defaults. jsonschema's path runs through $ref without naming it, and Ajv's schemaPath restarts at '#' for targets with their own $ref, as §10 says. The allErrors warning is accurate: without it Ajv drops codes in 33 mutations. Several codes for one fault where each rule is broken (`type: 3`, `pth:`) is what §10:1303-1305 asks.
- All 35 validator rows, and the 7 check and 7 runner rows, match rules.json in code, layer, level, statement and source. No validator rule needs records or results (§2:81). The five SHOULD rows match their prose, and no row is a MAY.
- A minimal pass/fail pair decides each validator row (address, option, insight, ambiguity, universe, constraint and pin rules, OUTPUT_CYCLE, NO_UNIVERSES). Every pair document is schema-valid in both validators, so only the validator layer decides them.
- Record-status precedence: MISSING_RECORD, INVALID_RECORD, MISPLACED_RECORD or MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS gives `missing` before STALE_RESULT is reached. MISPLACED_RECORD's place-to-address map is one-to-one.
- Cycles: 13 cyclic documents are schema-valid in both validators. Loop-back addresses resolve, and UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE, NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE, OUTPUT_CYCLE and INPUT_NOT_BUILT give one verdict under every reading. `previous: {path: ., commit: X}` is not a cycle, and a commit declaration can't be part of one (668-669).
- Inputs in odd places: an input under `.git/` is caught by INPUT_NOT_TRACKED (git never tracks .git), and one inside a nested analysis's results/ or .astra/ by INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS. `.gitmodules`, and a results/ path that is no planned place, are harmless.
- By design: a universe that drives sub sets sub's active decisions even when nothing reads sub (697-698), and no universe can be an incomplete template ('no privileged universe'). Environment.iri's description resolves UNRECREATABLE_ENVIRONMENT's 'at its version'. Report addresses leave the element kind to the framework (322-331).
- PIN_LIKE_COMMENT needs the YAML source, which the validator layer sees (§2:81). The flow form `[x #u1]` is a parse error in both loaders.
- Candidate set aside: whether step 3 for derived universes (spec:558) runs once or repeats until stable. The two readings differ only when a `when` names a dropped decision, which is the #93 point recorded in design §13:774-775 and out of scope (design §1:27). Spec:462 already favours repeating until stable.
- Candidate set aside: an active output that lists a decision inactive in some universe, so `{decisions.<entry>}` has no value. This is the first case of the open PR #93, 'Using conditional elements' (its §1 and validator row 1), which the brief (review-brief.md:86) and the design (schema-design.md:27) put out of scope. main's gap is unchanged; design §13 (774-776) could mention it.
- Candidate set aside: NO_UNIVERSES exempts a non-driven analysis that has decisions of its own but consumes another's decision. This is RFC 0004's rule word for word (0004:415, spec 608-611, 1151), and narrowing it would depart from an accepted RFC. The no-decision variant is a known open point (design:777-783).
- Candidate set aside: the spec never defines which text in Recipe.command is a placeholder (awk braces, `${VAR}`, `find -exec {}`, a lone brace). Recipes are out of scope (brief:88), and the gap is word for word main's (main spec:483-493). It is real but narrower than reported, and would be Important if recipes were in scope.
- Candidate set aside: a pinned Evidence.artifact that names an input breaks both UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS and PIN_ON_INPUT. Refuted: a validator reports every rule broken (spec:89-90, 1036, 1058), so no precedence is needed, and PIN_ON_INPUT on evidence follows RFC 0003:181-182.
- Candidate set aside: a derived_from naming its own universe gets UNKNOWN_DERIVED_FROM and DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE by §6 but only DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE by §9. Refuted: rules live only in §9 (spec:22-24, 1025-1026) and row 1133 holds; §6:545-546 gives the self case DERIVED_FROM_CYCLE, and RFC 0003 has the same wording (:77-78, :228).
- Candidate set aside: an unpinned read through an undriven declaration breaks both NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE and UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION, with no precedence. This is fine: RFC 0004 states them as two rules that can each break alone (0004:410-411), and §2 (89-91) has a validator report every rule broken.

### Author

- Four analyses were written from the spec and the LinkML descriptions alone: a Lassa CFR analysis with conditional decisions; Alpine downscaling with a driven stage; a T2D polygenic score with https, scp-like and path+commit declarations; and a KiDS-1000 S8 measurement with DOIs, versions, quotes and locations. They and their records are VALID in both validators and, checked by hand, consistent with RFCs 0003-0007 and the tidy-up (findings aside).
- Author mistakes are caught at their cause, with the same code and pointer in both validators: a pin on Output.decisions (INVALID_REFERENCE), capital or digit-leading ids (INVALID_ID), lists for maps or a nested drive map (WRONG_TYPE), a missing format (MISSING_FIELD), a `.pdf` format or a DOI written as a URL (INVALID_VALUE), `excluded: true` (WRONG_TYPE), a blank `excluded` (BLANK_EXCLUDED), `spec_version: 0.1` (WRONG_TYPE), short or uppercase commits (INVALID_COMMIT), and a url without a commit or with a path (URL_WITHOUT_COMMIT, INVALID_DECLARATION_SOURCE).
- The closed and open record schemas reject runner mistakes identically: a `+00:00` offset, missing or six-digit milliseconds, a short commit, an unprefixed hash, inputs as a map. `version` for `spec_version` gives UNKNOWN_FIELD + MISSING_FIELD closed and MISSING_FIELD open, as intended. `inputs: []` and an absent `inputs` are both valid and treated alike.
- Mistakes the schema rightly leaves to the validator layer are schema-valid in both validators, by design: address typos, unknown options in conditions, a space before a pin, a pin naming the wrong universe, a path naming astra.yaml, and a remote URL in `path`.
- Decision has `rationale` and no `description`, as on main at 1bc6eb8, and declarations take no label, description or tags (tidy-up 9). The tidy-up RFC's overgeneral wording on this is F085.
- Rebuilding a result in universes whose selections agree (a cohort built in six universes, a stage in every root universe) is by design in RFC 0003/0005; result identity is deferred to a later RFC.
- These match RFC 0004 §2-§3, RFC 0005 §1-§2 and RFC 0007 §3: driving by flat keys, pins through a driven declaration, placeholders such as `{inputs.prep.gcm_regridded#u}`, a root option incompatible with a driven option, a driven pipeline's record places, and a prior insight citing an artifact of an analysis from a commit.
- Eleven realistic url forms (https with and without .git, scp with and without a user, ssh:// with a port, git+ssh, file://, credentials, bracketed IPv6, scp with an absolute path, a private GitLab host) are accepted in both validators, and `../qc` as a url is INVALID_VALUE. The https and scp-like spellings of one repository at one commit are one analysis (768-772).
- Limits by design: choosing a declared analysis's universe by one's own decision needs driving (RFC 0004 defers presets; sets of universes are #82). A url declares only a top-level analysis (RFC 0007 §1). A driven commit analysis without built-once records can't be built through (RFC 0007 §7). Evidence cites only a DOI or an output (main, tidy-up 11). An input used under one option makes results stale in every universe (RFC 0005 §4).
- YAML pitfalls checked: a comma in a flow-mapping label gives UNKNOWN_FIELD naming the stray key, unquoted DOIs and labels like `0.1 mm/day` load as strings, and `artefact:` gives UNKNOWN_FIELD plus INVALID_EVIDENCE_SOURCE.
- Three cases were judged fine: an input path naming the `.dvc` stand-in itself (the spec is clear, and catching it is tooling's job); an `incompatible_with` written on one side only (symmetric, since 'never selected together'); and an output reading whichever branch is active (it can be written per branch, and `when` semantics are out of scope).

### Internal consistency

- All 65 §9 rows match rules.json's `rules` in order, code, layer, level, statement, source and status, and no code repeats. Every code in the prose is in §9, and each of the 121 `[CODE](#layer)` links points at its own layer's subsection, whose anchor resolves under Python-Markdown's slugify.
- Every parse- and schema-layer code has an invalid fixture. All 97 non-parse invalid fixtures give exactly the header's code at the header's location in Python and Ajv (Analysis-159's ancestor oneOf is known, design §12).
- §4's code assignment for string values, and the field lists of INVALID_VALUE, INVALID_ID, INVALID_REFERENCE, INVALID_PATH, INVALID_COMMIT, DUPLICATE_ENTRY, EMPTY_COLLECTION and MISSING_FIELD, match the error map and the generated `required` lists exactly. The Used-by cells of §4's Named types match every slot range, and D11 holds (wording F076 and non-string keys F001 aside).
- Prose paraphrases agree with §9's levels (COMMIT_NOT_ANCESTOR, UNPINNED_EVIDENCE, NO_UNIVERSES, ABSENT_RESULT), and LinkML descriptions that paraphrase rules agree with them (Output.inputs aside, F008). REMOTE_UNREACHABLE's MUST and §7's SHOULD on remotes have different subjects, the validator and the readers, as in RFC 0007 §6.
- §8's record fields and every class's requiredness match the LinkML sources and the generated contract, and no `null` appears in the generated schemas (D12). Maps and lists follow D2: duplicate evidence items are allowed, and duplicate recorded inputs are left to MISSING_RECORD_INPUTS. Empty optional collections (`tags: []`, `inputs: {}`) are a second spelling of absence with no effect (`when: []`/`decisions: []` aside, F006).
- Every `Class.field` span names an existing slot, and LinkML 1.12.1 gen-doc produces every `elements/` page the spec links (case-colliding names are known). The description style holds everywhere except OutputType and AnalysisDeclaration.url (F128, F129).
- Patterns against the grammar and descriptions, by exhaustive enumeration: the id family (30,949 strings), commit_id/content_hash/spec_version (55,992), the timestamp ranges, both path patterns (177,156 each; drive paths aside, F010), format, and doi (137,263). 0 disagreements.
- Python re against JS RegExp(u) on all 16 distinct patterns (~1.6M pairs, including astral characters, lone surrogates, NEL, BOM, U+2028 and NBSP): 0 disagreements, so §4:246-251's engine claims hold. propertyNames with the key's pattern is on all 9 maps, and the open schema's patterns equal the closed schema's.
- Spec examples: the §2 analysis is VALID in both validators and holds at the validator layer by hand ('three results' is right). The §5, §6 and §7 fragments, wrapped in a minimal document, are VALID and show what their prose says. The §8 listing matches the layout table, the record example is VALID against the closed and open schemas, and §10's abridged rules.json entries equal the committed ones (only `$comment`'s stamped version differs).
- Cross-references: the RFC links resolve on main (0007 was fetched), §9's RFC section citations exist, bare §n sources mention their code, and about 60 § and below/above references point at the right content.
- CLAUDE.md's claims hold against the sources: keys vs identifier, the compact form, rule annotations, the nine named types, explicit-class patterns, `version: 0.0.0` headers, no history words, generator behaviour, the justfile targets, the four parse fixtures excluded from both linters, no .py under src/astra, and llms.txt in the wheel (the pattern ending, F122, and the wheel README, F063, aside).
- The examples (iris, iris_pipeline) are VALID in both validators and hold by hand: placeholders name entries, addresses resolve, and pins name driver universes; the constraints, MISSING_DECISION and PARTIALLY_DRIVEN hold; and the declaration cycle has no output cycle (UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION's wording aside, F035).
- Error-map sweep: every entry except the three `required: []` ones fires, and no keyword fails without an entry over 21,473 closed and 1,652 open mutations. The Ajv-only entries (9 propertyNames, 2 `if`) carry the inner error's code, as §10 says, and Ajv's parentSchema always resolves by identity.
- For every class, nested-only ones included, the codes for each kind of mutation match §9: a dropped required field, an unknown field, a bad map key, a broken pattern, a duplicate, an empty required collection, other written forms. A non-string Output.type gives INVALID_VALUE + WRONG_TYPE, and both are true.
- Mutation testing: type-level, per-location, facet, slot-pattern, minimum, required, minItems, uniqueItems, class-rule and generator-extension mutants are caught (the survivors are F031-F031, F031, F031, F031). Making Environment.iri optional and changing DecisionSelection.option leave the generated contract unchanged, and swapping Evidence's version precondition gives the same verdicts. A sixth OutputType value changes the vocabulary; it doesn't loosen a constraint.
- Probes of the §9 extractor: subsections out of order, two rule tables, malformed code cells, empty cells, unknown columns, images, repeated codes and a Level in a code span are rejected. Reordered columns, double-backtick spans, autolinks and entities are accepted, as 'The catalogue as data' says. Rebuilding from unmutated copies reproduces rules.json (test coverage is F064).
- The generator's docstrings and rules.py's `if` comment match the code. conftest.py's core-schema regexes match YAML 1.2.2 §10.3.2, scan_directive rejects any `%YAML` other than 1.2, and no test uses PyYAML or LinkML's validators.
- Known, not re-reported: the blacklist pattern guard, the per-run wheel build, nested combinators, collection-time parsing, the rules.py docstring, the unused-annotation check's scope, ProvenanceRecord-205's name, and the load.py and label editorial items (design §12). The changes doc's stale '304 tests' and design §4's short-form patterns are design text, not under review.
- Candidate set aside: Evidence.artifact says evidence 'may pin' any output, though REDUNDANT_PIN warns against pinning a built-once one. This is fine: lowercase 'may' isn't BCP 14 (spec:12-16), and the pin is allowed with a SHOULD-level warning (1058, 1149). The wording follows RFC 0003 §3 (0003:217) and design:560, and per D8 the rule lives in §9.
- Candidate set aside: inline HTML in a §9 cell (a comment-only or `<br>` cell, a raw `<a href>`) gets past the extractor. This is what spec:1084 specifies: cells may hold 'links and inline HTML', and only Markdown links are reduced to their text (rules.py:126-143). No cell uses inline HTML today; narrowing the allowance is optional hardening.
- Candidate set aside: rule tables outside §9 are not rejected. The sentence 'no rule table stands outside them' is about §9's own tables (spec:1066-1073), the extractor enforces it inside §9, and CLAUDE.md:39 and D13 say the extractor reads §9 only. The 'only place' claim (1025) is true today, and the tests show that tables outside §9 are skipped on purpose.

### Standards

- key vs identifier (D4) matches LinkML's metamodel, and Environment.iri is the only identifier. Switching a key to identifier is caught by test_every_map_entry_has_one_form. Tidy-up 18's RDF claim (two options named `none` are two nodes) holds under linkml-convert -t ttl.
- `rule` annotations are legal on types, slots, anonymous expressions and class rules (linkml-lint is clean, also with --all), and none reaches the JSON Schemas. Class rules render faithfully (if/then, `not required`, oneOf of required) per the metamodel's preconditions and value_presence. LinkML marks rules as experimental, but the drift check covers that.
- LinkML's `pattern` is a search, as in JSON Schema, so the unanchored Option.excluded pattern means the same everywhere. `{id}` interpolation resolves per defining schema, and all five structured patterns sit in types.yaml.
- The compact form is narrower than LinkML's SimpleDict (settled, D3), and D1's and D3's claims about LinkML hold against its docs and stock 1.12.1. linkml-validate rejecting Analysis-007 doesn't matter, since LinkML's validators aren't the contract (D1, R19). The unused list_elements_unique is the known deferral of uniqueItems by range.
- JSON Schema structure: each file has a 2019-09 `$schema` and an absolute, fragment-free `$id` (the closed and open schemas' are distinct). All three validate against the 2019-09 meta-schema in jsonschema 4.26 and Ajv 8.20. Every $ref is same-document with only `description` beside it, every $def is reachable, and only standard top-level keywords remain (the version is in `$comment`, D3).
- All three schemas compile under Ajv's default options (and with strictTypes or strictTuples) without warnings, and the record schemas also compile under strict:true (the analysis schema under strict:true is F139).
- Keywords: every object is closed, with no patternProperties, and oneOf is the right combinator for exactly-one-of. uniqueItems sits only on lists of strings (code-point equality). `2.0` and `1.0` are integers per validation §6.1.1 (the spec's wording is F140). minProperties/minItems and propertyNames give the same verdicts in both validators.
- §10's absolute keyword location matches core §10.3.2 (no nested $id, and no token needs percent-encoding). §10's jsonschema and Ajv methods give the rules.json key for all 84 Analysis and 14 record fixtures.
- Cross-validator parity: all fixtures and examples give identical verdicts and code sets (Analysis-183 aside, F065), and 5,175 JSON-only mutants agree except on `__proto__` tags (F120).
- YAML core schema: the harness's resolution regexes match YAML 1.2.2 §10.3.2's table exactly. yes/no/on/off/y/n, unquoted dates, `1_000`, `0b101` and `12:30` are strings in both loaders. `<<` is an ordinary key (Analysis-181), and duplicate string keys in any spelling raise in both (Analysis-101, -146).
- The harness accepts `%YAML 1.2` and rejects 1.3 and 2.0, and unknown directives are ignored, as YAML 1.2.2 §6.8 allows. A UTF-8 BOM, an astra.yaml written as JSON and an empty file (WRONG_TYPE at the root) behave alike in both loaders.
- §4's '`#` inside a token is literal' holds for plain scalars in block and flow style, and `- f1_score #svm` is a comment in both loaders. CRLF gives a plain newline in block scalars. An unquoted 40-digit commit is an int (Analysis-153), while an unquoted `spec_version: 0.1.0` is a string, so §3's quoting rule is advice.
- BCP 14: §1's boilerplate matches RFC 8174 §2. Capitalised keywords outside the rule rows are §1's two recommendations, the runner statements, or level names in code spans, and lowercase should/may sits beside rows of the same level. RFC 0005:152's 'should' became SHOULD on purpose (design:604). The LinkML descriptions, JSON Schemas and context have no capitalised keyword, and the extractor's KEYWORDS table matches RFC 2119 §1-5 and is case-sensitive per RFC 8174.
- ABNF: the block parses as an RFC 5234 rulelist (Python abnf 2.9.1, apg-js strict), and DIGIT is the core rule §1 names. It quotes no letters, so case-insensitivity and RFC 7405 don't arise. LF line ends in Markdown are universal practice.
- Grammar against schema: 0 mismatches on 278,690 strings in both validators, at every location that uses id, address, reference, option-reference or condition, and the prose agrees. The ABNF equals design §4's and matches RFC 0003 §2, RFC 0004 §4, tidy-up 17 and RFC 0002's report addresses. Lookahead outside core §6.4's subset is settled by R5/R7.
- PROV-O: every class and slot URI is used within its W3C domain and range. Times are xsd:dateTime in both readings (rdflib's `+00:00` is its own normalisation), and there are no leap seconds, as in XSD 1.1. schema:softwareVersion on a SoftwareAgent is acceptable, since domainIncludes isn't a formal constraint.
- JSON-LD: the committed context equals a fresh stock run, it gives the same triples as LinkML (with a urn workaround), and rdflib and jsonld.js agree on §8's example. inputs are unordered prov:used, size is xsd:integer, and address/entry/commit stay literals. Term definitions such as `commit` aren't prefixes in JSON-LD 1.1 (the declared prefixes are F070). The plain reading has no rdf:type, as §10 says.
- Web Annotation: oa:TextQuoteSelector, exact, prefix, suffix, oa:FragmentSelector and rdf:value exist. exact and value are required, as the model's MUST says, and optional prefix and suffix conform to its SHOULD. There is no RDF reading of astra.yaml yet (known, design §12), and Runner.id as an astra:id literal is deliberate (design §6).
- Stand-ins: git-annex SHA256(E) keys and plain LFS pointers carry the lowercase SHA-256 and the size, so `absent` vs `modified` is decidable. An unlocked annex pointer as an input passes MISSING_INPUT_FILE and INPUT_NOT_TRACKED, and a locked link whose content is present is `present`. A DVC-tracked result leaves nothing at its place (`absent`). MD5E and DVC md5 stay comparable between commits, and writing over a locked link is a runner matter.
- DOI: printable non-ASCII suffixes are accepted (Handbook 2025 §4.3.4). Letter case never changes a verdict, and no rule compares DOIs. Pasted forms (`doi:`, https://doi.org/, urn:doi:) are rejected, per Handbook §4.4.1. Legacy `#<>;:(` and percent-encoded suffixes pass, and the non-empty suffix matches IANA. Python and Ajv agree on all 25 DOI documents.
- Candidate set aside: `option-reference = address "." id` (spec:213) fails in PEG-style ABNF tools (apg-js, npm abnf). The grammar is correct RFC 5234, which sets no greedy parsing. apg-js departs from RFC 5234 on purpose (apg-js#7) and also rejects RFC 3986's own dec-octet. The contract's regexes (types.yaml:78) are left-factored, so no validator disagrees.
- Candidate set aside: the comment `[declaration "."]... element` on `address` (spec:211) and `<declaration>.<id>` (327) can be misread as allowing at most one declaration. An ABNF comment is free text (RFC 5234 §3.9), and the rule `id *( "." id )` and spec:283-286 fix the count. Optional polish: write the comment as `*( declaration "." ) element`.

### Edge cases

- Every fixture and examples/ astra.yaml agrees in both toolkit validators (128 runs, records also against the open schema; Analysis-183 aside, F065). The spec's two whole examples are VALID, and the record example is VALID read through json.loads, JSON.parse and both YAML loaders.
- A YAML mutation corpus of 52,707 documents (wrong types, 28 number forms, 30 boundary characters, empty and huge strings, depth 600, 31 key spellings) agrees apart from the reported categories. No error in any corpus lacks a code (0 NO-CODE, 0 UNFOUND).
- Patterns: the 16 distinct patterns on 8.7M probes (every BMP code point, lone surrogates, sampled astral ones) give 0 differences between Python re and JS RegExp(u) (R5/R7). All run in linear time on ~200k-character adversarial inputs (worst 0.031 s), and 1 MB values validate in under 0.5 s.
- Unicode: 1,993 documents × 41 probes over every string field and key agree in both validators. Ids, references, commits, hashes, versions, timestamps, format and type reject every non-ASCII and whitespace probe, including the Kelvin sign, dotless i and long s.
- Paths accept NBSP, ZWSP, BOM, LS/PS, NEL, C1, astral characters, leading and trailing spaces, `.`, `./a`, `a//b` and `...`, and reject tab, LF, CR, DEL and NUL. This matches 'ASCII control character' in the descriptions, §5/§7 and tidy-up 26. A `.` that names a directory falls to MISSING_INPUT_FILE.
- There is no minLength or maxLength anywhere, so UTF-16 and code-point counts never diverge. Free-text fields accept "", whitespace and controls, which no description forbids (the "" cases and the non-ASCII-blank `excluded` are known, design §12).
- Empty collections are accepted for every optional map and list, and rejected with EMPTY_COLLECTION only on outputs, Decision.options and Insight.evidence (§9). Empty `{}` entries give the source codes or MISSING_FIELD in both validators, and `universes.u: {}` is valid.
- A BOM, CRLF and ZWSP/U+FEFF inside scalars read the same in both loaders, and NBSP in the YAML structure is rejected or INVALID_ID in both. A plain and an escaped spelling of one key are a duplicate in both. Implicit block keys over 1024 characters are rejected by both (YAML 1.2.2 §8.2.2). Neither schema is recursive, and 1000-deep wrong-typed nesting is rejected by both.
- Numbers: 1.0, 1e2, -0, 2**64 and 2**53+1 are integers in both validators, from YAML and JSON, and JS's precision loss changes no verdict. JSON records with lone-surrogate escapes, surrogate pairs, -0 and integers below 1.8e308 agree (NaN, BOM, UTF-16 and duplicates are F020).
- Plain scalars resolve the same in ruamel and yaml 2.9 over 875 generated documents and 32 edge tokens (keys F001 and tags F056 aside). The YAML 1.1 traps (yes/no ids, unquoted dates, merge keys, digit-and-e commits, numeric labels) are settled by §3 and R19 (Analysis-003, -007, -181, -153).
- Quoted "true" and "null", and yes, no, nan, inf and e3, are valid ids in every loader (D5 reserves no words). `flag: true` against the options "true"/"false" is WRONG_TYPE everywhere, and a fullwidth `ｙｅｓ` is INVALID_ID. Keys like `stage.true` are strings everywhere, and non-string keys in closed objects are UNKNOWN_FIELD in both validators.
- Explicit core tags (`!!str 2026-11-03`, `!!str true:`) load the same in the harness, yaml, PyYAML and ruamel. yaml-language-server 1.15.0 (YAML 1.2) checks key types like the harness.
- Generated names: ids and formats are lowercase ASCII with no `~` and no Windows-forbidden characters, and ids have no `.`. So record and result names can't collide by case or normalisation, can't equal an 8.3 alias, and a universe directory can't clash with a built-once file. `.astra` is a legal Windows name.
- Bytes on checkout: a CRLF checkout of the §2 astra.yaml parses to the same values in both loaders and is VALID, and records checked out with CRLF stay valid (they are compared by keys and values). astra-spec has no .gitattributes and examples/ holds no data, so the shipped examples are unaffected.
- url boundaries: a bare word, owner/repo, ../repo, C:/repo, c:\repo, C:repo, `host:` and `:path` are rejected, while https, ssh, file, git+ssh and ssh://user@host:22/p are accepted. Bracketed scp forms (`[::1]:repo`) pass, and git reads them as ssh.
- Boundary values are rejected as described: commit_id (39/41/63/65 digits, uppercase, branch names), content_hash (uppercase, `SHA256:`, `sha512:`), spec_version (leading zeros, -rc.1, +build, 1.2, v1.2.3) and timestamps (23:59:60, offsets, lowercase t/z, month 13, hour 24). The JSON-LD route keeps `urn:` IRIs; only LinkML's route crashes (F021).
- Candidate set aside: INVALID_PATH admits characters Windows forbids (`:*?"<>|`) and segments ending in `.` or a space. The patterns implement the tidy-up's path change (NNNN-settling-the-schema-for-v0.1.md:231) and design §11 item 15 word for word, and nothing claims Windows portability. Rejecting these names would be a new tidy-up change, not a defect.

## Not verified

Suspicions that no reviewer could show, one line each, with what would settle them.

- Which rule applies to a pin on a built-once output of a driven analysis from a commit, REDUNDANT_PIN or UNKNOWN_PIN_UNIVERSE, given that RFC 0007 §7 says such outputs aren't built. Settled by reading RFC 0007 §7 and adding one sentence to §7.
- Validation scope across the stack: is a rule broken in a path-declared analysis's own file reported, and does it make the root invalid? Examples are an UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS in an output nobody reads, a NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE the stack causes at that file's /outputs/y/inputs/0, or NO_UNIVERSES on RFC 0004's subanalysis3. DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID covers only analyses from a commit, and F047 covers only own universes. Settled by a sentence on whether every analysis of the stack must be valid.
- How an error inside an analysis from a commit is reported: by its own code, by DECLARED_ANALYSIS_INVALID on the declaration, or both. Settled by one sentence in §7 or by fixing the intended reporting model.
- Whether NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE and UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION apply to Evidence.artifact, or whether an unpinned citation of an undriven declaration's per-universe output gets only UNPINNED_EVIDENCE (a SHOULD). Settled by an explicit statement in §7.
- A root output built once that reads a built-once output of an undriven analysis (RFC 0007's `report`) has no universe being built, so NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE may be vacuous or broken. This goes beyond F005's per-universe case. Settled by defining 'the universe being built' for built-once outputs.
- NOT_BUILT_IN_UNIVERSE and UNDRIVEN_DECLARATION apply 'in the file of the analysis whose universe is being built'. Do they leave unchecked an unpinned read in a mid-stack file through a declaration that file doesn't drive (iris_pipeline's classification reading `feature_extraction.features`)? Settled by a worked three-level stack.
- How STALE_RESULT treats a driver's record of a result built from a commit after the driver changes the declaration's `commit`. It is probably derivable through 'pins every astra.yaml'. Settled by a worked example.
- `when` cycles between decisions (d when [e.e1], e when [d.d1]) are covered by no rule, so a universe may set neither decision or both. This is #93 when-semantics and out of scope; settled by a ruling on whether a rule is intended.
- An output with no recipe (Recipe is optional) is a planned result that can never be built, so it stays `missing`. Recipes are out of scope; settled by a ruling on whether outputs without recipes are planned.
- INPUT_IN_ANOTHER_ANALYSIS: whether an untracked or gitignored directory holding an astra.yaml counts as an analysis. Settled by saying whether 'analysis' means tracked or merely on disk.
- Which path `{inputs.<entry>}` and `{output}` give for an input read by address, or for a driven stage's recipe: as written, relative to the recipe's directory, or absolute. Also whether input paths with spaces are shell-quoted when substituted. Recipes are out of scope; settled by a runner reference implementation or a statement in the spec.
- Whether two universes with identical effective selections should warn, since they double every result. No source asks for this; it needs the authors' decision.
- How a validator words UNRESOLVED_ADDRESS for `incompatible_with: [prep.bilinear]` (read as decision `prep`, option `bilinear`), and whether the message points at the missing segment. Needs a validator implementation.
- A url-declared remote whose top-level analysis declares a sub-analysis that is a submodule of the remote probably has F026's gap too. Settled with a remote that holds a submodule and a reading of what 'read from git at its commit' fetches.
- The url pattern accepts `git@[2001:db8::1]:x/y.git`, apparently with host `[2001`. Whether that matches git's bracketed scp form. Settled against git's url parser.
- `C://repo` (a one-letter scheme) is accepted through the `scheme://` branch. Git for Windows may read it as a drive path, contradicting 'a Windows drive path is never read as a remote'. Settled by `git ls-remote C://repo` on Windows, or by tracing the order of is_url and url_is_local_not_ssh in transport_get.
- No implementation exists, so the validator, check and runner verdicts on all authored and worked examples were derived by hand from §§5-9. Settled by running astra-tools once those layers exist.
- Which YAML loader and settings astra-tools and astra-typescript will use: tests/conftest.py's ruamel loader or its C loader, or yaml with toJS key stringification, tag warnings as errors, and a UTF-8 read or BOM sniffing. This decides how far F001, F056, F066, F067, F072 and F141 reach beyond the test harness. Settled by their loading code (out of scope).
- These loaders and validators weren't run on non-string keys or the corpora: js-yaml, PyYAML stacks, yaml-language-server's tag handling (`!env` flagged, `!include` not), fastjsonschema, jsonschema-rs, Hyperjump, Go and Rust validators, and Ajv with unicodeRegExp:false. Settled by rerunning the corpora with each.
- Whether jschon's listing of untaken `if` results conforms to 2019-09 §10.4, and whether JsonSchema.Net and the Rust jsonschema crate list them too (the extent of F049). Settled by the output-format maintainers or the 2019-09 output tests, and by running each validator on the same documents.
- Whether Ajv without allErrors, or other Ajv 8.x minor versions, report the Ajv-only `if` and `propertyNames` entries at the same locations. Settled by rerunning the batch with those settings.
- Whether the specification's own example documents, once mutated, produce any code not seen in the fixtures and examples. Settled by mutating them as the fixtures were.
- Whether rdflib's rewriting of '…T14:00:00.000Z' as '+00:00' matters to any consumer; the two are equal as xsd:dateTime values. Settled by finding a consumer that compares lexical forms.
- Tidy-up change 27's RDF claim was checked only through the plain JSON-LD context with rdflib, not through LinkML's RDF conversion. Settled by running linkml-convert on a record with an https environment IRI, which avoids F021.
- Whether other LinkML RDF paths (linkml-convert -t json-ld, rdflib_dumper through the Python API), or a LinkML newer than 1.12.1, also read Environment.iri as a CURIE (the extent of F021). Settled by running them.
- LinkML's RDF-to-JSON round trip (linkml-convert -f ttl -t json) failed with 'No such slot entry as an attribute of ProvenanceRecord ancestors'. Because of that, whether two Generation slots sharing prov:used confuse rdflib_loader couldn't be tested. The spec claims no round trip; a minimal schema bisect would find the cause.
- Whether a JSON-LD 1.0-only processor (an old pyld or rdflib-jsonld) treats context terms such as `commit` or `size` as prefixes; jsonld.js in 1.0 mode did not. Settled by running such a processor.
- W3C Web Annotation says a FragmentSelector SHOULD carry dcterms:conformsTo; whether omitting it (design §5) makes the RDF reading non-conformant. Settled by the W3C text, which wasn't fetched.
- Changing Environment.iri's range to `uri` makes LinkML emit `format: uri`, which strict Ajv without ajv-formats may refuse to compile. This matters only when choosing a fix for F021/F070; settled by compiling the regenerated schema.
- Whether `partial_match: true` changes gen-doc output (docs/elements/, docs/schema/*.yaml); only gen-schema, pytest and linkml-lint were run. Settled by running `just gen-doc` before and after.
- Docs rendering under Zensical: the heading anchors (the slugs were computed with Python-Markdown's default slugify), whether its Rust side post-processes §9's tables differently from Zensical 0.0.40's parse_config, and whether the abnf block is highlighted. Settled by `just docs` in a clone and inspecting site/specification/index.html.
- RFC links 0002-0006 on GitHub weren't fetched (only 0007 was), though the files exist on origin/main. Settled by fetching them.
- Whether `main`'s maintainers meant Decision.options to be non-empty at the schema layer; no explicit main text says so. Settled by astra-tools' main branch (out of scope).
- Whether planned runners emit uppercase hex digests, which decides how soon F022's narrowing bites. Settled by their hashing code.
- A surrogate pair written as two `\u` escapes in a double-quoted YAML scalar loads as two lone surrogates in the harness and as one code point in yaml. JSON records read through the YAML loader may differ from a JSON parser in the same way. No verdict changed. Settled by a code-point-sensitive field or a test that re-encodes such a string to UTF-8.
- Whether YAML 1.2.2 makes a lone-surrogate `\u` escape ill-formed; both loaders accept it in any string field. Settled by the YAML spec text, and if it is ill-formed, by checking whether F072 covers it.
- `%YAML 1.02`: ruamel probably parses the version as (1, 2) and accepts it as 1.2, and YAML 1.2.2 doesn't define version equality. Settled by one load_yaml_text call and one yaml.parseDocument call.
- Opening one class (dropping its additionalProperties:false) can't be written as a LinkML edit, because the generator closes every class, so it wasn't mutated. A generator-level mutant would show whether test_closed_schemas_are_closed is the only guard.
- Mutants of tools/rules.py (the §9 extractor and the error-map walker) and of tests/conftest.py (the loader, absolute_keyword_location, the combinator exemption) weren't tried. Running them would show whether the harness itself can drift while the suite stays green.
- The open record schema wasn't mutated separately, so it is untested whether test_open_variant_has_the_closed_constraints_but_unknown_fields catches a generator change that touches only the open variant. Settled by such a mutant.
- Four test_package wheel tests failed in a local clone at baseline (probably environmental: wheel building or tags) and were deselected for the mutant runs. Settled by running them in a clean environment with tags fetched.
- CRLF line endings in the specification weren't tested against the extractor, which normalises its line list while markdown-it may number lines differently. Settled by running gen-schema on a CRLF copy.
- Under RFC 0005 Example 1's `git annex add results` or `results/** filter=lfs`, a declared analysis under results/ would have its astra.yaml (and, with LFS, its records) turned into links or pointers. That would break 'records can be read whether or not the bytes are there' (L887-888). Settled with git-annex/git-lfs in a scratch repo.
- Whether design §13's tie-break ('fewest declarations, then first in key order') picks RFC 0004's names for every result of a cyclic stack; chains were enumerated only to depths 4 and 6. Settled by an argument that a shortest chain never ties across a back-edge.
- Whether an input committed under `working-tree-encoding` or a clean filter (git-crypt) breaks MISSING_INPUT_FILE, since §5 says what is committed is the file itself or a stand-in tooling recognises. Settled by a ruling on what 'committed' means.
- Whether a VURL/URL annex key (addurl --relaxed/--fast) can stand for different bytes in two clones with no change to the key. If so, STALE_RESULT reads `unchanged` for changed content. Settled with an HTTP server whose content changes between two `git annex get`s.
- Windows-host behaviour wasn't run (the sandbox blocked Git for Windows under Wine). Open questions: whether Windows 11 creates `results\aux`, whether a runner writing `results\nul.json` records the hash of empty content, and whether MAX_PATH 260 with core.longpaths=false blocks deep `.astra/records/…` paths. Settled on a Windows host.
- Whether current macOS APFS refuses names with code points unassigned in Unicode 9.0, and whether an input path segment over 255 UTF-8 bytes (85+ CJK characters) can be created on NTFS or APFS but not checked out on ext4. Settled on a Mac or Windows host; the ext4 side can be shown with a hand-built tree.
- ISO 26324:2025 §4.1.2-4.1.3's own wording wasn't read: iso.org and the ANSI preview returned 403. The DOI findings rest on the DOI Handbook 2025 and IANA's URN:DOI registration. Settled by reading the standard.
- Whether any registered DOI uses an undivided registrant code of two or three digits. The prefix handles 0.NA/10.12 and 0.NA/10.23 exist, but Crossref and DataCite list no works for either. Settled by an mEDRA or Handle query on those prefixes.
